> This draft contains results derived from ATLAS 2022 low-pile-up data (runs 428770 and 427929) and ATLAS
> full-simulation MC (ntuple productions v1.1/v1.2/v2.0). No number, figure, or ntuple detail from this document may be shown outside
> ATLAS (talks, proceedings, preprints, public repositories) without approval through the standard ATLAS process.

# Signal-like background for open-charm mesons in ATLAS minimum-bias data: methods and validation

*Methods section, closing report of project B (`charmbkg`). Version v2.1 for review, 19 November 2026 (Secs. 2, 5.7, 5.10, 5.11, 6.2, 6.3 and 7 updated and Secs. 6.7–6.12 added on 18–19 November; results of 19 November added to Secs. 5.11, 5.12, 6.2, 6.11, 6.12 and 7, and Secs. 5.13 and 6.13 added; results of 20 November integrated in Secs. 2, 5.11–5.13, 6.1, 6.2, 6.6–6.9, 6.11, 6.12 and 7, Sec. 6.13 replaced by the final computation of 20 November).*

**Abstract.** In ATLAS 2022 low-pile-up data, D⁰, D⁺ and D*⁺ candidates have signal fractions below one percent
under the mass peak. We build *signal-like* control samples that differ from the true background under the peak by one
controlled ingredient: mass (sidebands), K↔π mass hypothesis, origin (MC reweighted to data with SALAD, FETA, flows) or
one track (rotation or mixing). We validate them by MC closure. Three results stand out.
(i) A classifier trained on far sidebands with a reflection veto is already at the information ceiling set by training on
the true background (Zproxy at WP70: 4.93 vs 4.95), so better background generators cannot raise the significance.
(ii) An adversarial closure shows that the usual acceptance test, a classifier two-sample test (C2ST) with AUC ≈ 0.50, is
blind to the background *composition*. For D⁺, a local composition change under the peak passes with C2ST = 0.507 while
ε_B is mis-predicted by 15–30 %. Only an ε_B(SR) check against a sideband fit to data after the cut detects it.
(iii) SALAD is therefore used as a background-shape systematic (±5/10/25 % at WP80/70/50 for D⁺) and as an ε_B
estimator under the peak, not as a training sample.
On the signal side, D*-tagged D⁰ give data/MC classifier scale factors (8–30 GeV: 1.00–0.91 from WP95 to WP50) and mass-peak
position and width (σ_data/σ_MC = 0.95 ± 0.05 for the D⁰ tag). They also give a reflection fraction R/S data/MC = 0.96 ± 0.04, and they
show that the `closest_mass` swap heuristic narrows the MC signal shape. In 5–8 GeV, D*-tagged D⁰ show an efficiency
deficit at WP95 (SF95 = 0.83 ± 0.10, 1.7σ from 1). This deficit is not explained by wider mass tails and is treated as
an ε_S systematic. An independent test on the minimum-bias run 427929 gives 1.05 ± 0.11 and neither confirms nor excludes it.
Two results concern the data sample itself. Run 428770, the run of the target AFP-tagged measurement in the base code and the main sample of Secs. 3–5, is dominated by jet triggers (81 % of the D*-tagged
pairs come from L1_J12 alone) and has no minimum-bias class; run 427929 is the clean minimum-bias reference, on which every absolute normalisation rests. The isolation efficiency of true D⁰ agrees with the MC in
minimum-bias data (SF = 0.98 ± 0.04 for ptcone40 < 2 GeV in run 427929) but not in run 428770 (0.51 ± 0.02), and no MC
reweighting that we tried without using the tracks of the cone itself, including one in track-jet variables, removes the
difference in every pT bin. These isolation scale factors are measured on D⁰ tagged by a D*, whose soft pion lies inside
the cone: on MC the 427929 number carries over to inclusive D⁰ within 0.5 %, the 428770 one is model dependent, and for a
classifier that sees the surrounding tracks a D*-tag scale factor does not calibrate the inclusive sample (Sec. 6.2). A preliminary estimate of the
accidental AFP-tag fraction from proton mixing gives 0.0087 ± 0.0044 (427929) and 0.048 ± 0.014 (428770); it is valid for
non-AFP triggers only, which hold 4.4 % of the tags in 428770. The integrated luminosity per run and trigger region was
determined by us (Sec. 6.11), and preliminary inclusive D⁰ and D⁺ cross-sections in run 427929 and the D⁺/D⁰ ratio are
given without proton tag (Sec. 6.13, post hoc): σ(D⁰ + anti-D⁰) = 278 ± 29 (stat) ± 49 (syst) ± 6 (lumi) µb at 5–8 GeV and 100 ± 10 ± 21 ± 2 µb at 8–30 GeV, and σ(D⁺ + D⁻) = 515 ± 44 ± 82 ± 11, 165 ± 10 ± 25 ± 4 and 49.2 ± 4.0 ± 6.9 ± 1.1 µb at 3–5, 5–8 and 8–30 GeV, in |η(D)| < 2.2, prompt and non-prompt together. The D⁺ values are a measurement with a scale factor from data, no longer a scenario, but that scale factor is conditional (next sentence), and the D⁺ fit model, fixed on 20.11 (Sec. 5.14), does not meet its closure criterion on simulation at 5–8 GeV (+9.2 ± 6.2 %; carried as an uncertainty, not corrected). D⁺/D⁰ = 0.50 ± 0.07 ± 0.15, 0.59 ± 0.07 ± 0.12 and 0.49 ± 0.06 ± 0.12; from a Δm fit without the classifier σ(D*±) = 134 ± 18 ± 23 ± 3 and 60.6 ± 7.1 ± 6.8 ± 1.3 µb and D*⁺/D⁰ = 0.48 ± 0.08 ± 0.11 and 0.61 ± 0.09 ± 0.13 at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV. Tensions: D⁰ at 5–8 GeV is 3.0σ below the CMS measurement at 13 TeV after the definitions were matched (data/CMS = 0.53 ± 0.13; 0.75 ± 0.20 at 8–30 GeV), and data over Pythia 8 A3 is about 0.6 above 5 GeV for D⁰ and D⁺ (D*±: 0.67 ± 0.15 and 0.87 ± 0.14); D⁰ lies inside the FONLL band. A comparison with ALICE (data/ALICE 0.8–1.1) was made post hoc with reference values that were not re-verified. The values of 19.11 (376 and 132 µb) are withdrawn. The absolute classifier scale factor at WP70, measured before for D⁰ above 5 GeV only, is now available for D⁺ (0.99 ± 0.05 above 5 GeV; 0.95 at 3–5 GeV, used with ± 0.08) and for D⁰ at 3–5 GeV (0.97 ± 0.03); it is conditional on a transfer from the D* tag and is supported for D⁺ by a cut-based selection (0.97 ± 0.05 ± 0.05; Sec. 6.8). No trigger region of run 428770 gives a second absolute point: L1_J12 selects 2.5–12 % of the events with a D, an efficiency known only from run 427929 (Sec. 6.11); the run remains useful for relative quantities inside one trigger class. We give recommendations for the AFP single-D analysis.

The D–D pair (double-charm) measurement is a separate analysis and is documented elsewhere; it uses `charmbkg`
only as a pinned dependency for the background-shape systematic and the K↔π hypothesis handling.
<!-- src: README.md (Osobny projekt od double charm ...); docs/SPLIT.md -->

---

## 1 Motivation

Single D-meson candidates (D⁰→K⁻π⁺, D⁺→K⁻π⁺π⁺, D*⁺→D⁰π⁺ₛ) in ATLAS minimum-bias data are dominated by
background: under the mass peak (signal region, SR) the signal is a fraction of a percent of all candidates; the remainder
is combinatorial background and reflections (wrong K↔π mass assignment). Both the classifier and the mass fit require a
reliable description of this background **inside the SR**, which is blinded in data and statistically poor in MC.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §1 -->

The approach studied here is to construct *signal-like* control objects that differ from the true SR background by
exactly one controlled ingredient — the mass (sidebands), the mass hypothesis (K↔π swap), the origin (reweighted MC),
or one track (rotation/mixing) — and to test whether they are indistinguishable from the true background in all other
features. Three goals were pursued, in increasing order of difficulty:

1. **Training**: a data-driven training background, avoiding MC mismodelling;
2. **Fit / subtraction**: background shape and level under the peak after the classifier cut;
3. **Significance gain**: whether a better background translates into higher significance Z.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §1 -->

**Headline result.** The hypothesis "better training background → higher Z" is *not* confirmed: the classifier trained
on data sidebands is already at the information ceiling defined by training on the true background. The generators are
nevertheless useful as a **background-shape systematic**, as an **ε_B estimator under the peak**, and — most
importantly as a methodological result — the adversarial closure test shows that the commonly used
classifier two-sample test (C2ST) is **blind to the composition of the background** (Sec. 4.2).
<!-- src: README.md "Stan (15.11.2026)"; docs/signal_like_background.md §3, §3a -->

## 2 Samples and selection

- **Data**: ATLAS 2022 low-pile-up runs, ntuple production v1.1 ("Aug7"), converted to HDF5 (one row = one D candidate).
  v1.2 adds daughter `pdgId`/`truthPdgId`/`trackId` and the per-candidate truth flag `Dmeson_hypo_swap`.
  Secs. 3–5 use run 428770 unless a run is named; run 427929 enters in the last paragraph of Sec. 5.2 and in Secs. 5.6–5.11.
  <!-- src: docs/data_format.md (Struktura; v1.2) -->
- **Run 428770 (target run of the AFP-tagged measurement; main sample of Secs. 3–5) is dominated by jet triggers.** Of its 7.99 M events, 56 % (4.49 M) fired L1_J12; the next
  chains are HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 (1.24 M) and HLT_mb_sptrk_pt4_L1MBTS_2 (0.84 M), and HLT_mb_mbts_L1MBTS_1 (prescale 5·10⁴) has 143 events, all of them accidental coincidences with a chain that wrote the event (157 expected from the prescale; 59 % also fired L1_J12). Of the 1 233 580 D⁰←D* pairs, 80.8 % are in the class "L1_J12 only", 12.9 % in the class called
  "MBTS-based" and 6.3 % in "other". The "MBTS-based" class is 93 % HLT_j20, a jet trigger. There is **no minimum-bias class** in this run, because the ntuples are a trigger skim (Sec. 6.11): an event with an MBTS or sptrk bit is present only if a chain of the skim set fired as well. This holds for the whole ntuple, not only for the proton-tagged region: HLT_noalg_mb_L1MBTS_1 has 1631 events, the accidental level (1568 expected), i.e. 0.4–0.5 % of a complete stream; HLT_mb_sptrk_pt4_L1MBTS_2 has about 30 % of the events expected from run 427929, 89 % of them with the HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 bit (28 % in 427929). The 18 "MBTS-only" candidates have no other bit among the 11 stored ones; they are not unbiased. The median μ is 0.051. Role of the run (20.11): in the base code the proton-tagged measurement is configured on this run alone, with one luminosity for the whole run and with trigger regions marked there as not final. The run gives relative quantities inside one trigger class (shapes, D*-tag scale factors, tests of the fit model) with 3 to 10 times the statistics of run 427929, but no absolute normalisation (Sec. 6.11).
  <!-- src (role, 20.11): charm_work/INDICO_1709031/ (review of 20.11) §R.2–§R.3 (configuration of the base code, state of 5.11); charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5 -->
  <!-- src: charm_work/SKIM_review_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md §1 -->
  <!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/PROCEDURA.md §6 (event counts per chain); B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/RAPORT_DANE.md (G3: pair fractions);
       B_trig/ISO_origin/RAPORT.md §0 pkt 2, §2a; μ: AFP_mix/runs/lxplus/out_v2/RAPORT_DANE_v2.md (428770, μ 5/50/95 %) -->
- **Run 427929 (clean reference run, minimum bias; reference for the absolute normalisation)**: MBTS trigger HLT_mb_mbts_L1MBTS_1 without prescale (prescale 1 in
  100.00 % of the good-run-list events with a candidate), μ ≈ 0.005, 23.9 M events in the good-run list and 289 437
  D⁰←D* pairs after the good-run list (402 470 before). The D* yield relative to 428770 is 0.30 ± 0.14 in 5–8 GeV and 0.14 ± 0.02 in 8–30 GeV. The ntuple
  container holds 458 of 583 input files; its completeness is not confirmed (Sec. 7). Role of the run (20.11): the only sample with an unbiased and measured trigger; absolute quantities (Sec. 6.13) and the D⁺/D⁰ ratio are quoted from this run. This matches the published ATLAS practice: in arXiv:1512.02913 the range pT < 20 GeV comes from minimum-bias triggers only.
  <!-- src (role, 20.11): charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5; charm_work/INDICO_1709031/ (review of 20.11) §R.5 (reading of arXiv:1512.02913) -->
  <!-- src: B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/RAPORT_DANE.md (G3, table "Próbki", Uwagi); B_trig/PROCEDURA.md §6 (yield ratio with GRL, 458/583);
       μ: RAPORT_DANE_v2.md (427929, median 0.0048) -->
- **v2.0 files** (both runs and MC, used wherever run 427929 or a trigger class appears: parts of Secs. 5.2 and 5.6–5.13, and Secs. 6.2–6.13): the v2.0 HDF5 conversion stores L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm and
  a D⁰ window of 1.70–2.05 GeV. The analyses apply **L_xy ≥ 0.25 mm** and the 1.75–2.00 GeV window (exceptions: the CharmNet
  clone of Sec. 6.2.4c uses L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm, the accidental-tag study of Sec. 6.3 the loose v2.0 selection, Sec. 6.10 L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm, Sec. 6.12 pT 2–3 GeV), after which v2.0
  reproduces the nominal v1.x result (SF95 in 5–8 GeV: 0.84 ± 0.10 vs 0.83 ± 0.10).
  <!-- src: B_trig/PROCEDURA.md §1 (L_xy 0.10 vs 0.25, window; "v2.0 ≡ v1.2" is quoted there from the h5_v20 README, not re-checked here);
       RAPORT_DANE.md G2 -->
- **MC**: inclusive minimum-bias (`mb_*`) and forced-decay (`fd_*`) full simulation. True background =
  `truth_category ≥ 8` within |m − m₀| < 55 MeV (D⁰) / 45 MeV (D⁺).
  <!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (Wspólne metryki) -->
- **Preselection**: production cut Lxy ≥ 0.25 mm; D⁰ candidates are stored under both K/π hypotheses of the same track
  pair (≈10 % of rows in data, 24 % in fd MC). Wrong-sign candidates are rejected by the CharmAnalysis finders before
  writing and are therefore unavailable.
  <!-- src: docs/data_format.md (tabela decayType; Lxy; v1.2 "kandydatów wrong-sign w ntuplach nie ma"); docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (Wrong-sign) -->
- **Classifier**: XGBoost "H" with 18 features, k-fold out-of-fold (OOF) by event; isolation variables are forbidden as
  features. Working points WP80/70/50 are defined by signal efficiency ε_S. Kinematic range pT 3–30 GeV.
  <!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3a (18 cech H); docs/data_format.md (izolacja zakazana); charm_work/WK2_wklad_AFP/WKLAD.md (Zastrzeżenia: XGB H, pT 3–30) -->
- **Common metrics**: C2ST AUC (control vs true SR background on MC; 0.50 = indistinguishable, target < 0.52);
  ε_B ratio = ε_B(control)/ε_B(true) at WP80/70/50; χ²/ndf of the mass shape; Zproxy = ε_S/√ε_B.
  These definitions are used throughout and not repeated below. "pred/true" denotes ε_B predicted by a background model
  divided by the true ε_B in the SR; SF = ε_S(data)/ε_S(MC).
  <!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 -->

Mass spectra with the SR, the far sidebands and the MC truth-category composition are shown in
[Fig. F1](figures/F1_mass_spectra.pdf) (subsamples of h5 v1.1, before any classifier cut).

## 3 Methods

### 3.1 Sidebands (SB)
Candidates from the mass sidebands differ from the SR background only in mass; the assumption is that the classifier
features depend weakly on m. The near sideband is contaminated by K↔π swaps and by true charm (`truth_category` 2–7);
the nominal training background is therefore the **far sideband with a reflection veto** |m_swap − m₀| > 25 MeV, with
SB_min = 70 MeV.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (SB, SB daleki + weto), §3a wniosek (4) SB_min 70 MeV, §5 pkt 1 -->

### 3.2 SALAD reweighting (MC → data, conditioned on mass)
SALAD [2001.05001] learns a mass-conditioned classifier weight w(x | m) that reweights background MC to data in
the sidebands, and interpolates it into the SR. Assumptions: the data/MC mismodelling is smooth in m **and the background
composition is smooth in m** (the latter is the assumption attacked in Sec. 4.2). The frozen production configuration
**SALAD v1.0** uses a histogram gradient-boosted classifier (`hgb`; `sk_mlp` only as cross-check, ~10 % subsample spread in
ε_B), 18 H features, t = (m − m₀)/0.1, weight clipping 0.05–20, fixed seed, background MC `truth ≥ 8`; the output carries
input SHA-256, n_eff/N, AUC_SB, band C2ST and an `ok` flag.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (SALAD), §3a (SALAD v1.0 — zamrożony artefakt) -->

### 3.3 Flow-based generators: FETA, flow p(x|m), CATHODE
- **FETA** [2212.11285]: a normalising flow transports MC to data in the SB and is applied to MC in the SR. Limited by the
  base flow p_MC(z|m).
- **Conditional flow p(x|m)** sampled into the SR, with H features *derived* from unconstrained generated variables
  (`derive_H`, `kin3` for D⁺) — generating H directly breaks kinematic constraints (C2ST 0.96–0.999).
- **CATHODE** [2109.00546]: weakly-supervised classifier of SR data vs. SR-interpolated background.
- A Gaussian copula served as a simple baseline generator.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (FETA, Flow+CATHODE), §5 pkt 5; README.md (kopuła) -->

### 3.4 Track rotation and mixing (`candrot`, event mixing)
`candrot` builds control objects from real candidates by φ rotation, η reflection, K↔π swap or replacement of one track
(track mixing). Leg mixing (pairs from different events) was used as a factorisation control for pairs only.
These methods keep the original vertex, which no longer matches the modified kinematics.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (candrot, Mixing nóg), §5 pkt 6 -->

### 3.5 K↔π hypothesis swap and PID arbiter
The same D⁰ track pair yields two candidates (K⁻π⁺ and π⁻K⁺). The wrong hypothesis is "background by construction":
identical tracks, vertex and kinematics as the signal, differing only in the mass assignment. A PID-only arbiter selects
the hypothesis from pixel dE/dx, number of dE/dx hits, log p, Bethe–Bloch pulls and Δχ² — explicitly *without* α,
cos θ*, pT, which reconstruct the mass and sculpt the background. The classifier is antisymmetric (P_A + P_B = 1),
k-fold OOF by event; v2 operates per track (`mode: pertrack`). Alternatives: random hash, `closest_mass`
(hypothesis closer to m₀ = correct), `keep_both`.
<!-- src: docs/hyp.md (Idea, Wyniki) -->

The truth of the swap in MC is taken either from the `closest_mass` heuristic (v1.1, definitional) or from
`Dmeson_hypo_swap` built from the daughters' truth PDG IDs (v1.2, `signal.truth_swap_source: pdgid`).
<!-- src: docs/refl_truth.md (Po co, Konfiguracja); CHANGELOG.md (Niewydane 2026-11-16) -->

### 3.6 D*-tagged D⁰ as a data-driven signal sample; scale factors
The only signal sample available in data is D⁰ from D*⁺→D⁰π⁺ₛ, statistically separated in Δm with sWeights/COWs per pT
bin. It is used to (i) compare all 18 H features data vs MC, (ii) measure the classifier efficiency scale factor
SF = ε_S(data)/ε_S(MC) per WP via a pass/fail Δm fit, sWeights and sideband counting, and SF_rel = SF(WP)/SF(WP95), and
(iii) measure the reflection fraction R/S and mass-tail fractions in data.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3a (Strona sygnałowa B2); charm_work/WK2_wklad_AFP/WKLAD.md (#2) -->

## 4 Validation

### 4.1 Closure tests on MC
All generators were validated by closure on MC: control objects in the SR versus true background in the SR, via C2ST,
ε_B ratio at three WPs, and χ²/ndf of the mass shape. A hand-written physics discriminant (no labels) and mass-decorrelation
diagnostics (symmetric R_win) were used as sculpting controls. Rule adopted after an independent review: SR and SB are
evaluated by the *same* classifier, and every fit is preceded by a signal-free MC test (an OOF/ensemble mismatch had produced
a fake "16σ" peak with 124 true D⁰).
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (Ręczny dyskryminant, Dekorelacja masy), §5 pkt 2 -->

| Method (D⁰ unless noted) | C2ST | ε_B ratio @WP80/70/50 | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Both sidebands | 0.55 | 1.02 / 1.07 / 1.32 | baseline |
| Far SB + reflection veto | — | 0.91 / 0.93 / 1.06 | **nominal training background** |
| SALAD (D⁰ / D⁺) | 0.507 / 0.510 (floor 0.50); data band 0.510–0.515 | pred/true 0.98/0.94/0.88 (D⁰); 0.98/0.93/1.04 (D⁺) | systematic + ε_B estimator |
| FETA (CPU) | 0.63 → 0.60 (base-flow limit 0.618) | — | cross-check, pending GPU |
| Flow p(x\|m) (CPU) | 0.61 | — | does not beat SB |
| candrot | 0.7–0.97 | ×0.2–0.7 | rejected (Z −7…−32 %) |
| Leg mixing (pairs) | 0.63–0.91 vs same-event pairs | ABCD closure 1.002/1.003 | pair control only |
| pseudo-WS D⁺ (fit shape) | — | N_pred/N_true 0.99/0.97/0.97, but full fit χ²/ndf 3–6, N_sig ×1.4–2.5 | refuted |
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (tabela Katalog metod), all rows -->
<!-- NOTE: "—" = number not given in the source docs; fill from the study notes S4/S5/S6/S9 if needed. -->

### 4.2 Adversarial closure (B1): C2ST is blind to background composition

**This is the central methodological result of project B.**

SALAD assumes that the background *composition* varies smoothly with mass. To test whether the standard acceptance
criterion (C2ST close to 0.50) protects against a violation of this assumption, pseudo-data were built from D⁺ MC with
deliberate distortions of the background-category composition (not of a smooth function of m), and SALAD was applied
blind:

| Distortion of pseudo-data | C2ST (SALAD, SR) | ε_B pred/true @WP80 / 70 / 50 |
|---|---|---|
| smooth in m (as in the standard closure) | 0.520 | 1.01 / 1.03 / 1.10 |
| global composition (MisMatch* ×1.5 everywhere) | 0.501 | 0.91 / 0.86 / 0.80 |
| **local composition under the peak** (+4.7 pp MisMatch*, σ = 35 MeV) | **0.507** | **0.85 / 0.81 / 0.70** |
| step in m (left SB ×1.5) | 0.503 | 0.94 / 0.93 / 0.85 |
| local physics cuts (Lxy, IP significance) | 0.536 | 0.92 / 0.95 / 0.94 |
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3a (Closure adwersarialny B1, tabela) -->

Findings:

1. **C2ST is blind to the background composition.** A localised composition change under the peak gives C2ST = 0.507,
   at the floor of the test, while ε_B is mis-predicted by 15–30 %. C2ST is a *necessary precondition*, not a test.
2. **Sensitivity**: −3.3 / −4.1 / −6.4 % in ε_B per +1 pp of MisMatch* under the peak at WP80/70/50; this component passes
   into ε_B without correction.
3. **The only test that detects composition** is ε_B(SR) from a pol2 fit to data after the cut. On data,
   SALAD/data = 1.02 ± 0.03 / 1.09 ± 0.04 / 1.25 ± 0.13 (hgb), which bounds an unknown composition change to
   |Δf_MM| ≲ 1.5 pp at WP80.
4. **Signal leakage from the SB edge**: a 2 % signal admixture at 70–90 MeV shifts ε_B by +2.4 / +3.8 / +6.5 % with no C2ST
   response → reflection/D* veto before the fit is mandatory and SB_min = 70 MeV.
5. **Condition of use for SALAD v1.0**: agreement of ε_B with the data fit within ±5/10 %; C2ST ≤ 0.53 only as a
   preliminary check.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3a (Wnioski 1–4, Warunek użycia v1.0) -->

**D⁰ (same protocol, h5 v1.1 subsamples after the reflection veto).** C2ST is again blind to composition (local composition:
C2ST 0.501; admixture of partially matched charm, truth 2–7: 0.501). D⁰ is about 3× less sensitive than D⁺. MisMatch* is
5.4 % of the SR background (D⁺: 8.9 %) and is only ×1.8 more signal-like than OtherNoB (D⁺: ×5). The sensitivity is
−1.0 / −1.7 / −4.6 % per +1 pp MisMatch* at WP80/70/50. A local +2.8 pp change gives pred/true 0.97 / 0.95 / 0.85.
The criterion |pred/true − 1| ≤ 10 % at WP80/70 holds in all D⁰ modes (it failed for D⁺).
D⁰ has its own weak point: truth 2–7, 1.05 % of the SR background, smooth in m. C2ST does not see it and SALAD with
background MC `truth ≥ 8` does not correct it (pred/true at WP50 0.88; data 0.84). The fix is a configuration change,
`mc_truth_min: 2`: closure 1.00 / 1.00 / 0.99, and SALAD/data 1.05 / 1.03 / 0.98. This change is not yet in the frozen
v1.0 yaml. Residual K↔π swaps after the 25 MeV veto are 0.025 % of the SR background, so |Δε_B| < 0.3 % at WP80/70: the
veto is sufficient.
<!-- src: charm_work/B1_salad_v1/out_D0/charm-B1-salad-v1.md §6 (6a, 6c, 6e, 6f); out_D0/b1_closure_D0.md; closure_*_hgb.json.
     NOTE: repo docs/signal_like_background.md §6 still lists the D⁰ adversarial closure as open — not yet synchronised. -->

The D⁺ and D⁰ adversarial points are shown in [Fig. F4](figures/F4_B1_blind_quadrant.pdf): C2ST vs |pred/true − 1| at
each WP. Points in the shaded quadrant pass the C2ST criterion but mis-predict ε_B by more than 5 %.
<!-- F4 input: B1_salad_v1/out/closure_{smooth,cat_global,cat_local,cat_step,cuts_local}_hgb.json (D⁺),
     out_D0/closure_{…,cat_local_MM,real_t27}_hgb.json (D⁰); values reproduced exactly from the tables above (F4_points.json). -->

*Relation to literature.* The general fact that AUC ≈ 0.5 can hide local or tail discrepancies is known
[2305.16774; 2310.08994; see also 2409.16336, 2508.02275]; C2ST itself follows [1610.06545]. What is specific here is
an adversarial closure designed against the particular assumption of the method (smooth composition in m) on a real SM
resonance, and the demonstration that the popular acceptance criterion "C2ST ≤ 0.52" does not guarantee a correct ε_B
after the cut.
<!-- src: charm_work/RECON_methods/RAPORT.md §1 teza (3) -->

### 4.3 Signal side
D*-tagged D⁰, 8–30 GeV: the 18 H features agree with MC at the level of a few tenths of σ_MC. In B2, all features had
|Δμ| ≤ 0.2 σ_MC except |cos θ*| at −0.35 σ_MC, which depends on the background level α. On the full luminosity (B2b,
sWeights) the largest shifts are |cos θ*| −0.22 σ, d_max_abs_d0 +0.32 σ, m_swap_dist −0.25 σ and Lxy +0.46 σ (a tail
effect). Together they are invisible to a classifier: the feature C2ST data-sW vs MC signal is 0.512 ± 0.008, against a
MC–MC floor of 0.501 ± 0.022 (AUC − floor = 0.011 ± 0.023).
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3a (B2); charm_work/B2b_dstar/charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md §0 (e), §6.
     v0.2 said "all 18 within |Δμ| ≤ 0.2 σ_MC (largest −0.35 σ)" — internally inconsistent and superseded by B2b §6. -->

## 5 Results

### 5.1 Classifier ceiling
Zproxy at WP70 (D⁰, MC closure) for different training backgrounds:

| Training background | SB (nominal) | SALAD | FETA | **ideal (true bkg)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Zproxy @WP70 (setup S4) | **4.93** | 4.96 | 4.83 | **4.95** |

([Fig. F6](figures/F6_zproxy_ceiling.pdf); plotted from the tabulated values, no per-run file is kept.)

In an earlier, independent setup (study S, SB baseline 3.45) flow p(x|m) gave 3.20 (−7 %) and the copula 2.56 (−26 %);
far SB + veto vs near SB: 3.43 vs 3.46 (S5). These are relative statements within that setup and are not comparable to the
S4 row in absolute terms.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3 (tabela Zproxy), §3a (Sufit); README.md -->

SB, SALAD and ideal background agree within ±1 %: the classifier trained on data sidebands is **at the information ceiling
of the background**. On data (fit after the cut) there is no gain either: SB 1.6/2.7/3.5, FETA 1.6/2.4/3.4, SALAD 1.1/2.3/3.1 (Z = S/σ_S at WP80/70/50; setup S4: D⁰, pT 3–30 GeV, subsample data_p0, mass fit after the cut on half B). Further background generators (FETA on GPU, flow, CATHODE, copula) are closed as a lever on Z.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3, §3a; WP labels and sample: charm_work/S4_feta/README_S4.md l.52–56 -->
This confirms a known mechanism (cf. the "idealized AD" benchmark in CATHODE [2109.00546]); the new element is the
quantification on a supervised heavy-flavour classifier with an explicit ideal-background upper bound.
<!-- src: charm_work/RECON_methods/RAPORT.md §1 teza (1) -->

Weak supervision: CATHODE on data at S/B ≈ 0.2 % is blind; after a preselection raising S/B to ≈ 3.8 %, with SALAD as
reference background, it sees the D⁰ peak (Z 5.0 vs 3.7 for preselection alone), still below supervised XGB. SALAD as
reference ≈ ideal (SIC 7.2 vs 6.4 at S/B 3 %). Anomaly detection for D–D pairs is closed: pair S/B in data ≈ 0.05 %,
10–20× below the SIC > 1 threshold.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2 (Flow + CATHODE), §3 (Tło odniesienia dla CATHODE; Anomaly detection dla par) -->
Comparable known-SM-peak AD result: Υ in CMS Open Data [2502.14036]; threshold behaviour cf. [2104.02092, 2501.01778].

### 5.2 SALAD as ε_B systematic and estimator
- **Background-shape systematic** (SALAD vs SB): **±5 % @WP80, ±10 % @WP70, ±25 % @WP50** (D⁺; WP50 driven by the B1
  composition sensitivity). For D⁰ the B1-D⁰ study recommends ±5 / ±10 % at WP80/70, and at WP50 ±15 % with
  `mc_truth_min: 2` or ±20 % with `truth ≥ 8`. Other components: exp vs pol2 ±3–4 %; reflection hybrid vs MC ±1 % in Z.
  <!-- src (D⁰): charm_work/B1_salad_v1/out_D0/charm-B1-salad-v1.md §6f -->
- **ε_B estimator under the peak**: the proxy from asymmetric SBs underestimates ε_B by ~20 % (background ε_B falls with
  m by ×2.7); symmetric SBs or SALAD give 1.0–1.1 of the truth.
- Data, D⁺ subsample: AUC_SB 0.643, n_eff/N 0.67, band C2ST 0.507.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3 (Właściwe zastosowania), §3a -->

**Run 427929 (SALAD_427929, 17.11).** The production configuration was repeated per trigger class on full statistics. In the clean minimum-bias run the D⁰ gates pass (n_eff/N = 0.93) and the prediction under the peak agrees with the sideband interpolation: R − 1 = +3.6 ± 4.7 % (WP80), +4.1 ± 6.6 % (WP70), +6.1 ± 11.1 % (WP50) for all pT, and +3.2 / +3.4 / +8.0 % at 3–5 GeV; 5–8 and 8–30 GeV are inconclusive (+7.8 ± 5.7 % and +8.6 ± 10.0 % at WP80). No cell exceeds the budget by 2σ in any class. For D⁺ the test under the peak is not informative (σ = 17 % at WP80, all from the sideband interpolation); in the validation band SALAD closes 3–5 GeV (1.186 → 1.008 ± 0.009 at WP80) but not 8–30 GeV (0.873 ± 0.053). Weights trained on 427929 and applied to the J12-only data of 428770 reproduce ε_B for all pT (1.052 / 1.018 / 0.993 ± 0.034) but not the feature distributions (C2ST 0.637), and fail at 5–8 GeV (1.171 ± 0.060). The sensitivity to a wrong background composition is unchanged: with a locally enhanced component SALAD predicts 0.857 / 0.797 / 0.727 of the true ε_B for D⁺ and 0.974 / 0.941 / 0.873 for D⁰, the same as the unweighted MC. Source: `charm_work/SALAD_427929/WYNIKI.md`.

### 5.3 D*-tag scale factors
Abbreviations: pf = pass/fail fit of Δm, sb = sideband subtraction (the background under the peak is normalised from a fit with a threshold function, not from a linear interpolation of the sidebands; checked for every use of the D* tag on 20.11, Sec. 7), sW = sWeights (defined in Sec. 5.6). B1, B2, B2b, EV, S, S4, S5 and
V are internal names of studies, kept for traceability to the sources. The letter R denotes several
different ratios, each defined where it is used (R/S, R_sculpt, R_SF, R − 1 of SALAD, R of Sec. 6.5).
<!-- src (sideband wording, 20.11): charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1 (rows 1–4), §8 pkt 4 -->

| pT [GeV] | SF @WP95 / 90 / 80 / 70 / 50 (pf, full lumi) | SF_rel (÷WP95), full lumi: sW / pf | SF_rel, B2 (earlier) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8–30 | 1.00 / 0.98 / 0.96 / 0.96 / 0.91 (± 0.035–0.046) | sW 0.99 / 0.96 / 0.97 / 0.95 (± 0.01–0.03); pf 0.98 / 0.96 / 0.96 / 0.91 (± 0.05–0.06) @WP90/80/70/50 | 0.97 ± 0.05 (WP70–90); 0.94 ± 0.05 (WP50) |
| 5–8 | 0.83 / 0.85 / 0.85 / 0.90 / 0.93 (± 0.10–0.13) | sW 1.01 / 1.00 / 1.01 / 1.06 (± 0.04–0.07) | features not measurable (±0.15 in SF) |
| 3–5 | not determined (open interval, signal < 3σ) | not determined (sW n_eff = 14) | not measurable |

([Fig. F8](figures/F8_sf_vs_wp.pdf): pf SF and SF_rel vs WP, refit of 13.11.)
<!-- src (full lumi): charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md §0 (a), (e), §2 table; NUM_audit_2026-11-12/b2b_recompute/out/b2b_sf_new.json (F8 input).
     v0.2 quoted only the B2 SF_rel; the full-luminosity sW/pf values are added. pf SF_rel 8–30 no longer meets the < 0.05 error criterion. -->
<!-- src: charm_work/WK2_wklad_AFP/WKLAD.md #2 (SF per WP); docs/signal_like_background.md §3a (SF_rel, bins 5–8, 3–5) -->
<!-- src (WP labels 95/90/80/70/50, pass/fail fit, korekta 13.11): charm_work/B2b_dstar/charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md §1 (a) -->

Three methods × two windows agree within ±0.03. The upper bound on the gain from reweighting MC signal to data-sW and
retraining H is ≤ 3–5 % (WP ≥ 70), ≤ 5–8 % (WP50) — below the SF uncertainty → **no-go for signal reweighting**; SF_rel
enters as an ε_S systematic. Generative signal augmentation: gain ≈ 0.
<!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §3a (B2) -->

PID arbiter v2 on D*-tagged data: SF = 0.977 ± 0.041 (data = MC within 0.6σ); it does not sculpt (|Z| < 3 in 24 tests)
but differs from random only at 1.9σ, and after the r(m) correction it is equivalent to hash.
<!-- src: docs/hyp.md (Wyniki) -->

### 5.4 Reflections: R/S and hypothesis choice
- Reflection fraction R/S data/MC at WP70: **0.96 ± 0.04** (reflected over correctly assigned candidates inside the D⁰ mass window, against the truth of the forced-decay MC, L_xy ≥ 0.25 mm; pass/fail fit `fit_passfail`, 13.11; earlier fit 0.95) → the
  systematic on the reflection normalisation can be narrowed from ±10 % to ~±5 % at L_xy ≥ 0.25 mm (for L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm see Sec. 6.10: ±15 %). The reflection shape is not confirmed (Sec. 6.6).
  <!-- src: docs/hyp.md (R/S refleksji @WP70, t4 §4; updated 16.11); charm_work/HYP_d0/t4/t4_results.md §4–5 -->
- Toy dE/dx, correct-hypothesis efficiency: arbiter 54.8 % (92 % for p_min 0.7–1 GeV), hash 49.7 %, closest_mass 83 %
  **but sculpts the background (Z = +168)**, pid_kin sculpts (Z = +72).
- Single D⁰: `keep_both` best in all bins (σ_rel 7–15 % lower than v2) → adopted. Pairs: hash (separate analysis).
<!-- src: docs/hyp.md (Wyniki, Decyzje) -->

### 5.5 Sculpting of the MC signal by `closest_mass`
fd_nd MC, true D⁰ (321 378 rows; `hypo_swap` = 1: 106 390). The heuristic misassigns ~20 % of rows in each direction
(23 054 true swaps labelled signal, 21 866 correct labelled swap). Counts nearly cancel, shapes do not:

| WP | pT [GeV] | r = N_refl/N_sig (cm → pdgid) | signal RMS in ±100 MeV [MeV] | tail \|m−m₀\| > 55 MeV |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| none | 3–5 | 0.493 → 0.501 | 28.0 → 29.9 | 7.5 % → 9.1 % |
| none | 5–8 | 0.483 → 0.492 | 27.7 → 29.6 | 7.2 % → 8.8 % |
| none | 8–30 | 0.467 → 0.475 | 28.2 → 30.1 | 7.7 % → 9.3 % |
| WP70 | 3–5 | 0.600 → 0.609 | 26.9 → 29.2 | 6.5 % → 8.3 % |
| WP70 | 5–8 | 0.603 → 0.613 | 27.0 → 29.4 | 6.6 % → 8.6 % |
| WP70 | 8–30 | 0.567 → 0.576 | 27.9 → 30.2 | 7.3 % → 9.3 % |
<!-- src: docs/refl_truth.md (Wynik, tabele) -->

Conclusion: r changes by +1.5 % (below the ±5 % template systematic); the MC signal shape is artificially **narrowed** by
the heuristic (RMS +8–9 % and tail ×1.25–1.3 when using the truth), because `closest_mass` moves true D⁰ from the mass
tails into the "reflection" and near-m₀ swaps into the "signal". Reflection template mean/RMS essentially unchanged
(m − m₀ ≈ +7…+9 MeV; RMS 68–69 → 66–67 MeV). Recommendation: `truth_swap_source: pdgid` for v1.2.
[Fig. F9](figures/F9_refl_truth.pdf) shows the per-pT shapes. When the figure was made, the row counts above were
reproduced exactly with `charmbkg.samples.mark_swaps` (figures/F9_counts.json).
<!-- src: docs/refl_truth.md (Wnioski 1–2, 4); CHANGELOG.md -->

### 5.6 Anomaly at 5–8 GeV (treated as an ε_S systematic; independent test of 17.11 inconclusive)
**Method.** D⁰←D*⁺ pairs on the full luminosity, event-level (EV), duplicates removed (0.68 % of pairs). The nominal
estimator is the pass/fail fit of Δm (`fit_passfail`, two MC Gaussians with free scale + threshold background, profile
intervals; corrected 13.11); sideband subtraction (sb) and sWeights (sW) are cross-checks.
<!-- src: charm_work/B2b_dstar/charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md (KOREKTA 13.11; §1 tabela metod "fit Δm (pf)") -->

**Observation.** In 5–8 GeV at WP95, ε_data = 0.79 ± 0.09 (pf) vs ε_MC = 0.95 in the nominal ±55 MeV window →
**SF95 = 0.83 ± 0.10** (1.7σ from 1; sb 0.74 ± 0.06, sW 0.82 ± 0.07), flat in WP (SF 0.83/0.85/0.85/0.90/0.93 at
WP95…50, so SF_rel(÷WP95) = 1.00–1.06 ± 0.04–0.07, sW). In 8–30 GeV there is no effect (SF95 = 1.00).
<!-- src: charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md §0 (d), §2, §5 (iii).
     v0.2 quoted "sW 0.78 ± 0.06" — that is ε_data(sW) = 0.779 ± 0.064, not the SF; SF(sW) = 0.82 ± 0.07 (B2b §5). -->

**Status on the full luminosity with PDG-ID swap truth (EV_pdgid, 16.11).** The EV analysis was re-run with the MC swap
truth taken from `Dmeson_hypo_swap` (v1.2) instead of `closest_mass`. ε_MC is practically unchanged (+0.004). The result is
SF95 = 0.83 ± 0.07 (nominal window) and 0.85 ± 0.08 in the core ±36 MeV, for both truth definitions; 8–30 GeV gives
1.00 ± 0.03. With the pf estimator the deficit is therefore **not confined to the ring**: the core has the same SF. The sb estimator disagrees
(core 0.97 ± 0.10, ring 0.45 ± 0.09, table below), so a mass-tail origin is not excluded by sb alone. The anomaly is also smaller
on the full luminosity than on the subsample (ε_data 0.74 → 0.79). The hypothesis "wider m(D⁰) tails in data" is **not
confirmed**. The anomaly is closed as an **ε_S systematic in 5–8 GeV**: |1 − SF| ≈ 0.17 at WP95, and SF = 0.86–0.90 ± 0.09–0.12
at WP70.
*Error caveat:* EV_pdgid used the study-V copy of the pass/fail fit (`EV_dstar/dstar_refit.py`). The 13.11 audit found
that this fit underestimates HESSE errors in 5–8 GeV by 15–32 %. The central values agree with the refit, but the
significance should be quoted with the refit error: SF95 = 0.83 ± 0.10, 1.7σ, not the ≈2.3σ implied by ± 0.07.
([Fig. F10](figures/F10_ev_anomaly.pdf): ring/core per pT and SF95 per m(D⁰) region, closest_mass vs PDG-ID.)
<!-- src: charm_work/EV_dstar/EV_pdgid.md (table, Wniosek); EV_dstar/ev_analysis.py l.22 imports local dstar_refit (etap V);
     B2b KOREKTA 13.11 (σ HESSE zaniżone o 15–32 % w 5–8). F10 input: EV_dstar/lxplus/ev_analysis_cm.json, EV_dstar/pdgid/ev_analysis.json. -->

**Independent check of the peak width (PEAK_pos, 16.11).** A separate fit of the D*-tagged m(Kπ) peak gives
σ_eff(data)/σ_eff(MC) = **0.95 ± 0.05** at WP70, averaged over pT 3–5/5–8/8–30 on the full luminosity. The fit model has two
MC Gaussians with a free common scale and a Chebyshev background, and selects |Δm − 145.43| < 1.2 MeV. In 5–8 GeV alone the
ratio is 0.97 ± 0.11. D⁺ (30 % luminosity) gives 0.97 ± 0.05, and the two combined give 0.956 ± 0.034. The D⁰ mass
resolution in data is therefore **not wider** than in MC, which independently disfavours hypothesis (i).
Note that the tight Δm window of this fit largely excludes the broad-Δm component (σ ≈ 2.3 MeV) that carries the
deficit. The two results are therefore consistent with a component that peaks broadly in Δm, not with a resolution effect
in m(D⁰). This reading is our interpretation and has not been tested separately.
<!-- src: charm_work/PEAK_pos/RAPORT.md (Krótko; §2 tabela "D⁰ z tagu D*" WP70: 5–8 0.97 ± 0.11; §1 selekcja (2)).
     F13 shows the per-bin values (input PEAK_pos/out/peaks_rows.csv, dstar_rows.csv). -->

**Independent test on run 427929 and per trigger class (B_trig, 17.11, v2.0 files).** The new chain reproduces the
nominal value on 428770 (SF95 = 0.84 ± 0.10 vs 0.83 ± 0.10 in 5–8 GeV; 1.01 ± 0.04 vs 1.00 ± 0.04 in 8–30 GeV). SF95 per
trigger class and run, for 5–8 / 8–30 GeV:

| sample | SF95, 5–8 GeV | SF95, 8–30 GeV |
|---|---|---|
| 428770, L1_J12 only* | 0.90 ± 0.15 | 1.05 ± 0.04 |
| 428770, "MBTS-based" (93 % HLT_j20)* | 0.72 ± 0.12 | 0.97 ± 0.05 |
| 427929, MBTS | 1.05 ± 0.11 | 0.91 ± 0.07 |

"MBTS-based" is the name of the class in our code; it is 92 % HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 by events (93 % by pairs) and contains no events written by a minimum-bias chain (Secs. 2 and 6.11).

*MC efficiency with trigger-emulation weights that do not close (Sec. 6.2.2).

At WP95 the disjoint classes are mutually consistent (χ² p = 0.23 in 5–8 GeV and 0.13 in 8–30 GeV), so the classifier SF
shows no significant trigger dependence there. Run 427929 is +1.5σ from 0.83 and +0.5σ from 1: it **neither confirms nor
excludes** the deficit. Its D* yield is 0.30 ± 0.14 of that of 428770 in 5–8 GeV, so this is a consistency check, not a
precision measurement. Two caveats. At WP50 in 8–30 GeV the classes are less consistent (p = 0.02: L1_J12 only
1.15 ± 0.07, "MBTS-based" 0.84 ± 0.10). In 5–8 GeV the "MBTS-based" class is low at all working points
(0.72 / 0.70 / 0.70 ± 0.12–0.14 at WP95/80/50). Both statements depend on the emulated MC efficiencies.
([Fig. F15](figures/F15_clf_sf_trigger.pdf).)
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/RAPORT_DANE.md (G2, G6, tables WP 0.95/0.80/0.50), btrig_sf.md ("Testy zgodności");
     yield ratio: B_trig/PROCEDURA.md §6 (replaces the v1 "≈0.27×, [unverified]" outlook). F15 input: btrig_sf.json (figures/F15_points.json).
     v1 → v2: the v1 text "no significant trigger dependence" held for WP95 only; the WP50 result is now stated. -->

| 5–8 GeV, WP95 | SF95 pf | SF95 sb |
|---|---|---|
| core (< 36 MeV) | 0.84 ± 0.10 | 0.97 ± 0.10 |
| ring (36–55 MeV) | not determined (signal < 3σ) | 0.45 ± 0.09 |
| nominal (< 55 MeV) | **0.83 ± 0.10** | 0.74 ± 0.06 |
<!-- src: charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md §3 "SF95 per region" (pf nowy / sb) -->

**Characterisation.** The deficit is ~17 ± 10 % of the Δm peak (pf; sb 26 ± 6 %, sW 18 ± 7 %), rejected entirely by H
at WP95, concentrated on the **low-mass side** of m(D⁰) (−55…−36 MeV: ε95 = 0.41 ± 0.15 pf vs 0.95 MC), with a **broad
Δm peak** (σ = 2.30 ± 0.37 MeV in the fail sample vs 0.82 ± 0.07 pass and 0.79 MC) and background-like features
(|cos θ*| ≈ 0.86, fewer K pixel hits, Lxy/d0 tails, soft π_s). In fd MC such a Δm-peaking component rejected at WP95 is
1.2 % of the signal (5–8) — ~15× less than in data. K↔π swaps pass WP95 (ε_MC 0.99–1.00) with the same share as in MC.
<!-- src: charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md §1 (d), §3 (szerokość nadwyżki Δm), §4 (wniosek) -->

**Interpretation (B2b verdict iii).** A feature of the D*-tag control sample (degraded track reconstruction, e.g. K decay
in flight, or Δm-peaking background), **not** a mismodelling of the H features: the m(D⁰) sW core is not wider than in MC,
pass95 features agree (|Δμ| ≤ 0.3 σ_MC), C2ST 5–8 = 0.506. Hypothesis (i), "wider resolution tails", is not supported.
In B2b it looked partly supported, because the sb estimator recovers SF95 0.97 ± 0.10 within ±36 MeV. On the full
luminosity, however, the pf core has the same deficit as the nominal window (0.84 ± 0.10 vs 0.83 ± 0.10), and the excess has
background features. The PEAK_pos width ratio of 0.95 ± 0.05 also shows no wider resolution. Using the pdgid swap truth
instead of `closest_mass` does not change the picture (core SF95 0.85 → 0.85; ring/core in MC +~15 %). The pf fit alone
sees the deficit at 1.7σ; the verdict rests jointly on sb, sW, m(D⁰) slices, Δm width and features.
**Practical consequence:** an ε_S systematic in 5–8 GeV (|1 − SF95| ≈ 0.17; B2b proposes SF(5–8) = 0.91 ± 0.10, which
covers 0.83–1.00 until the nature of the component is known), and no correction to H.
<!-- src: charm-B2b-dstar-anomalia.md §5 (tabela hipotez i–iii), §8 pkt 1; EV_dstar/EV_pdgid.md (Wniosek); docs/refl_truth.md; PEAK_pos/RAPORT.md -->

### 5.7 Simultaneous fit in score categories instead of a single cut (toy study, 17.11)
Not done before for single D⁰/D⁺ (score categories exist only in the pair fit). Pseudo-experiments with the MC signal and the
background level and shape from data sidebands (run 428770, 30 % of the events; 1000 toys per configuration; no fit of the signal peak in data). Statistical
gain in σ(N_S)/N_S of a simultaneous fit in K = 5 score categories (signal fractions from MC, background free per category)
over the best single working point, for pT 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV: D⁰ +14.8 ± 1.3 / +6.5 ± 1.3 / +5.5 ± 1.1 %; D⁺ +10.7 ± 1.5 /
+9.5 ± 1.1 / +9.4 ± 1.4 %. K = 2 gives nothing; the gain saturates at K = 5–8. The criterion fixed in advance (gain ≥ 10 % in two
pT bins with a bias below 0.2σ) is met only by D⁺ with K = 8. The best single working point at 3–5 GeV is 35–40 %, not 70 %.
Robustness: with MC fractions and the measured data/MC scale factors left uncorrected the bias is −4 to −10 %, the same as for an
uncorrected cut; with free fractions the fit is unbiased but as imprecise as a fit without any cut (σ = 8–84 %), so scale
factors are still needed; with fractions constrained by a Gaussian of the SF uncertainty the total error (statistical ⊕ SF) is
23–37 % smaller than for the cut at σ_SF = 5 % and 32–54 % smaller at 10 % — this assumes independent uncertainties at the
category boundaries. Mass sculpting of the background at the level R = 0.89 is not absorbed (categories worse than the cut by
0.2–0.45σ for D⁰); at R = 0.97 the difference is ≤ 0.1σ. Recommendation: use categories (K = 5 over the full score range,
fractions MC × SF with a constraint, background free per category) mainly as a way to reduce the scale-factor error; in a
TRExFitter analysis the categories are regions. Caveats: N_S is taken from the MC S/B and is probably too high for D⁰ at 5–8 GeV;
an unbinned 2D fit was not done (K = 17 used instead).
<!-- src: charm_work/FIT_scorebins/RAPORT.md, out/tables.md (17.11) -->

**On data (18.11, D⁰←D*, criteria fixed beforehand).** The same idea used as a calibration: simultaneous Δm fit in six score categories (boundaries at WP95/80/70/50/25 plus the rejected category, fractions summing to one), errors from a paired event bootstrap. It does not improve on the pass/fail measurement: SF(WP70) = 0.91 ± 0.13 (5–8 GeV) and 0.99 ± 0.04 (8–30 GeV) in run 428770, 1.00 ± 0.12 and 0.98 ± 0.10 in run 427929, with errors 0.91–1.00 of the pass/fail ones and a correlation of 0.95–0.99 between the two methods. The precision is limited by the D* statistics and by the background in the rejected category, not by the choice of a single working point. Two by-products: (i) the correlation of the scale factors between working points, ρ(95,80) = 0.78–0.87, ρ(95,70) = 0.75–0.84, ρ(80,70) = 0.94–0.97, ρ(70,50) = 0.86–0.97; (ii) post hoc, the 5–8 GeV deficit in 428770 (Sec. 5.6) is not significant in the score shape (χ² = 7.2/5): the upper half of the score distribution agrees with MC (0.91 ± 0.14, 0.99 ± 0.16) and the deficit is signal assigned to the rejected category, where S/B ≲ 1.5 % and the background model decides. At 8–30 GeV the signal fraction above WP25 is low in data, 0.72 ± 0.05 (428770) and 0.74 ± 0.12 (427929); this concerns working points tighter than WP50 only. Exploratory follow-up (post hoc, local significances): about two thirds of the effect in 428770 is the pT spectrum inside the wide bin. The jet-triggered data have 64 % of the D* signal above 12 GeV against 28 % in MC, and in MC the fraction above a single WP25 threshold falls from 0.28 at 8–10 GeV to 0.08 at 20–30 GeV. The effect is absent at 8–12 GeV (ratio of the yields above WP25 and above WP50, data/MC: 1.01 ± 0.06) and sits at 12–30 GeV (0.66 ± 0.05); reweighting the MC to the data pT spectrum brings the score shape into agreement (χ² 97/13 → 16.5/13). A residual of 0.88 ± 0.04 for the two runs combined is left unexplained; in 427929 the pT spectra agree, so the explanation does not apply there (0.76 ± 0.10). A different non-prompt fraction, the K↔π swap, the Δm window, the background model and single input features are excluded as the main cause. Consequence: in run 428770 scale factors for working points tighter than WP50 must be measured in narrow pT bins or with the MC reweighted to the data spectrum.

### 5.8 Signal from data instead of background from data (SIG_constr, 17.11)
The reverse of the main idea: use the D*-tagged D⁰ as a data-driven signal sample for training or for the efficiency. Effective
signal size after sideband subtraction (background normalised from a threshold-function fit; score_H > 0.6): 440 in 427929 (184/240/107 for 3–5/5–8/8–30 GeV) and 894 in the
L1_J12-only class of 428770 (126/249/911); sWeights are unstable below a purity of 0.5. The learning curve needs about 3000 signal
events to come within 5 % of the full training for the nominal inputs (about 1000 with isolation), so training from scratch on
data is not feasible: it reaches 0.90–0.93 of the nominal Z, fine-tuning 0.97–0.98. With isolation among the inputs, training on
the data signal helps only in L1_J12-only (+5 to +12 %, above 3σ), because the MC model has the wrong isolation there; it does not
help in 427929. Reweighting the MC signal to the data signal: for ptcone40 < 2 GeV in L1_J12-only the pT-only reweighting gives
data/MC 0.62 ± 0.04; weights that do not use the cone tracks give 0.90 ± 0.09 overall but do not close per pT bin (pulls +2.6 and
+3.0 at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV); weights that use them close (0.99 ± 0.13) but with an error larger than the direct measurement; weights
do not transfer from 427929 to L1_J12-only (pull +7.7). For the nominal classifier everything closes, but the reweighting changes ε
by at most 2 %. Verdicts against criteria fixed in advance: no gain in significance for the nominal classifier; no reduction of the
ε_S systematic; as an isolation calibration it is usable with conditions — ε_S is measurable to 3–4 %, but the transfer from
D*-tagged to inclusive D⁰ fails for an absolute cut at 8–30 GeV (−10 % in MC truth) and holds for ptcone40/pT and for pT < 8 GeV;
the transfer for a network output with isolation was not tested. Embedding (full run): true-D⁰ kinematics from MC placed into data events, isolation computed from the data tracks.
In L1_J12-only (3–30 GeV) the tag gives ε(ptcone40 < 2 GeV) = 0.387 ± 0.016; a random direction gives 0.809 (difference
0.42 ± 0.02; 0.24 ± 0.02 even in 427929), a placement reweighted to the position relative to jets built without the cone tracks
gives 0.597 (difference 0.21 ± 0.02, the pre-registered test: failed at 13σ), and a placement reweighted to the distance and pT of
the nearest track jet as measured for true D⁰ gives 0.408 ± 0.014 (difference 0.02 ± 0.02; the same 0.03 residual appears in MC).
The last variant is about 80 % circular: ε ≈ (1 − f_IN)·ε_OUT, with f_IN the fraction of D⁰ that have a track jet above 2 GeV
within ΔR < 0.4, taken from the tag itself (0.564 in L1_J12-only against 0.236 in 427929 and in MC); the non-circular part is too
high by 0.040 ± 0.020. Embedding is therefore not a calibration. It is consistent with the following mechanism: about 80 % of the deficit is the higher
fraction of D⁰ sitting next to a track jet in jet-triggered events, the rest is the raised pedestal. This does not contradict
Sec. 6.2.4, where harder jets (pT > 10 GeV, or > 5 GeV outside the cone) do not explain the deficit: a track jet above 2 GeV within
ΔR < 0.4 largely overlaps the isolation cone itself. Caveat: reduced classifier settings were used.
<!-- src: charm_work/SIG_constr/RAPORT.md §6.3–§6.4, KRYTERIA.md, out/tables.md, out/s7_embed.json, out/s7b_wb_circ.json (17.11); sideband wording (20.11): charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1 row 13 -->

### 5.9 D* background by construction: D⁰ × free track (DSTWS, 17.11)

**Status: rejected as a replacement for the threshold function.** A D⁰ candidate with an untouched vertex is combined with any other track of the event (pT > 0.2 GeV): right sign (RS), wrong sign (WS), RS rotated in φ (ROT), RS track from another event of similar multiplicity (MIX). The WS, ROT and MIX Δm shapes were tried as background templates in the pass/fail fit of the D*-tag scale factor. Full runs, pT 3–30 GeV; criteria fixed before the data.

- Validation: 99.2–99.4 % of the nominal D⁰←D* pairs are recovered, ⟨δΔm⟩ ≤ 0.01 MeV.
- WS is not signal-free: WS combinations containing the true D* pion (mostly through the K↔π twin of the D⁰) are 30–44 % of the signal in the signal MC (36–118 % in the minimum-bias MC). The template peaks and subtracts signal: N_fit/N_true = 0.86–0.91.
- ROT and MIX reproduce the true RS background shape in the minimum-bias MC (χ²/ndf ≤ 1.5 in 8 of 9 categories) but not in the signal MC (χ²/ndf 4–120).
- Data, run 428770, WP80: SF = 0.49 ± 0.06 (ROT) and 0.46 ± 0.05 (MIX) at 3–5 GeV and 0.49 ± 0.04 at 5–8 GeV, against 0.96 ± 0.27 and 0.84 ± 0.11 with the threshold function. ROT and WS shapes differ (χ²/ndf = 146/38 to 222/38). Adding a linear correction moves ROT to 0.79 ± 0.17. In run 427929 the templates agree with the nominal within errors at 5–30 GeV (0.89–0.97 against 0.95–1.01).
- The 3–5 GeV bin stays unmeasurable in 427929 with every template; in 428770 ROT and MIX give a defined fit, but its value is not trusted (previous item).

Conclusion: in the jet-triggered run the tracks around a D⁰ are correlated with it, and rotation or mixing removes this correlation; WS keeps it but carries signal. None of the templates meets the closure criterion on MC (|ΔN| < 10 %), although the threshold function does not meet it either (N_fit/N = 1.18–1.26, due to D* pions paired with non-signal D⁰). The threshold function stays. Source: `charm_work/DSTWS/RAPORT.md`.

### 5.10 D⁺ classifier with Dalitz and daughter-pT features (H+DLZ) on data; new D⁰ features (17.11)

**Status: H+DLZ not accepted; H stays nominal for D⁺.** Criteria were fixed before the D⁺ peak in data was looked at. Full v2.0 samples, WP70, pT bins 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV.

- Statistical gain in σ(N)/N from the mass fit: +20.7 ± 2.9 / +9.1 ± 3.0 / +4.2 ± 2.9 % in run 427929 and +32.9 ± 3.5 / +10.9 ± 3.1 / +6.4 ± 1.4 % in run 428770. Caveat: at the same MC signal efficiency H+DLZ returns a larger fitted yield (N_DLZ/N_H = 1.16 ± 0.09 / 1.10 ± 0.04 / 1.00 ± 0.04 in 427929 and 1.53 ± 0.14 / 1.17 ± 0.05 / 1.03 ± 0.02 in 428770), so part of the gain below 8 GeV is a larger fitted N_S, not a smaller error.
- Signal modelling (sWeighted C2ST, data against MC) is not worse than for H: Δ = −0.032 ± 0.006 and −0.028 ± 0.003.
- Relative scale factor, SF_rel(H+DLZ)/SF_rel(H) with WP90 as reference: 0.73 ± 0.07 / 0.88 ± 0.07 / 0.86 ± 0.06 (427929) and 0.53 ± 0.06 / 0.74 ± 0.09 / 0.88 ± 0.03 (428770). The same quantity on minimum-bias MC, where the true value is 1, is 0.59 ± 0.07 / 0.81 ± 0.07 / 0.93 ± 0.09 for H+DLZ (1.20 ± 0.14 / 0.92 ± 0.08 / 0.98 ± 0.10 for H). The measure does not close for H+DLZ below 8 GeV, because the WP90 reference fit overestimates the yield; the deficit in data is therefore not evidence of feature mismodelling, but the efficiency of H+DLZ cannot be calibrated with this fit.
- Background sculpting: the ratio R of the background efficiency in the 45–70 MeV band to the sideband extrapolation moves from 0.93 to 1.03 at 3–5 GeV (closer to 1) but from 0.99 to 1.11 ± 0.04 at 5–8 GeV in 427929; the fitted peak width grows by 10 ± 4 % and 13 ± 5 % at 5–8 GeV.

Condition for return: an efficiency measurement that closes on MC at loose working points (reference without a score cut, or the simultaneous fit in score categories of Sec. 5.7).

**D⁰, new feature groups** (ΔZproxy/Z_H at WP70; h5 v1.2 subsamples: 6 of 39 data files of run 428770, 40 of 80 minimum-bias MC files; data sidebands of run 428770): daughter impact parameters give +10.6 ± 0.5 / +8.1 ± 1.0 / +8.0 ± 2.5 % on MC and ×1.095 ± 0.006 / 1.053 ± 0.013 / 1.057 ± 0.017 after transfer to data sidebands, with no additional sculpting (mass-regression R² 0.277 against 0.278 for H) and ΔC2ST = +0.005. Decay kinematics reconstruct the mass (R² 0.80) and are rejected; decay time, Bethe-Bloch PID pulls and the two-variable kinematic set give nothing (|Δ| ≤ 1 % at 3–8 GeV). The D*-tag scale factor of a tree with the impact-parameter and decay-time inputs added to H (trained on data sidebands, v2.0 files) equals that of H: 1.06 ± 0.13 / 0.98 ± 0.09 against 1.01 ± 0.13 / 0.97 ± 0.10 in run 427929 and 0.87 ± 0.12 / 0.97 ± 0.04 against 0.88 ± 0.12 / 0.97 ± 0.04 in run 428770 (WP70, 5–8 / 8–30 GeV; 3–5 GeV not measurable); its gain on data is 1.08 / 1.06 / 1.06, below the 10 % threshold set for a change of the nominal classifier. Sources: `charm_work/DLZ_dplus/RAPORT.md` §9, `charm_work/DLZ_d0/RAPORT.md`.
- Cut scan and fit-bias test (criteria fixed beforehand; from the post-cut histograms of the same job, WP50/70/80/90, pseudo-data with a parametric signal on an exp(pol4) sideband background). The ratio (N/ε_MC)_H+DLZ / (N/ε_MC)_H at WP70, corrected for the fit bias, is 1.02 ± 0.04 / 1.06 ± 0.04 / 0.95 ± 0.04 in run 427929 and 1.11 ± 0.07 / 1.01 ± 0.05 / 0.98 ± 0.02 in run 428770; averaged over working points and bins 0.990 ± 0.015 and 1.010 ± 0.012. The signal efficiency of H+DLZ relative to H is therefore modelled to 4–7 % per bin, and the scale-factor deficit above is mostly a fit artefact. The same test shows that the nominal exp(pol2) background makes the fit overestimate the H+DLZ yield at WP70 by 7–14 % at 3–5 GeV and 3–7 % above (H: within 1 %, except −7 % at 5–8 GeV in 428770), so part of the statistical gain quoted above is a larger fitted yield. The bias is a lower bound: peaking components are not in the pseudo-data. By the pre-set criteria the test is inconclusive in 427929 and fails flatness at 8–30 GeV in 428770, where H itself is not flat. H stays nominal.
- Gain re-measured on the per-candidate data with a more flexible background (paired event bootstrap, 200 replicas; gain defined as above, WP70, average over the three bins): with the same polynomial order for both classifiers it is +11.3 ± 1.7 / +10.7 ± 2.0 / +8.9 ± 2.4 % for exp(pol2) / exp(pol3) / exp(pol4) in run 427929 and +16.7 ± 1.6 / +14.9 ± 1.9 / +12.9 ± 2.3 % in run 428770; nearly all of it comes from 3–5 GeV (+19.2 ± 3.6 % and +29.7 ± 4.0 % with exp(pol3)). Choosing the order separately per classifier by an F-test moves the gain by 7–12 points in single bins (H+DLZ needs pol3 at 3–5 GeV, H does not), so a common order is required. The fit error is reliable for pol2–pol4 (pull width 0.89–1.19). With exp(pol3) the uncorrected ratio (N/ε_MC)_H+DLZ / (N/ε_MC)_H in 427929 is 1.03 / 1.08 / 0.96 (± 0.04–0.05) at WP70 and 0.99–1.02 ± 0.025 averaged over WP50–90; an excess remains at WP80–90 in 3–5 GeV (1.18–1.21), and in 428770 at 3–5 GeV the ratio rises with the working point for every order. The fit bias could not be determined to 5 %: it depends on the sideband variant used to build the pseudo-data background, and the same interpolation fails by up to 9–15 % in a signal-free gap. **Conclusion: a real gain of about 10 % (427929) to 15 % (428770), concentrated at 3–5 GeV, with the efficiency modelled as well as for H at WP ≤ 70; not adopted, because the fit bias is not demonstrated.**
- Fit bias from minimum-bias MC with its full background (true yield known, true background under the peak; criteria fixed beforehand; WP70, common exp(pol3)): N_fit/N_true for H / H+DLZ is 1.04 ± 0.05 / 1.09 ± 0.04 (3–5 GeV), 1.11 ± 0.07 / 1.12 ± 0.06 (5–8 GeV) and 0.98 ± 0.07 / 1.01 ± 0.05 (8–30 GeV); the difference between the classifiers is compatible with zero (+0.05 ± 0.04, +0.01 ± 0.05, +0.03 ± 0.04). The 5 % requirement is not met for H+DLZ and the MC sample (about the size of run 427929) has no power to establish it. The excess is combinatorial background absorbed by the peak, not reflections; at 3–5 GeV it grows with looser working points for H+DLZ (post hoc). The true gain on MC, +12.2 ± 2.1 %, agrees with the data of run 427929. The line is closed here; a decision would need a minimum-bias MC sample at least four times larger. Note that the nominal H fit itself shows 1.11 ± 0.07 at 5–8 GeV with exp(pol3).

### 5.11 Neural network against the tree (DNET stages 1 to 6, 17–20.11)

**Status: the tree (XGB H) stays for D⁰; no network configuration without isolation is accepted.** Residual MLP (about 56k weights), 5-fold out-of-fold by event, mass decorrelation by distance correlation (DisCo) with weight λ; criteria fixed before the full-statistics results. G = Zproxy relative to XGB H at WP70, pT 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV; "data sidebands" are those of run 428770.

- Same 18 inputs: network 1.04 / 1.08 / 1.07 on MC, tree trained on the same MC background 1.03 / 1.06 / 1.07. The technology does not matter.
- D⁰, H plus decay kinematics, λ = 5 (3 seeds): 1.159 ± 0.002 / 1.167 ± 0.004 / 1.142 ± 0.008 on MC but 1.03 / 1.05 / 1.04 with the background efficiency from data sidebands. The gain does not transfer.
- Without decorrelation the kinematic inputs sculpt the background mass: R_sculpt = 10.9 (λ = 0), 2.60 (λ = 1), 0.99 (λ = 5); λ = 25 removes the gain, λ = 100 destroys the classifier. A tree with the same inputs has R_sculpt = 1.07 ± 0.01.
- With isolation inputs, λ = 5: 1.34 / 1.35 / 1.34 on MC and 1.61 / 1.59 / 1.40 on data sidebands (run 428770, a jet-triggered skim: an upper estimate, see Stage 3), R_sculpt = 1.00. D*-tag scale factor at WP70: 1.04 ± 0.13 / 0.92 ± 0.09 (5–8 / 8–30 GeV) in run 427929, 0.81 ± 0.11 / 0.94 ± 0.04 in run 428770 (0.85 ± 0.04 at WP50, 8–30 GeV). (19.11: these scale factors hold for D⁰ from D* only, see Stages 4 and 5 below.) This configuration has one training seed, and the 5–8 GeV values of the two runs differ by 1.4σ. Without isolation the network SF equals that of H.
- D⁺, H+DLZ inputs, λ = 5 (3 seeds): 1.256 ± 0.010 / 1.201 ± 0.005 / 1.107 ± 0.016 on MC, 1.24 / 1.16 / 1.04 on data sidebands; corr(score, m) = 0.02 against −0.22 for the tree. No efficiency calibration exists for D⁺ (Sec. 5.10).

**Stage 2 (18.11): track-set models.** Inputs are the 18 H features plus a set of tracks (the two daughters, optionally the tracks within ΔR < 0.4 or < 1.0 of the candidate; 11 features per track), DeepSets or a small transformer, DisCo λ = 5; numbers are relative to the XGBoost H reference at WP70 in 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV.
- Daughters only (transformer, 3 seeds): 1.042 ± 0.007 / 1.061 ± 0.008 / 1.050 ± 0.015 on MC and 0.94 / 0.98 / 0.97 on the sidebands of run 428770. The architecture and the per-daughter track parameters bring nothing.
- Daughters plus tracks within ΔR < 0.4 (transformer, 3 seeds): 1.450 ± 0.024 / 1.615 ± 0.032 / 1.546 ± 0.008 on MC and 1.54–1.79 on the 428770 sidebands (1.31–1.44 on the 427929 sidebands, see Stage 3); R_sculpt = 0.975 ± 0.015, corr(score, m) < 0.01. DeepSets with the same inputs gives 1.20–1.26 on MC and 1.29–1.47 on data; ΔR < 1.0 gives 1.62–1.71 and 1.75–1.94 (one seed each). This is more than the two isolation cones of stage 1.
- D*-tag scale factor at WP70 (5–8 / 8–30 GeV) for the ΔR < 0.4 transformer: 1.02 ± 0.11 / 0.97 ± 0.06 in run 427929 (H: 1.01 ± 0.13 / 0.97 ± 0.10) and 0.84 ± 0.11 / 0.90 ± 0.04 in run 428770 (H: 0.88 ± 0.12 / 0.97 ± 0.04); at WP50 in 428770, 8–30 GeV, 0.83 ± 0.04. Not measurable at 3–5 GeV. (19.11: these network scale factors hold for D⁰ from D* only, see Stages 4 and 5 below; the tree values are not affected.)
- Training with data sidebands added to the background gives the largest gain on data and the worst scale factor (0.79 ± 0.04 at 8–30 GeV, 0.65 ± 0.03 at WP50): the network learns the data/MC difference of the surroundings. Rejected.

**Status: not adopted as nominal.** The gain, the absence of mass sculpting and the seed stability pass the pre-set criteria; the calibration criterion fails in the jet-triggered run and passes in the minimum-bias run. The whole gain comes from the tracks around the candidate, and it carries the same data/MC cost as isolation: 7–10 % in signal efficiency at WP70 in 428770. A track-set classifier is usable where the sample is minimum-bias-like and a scale factor per trigger class is measured.

Source: `charm_work/DNET/RAPORT.md` §10–§12.

**Stage 3 (18.11): where the gain and the mismodelling come from; line closed.** Variants of the transformer with tracks within ΔR < 0.4, one seed, WP70, criteria fixed beforehand (`DNET/KRYTERIA_etap3.md`; the SF criterion has low power, so this is an indication).

| configuration | SF 428770, 8–30 GeV | gain on 428770 sidebands (3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV) | gain on 427929 sidebands |
|---|---|---|---|
| tree H | 0.97 ± 0.04 | 1 | 1 |
| full network (stage 2) | 0.90 ± 0.04 | 1.58 / 1.79 / 1.60 | 1.31 / 1.37 / 1.44 |
| surrounding tracks without momenta | 0.94 ± 0.04 | 1.14 / 1.26 / 1.28 | 1.02 / 1.01 / 1.17 |
| surrounding tracks with pT > 1 GeV only | 0.95 ± 0.04 | 1.21 / 1.35 / 1.40 | 1.01 / 1.04 / 1.19 |
| score decorrelated from activity on signal (λ = 2 / 10) | 0.93 / 0.91 ± 0.04 | 1.41–1.58 / 1.21–1.49 | 1.23–1.36 / 1.10–1.26 |
| score decorrelated from activity on background | 0.96 ± 0.04 | 0.86 / 1.05 / 0.96 | 0.88 / 1.02 / 0.99 |

Reading: (i) the gain of the network is the separation of background by the activity around the candidate: when the background score is made independent of the number and summed pT of surrounding tracks, the gain disappears and the scale factor returns to that of the tree; (ii) the calibration can be recovered by removing the soft tracks or their momenta, at the cost of most of the gain, so the gain and the data/MC difference sit in the same information; (iii) decorrelating the signal score from the activity works technically (distance correlation 5–10 times smaller) but recovers the scale factor only partly; (iv) **the gain on data has to be quoted from the minimum-bias run: 1.31 / 1.37 / 1.44 on the 427929 sidebands**, not 1.54–1.79 from the 428770 sidebands, which are a jet-triggered skim (Sec. 6.11) and the training sample of the reference tree. Two variants formally pass all four criteria; they were not taken to the three-seed confirmation, because in 427929 they give no gain below 8 GeV while the full network is calibrated there without changes (SF 1.02 ± 0.11 and 0.97 ± 0.06). Final state: the tree H stays nominal; the full network is a conditional candidate for a minimum-bias sample only, with a gain of 1.3–1.4 on data.
<!-- src: charm_work/DNET/RAPORT.md §13; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus3/dnet3_results.json -->

**Stages 4 and 5 (19.11): calibration on the inclusive peak; the D* tag does not calibrate a classifier that sees the surroundings.** Transformer with the tracks within ΔR < 0.4 (as in Stage 2) against the tree H, WP70, pT 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV, inclusive D⁰ and D⁺ without proton tag; criteria fixed beforehand (`DNET/KRYTERIA_etap4.md`, `DNET/KRYTERIA_etap5.md`) and read once.

- (i) **The network recognises the soft pion of the D\*.** On MC its signal efficiency at WP70 is 0.868 / 0.890 / 0.887 for D⁰ from D*, 0.630 / 0.608 / 0.604 for all other D⁰, and 0.617 / 0.600 / 0.597 for D⁰ from D* when the soft pion is masked in the input. The ratio of the first two, network over tree, is 1.37 / 1.45 / 1.44 (the tree alone: 1.00–1.02); the same holds for all three seeds and for the variant trained with data sidebands. **A scale factor from the D* tag therefore does not calibrate the inclusive sample for any classifier that sees the surroundings of the candidate.** All network scale factors quoted above in this section (Stages 1 to 3) hold for D⁰ from D* only, and part of the gain of the network on MC signal is a preference for D⁰ from D*. The scale factors of the tree H are not affected.
- (ii) **Relative calibration network/tree from the inclusive peak.** R = [N_S/ε_MC]_network / [N_S/ε_MC]_tree from a fit of the inclusive mass peak in score categories of the two classifiers, on the same candidates (the network SF would be R times the SF of the tree). For D⁰ the method does not close on MC with the required precision: R_fit − R_true = +0.16 ± 0.15 / +0.01 ± 0.06 / −0.03 ± 0.07, unbiased, but the error exceeds the pre-set 0.05 in every bin, so the data were not opened. Recomputed post hoc (20.11) with the M8 fit model of Sec. 5.13, extended to the three joint categories (the normalisation of the other-decay-mode template per category is taken from MC truth, which the M8 prescription does not define): +0.19 ± 0.12 / +0.05 ± 0.05 / −0.02 ± 0.05. The error falls by 13–25 %, as predicted from the nested pass/fail fit in Sec. 5.13, but the gate is still failed at 3–5 GeV (error 0.12) and at 5–8 GeV (error 0.050, deviation +0.05 to +0.09 in the three seeds against the allowed 0.04); at 8–30 GeV it is passed marginally (error 0.047 for seeds 1 and 2, 0.052 for seed 3). The verdict is unchanged: the D⁰ network has no calibration on the inclusive sample, and the D⁰ data were not opened for R. The gain in the fitted yield error, G_fit, for D⁰ is 1.54 / 1.29 / 1.04 on MC with M8 (1.50 / 1.25 / 1.05 with the old model); from single-category fits to data it is 1.27 / 0.99 / 1.04 in run 427929 and 1.11 / 1.35 / 1.05 in run 428770 (errors of 15–20 %; old model at 8–30 GeV in 428770, where the data reject the template), so the pre-set 1.15 is met in one bin of three per run. Scale caveat (uniform for Secs. 5.11, 5.12, 6.11 and 6.12; audit of 20.11, nothing refitted): these D⁰ fits to data used the template normalisation of 19.11, κ × 1.364, which is withdrawn (Sec. 5.13). From the stored fits the scale alone moves the WP70 yield of run 427929 by −0.5 % / +1.4 to +1.8 % / +2.6 to +3.1 % at 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV (run 428770: −1.0 % and within ±0.2 % at 3–5 and 5–8 GeV; the 8–30 GeV cell uses the old model without template), at most 0.3σ of the statistical error. Here the scale touches only the G_fit values from data (their errors are 15–20 %); the closure numbers above are from MC, where no scale was applied, so the gate verdict, including the marginal 8–30 GeV case, does not depend on it. <!-- src: charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §4; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1; charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md (nagłówek, §3) --> For D⁺ the closure passes from 5 GeV (3–5 GeV is blocked, closure error 0.054). At 5–8 / 8–30 GeV: R = 0.933 ± 0.043 / 0.940 ± 0.038 in run 427929 and 0.874 ± 0.036 / 0.892 ± 0.027 in run 428770 (one seed; in 428770 at 8–30 GeV R depends on the trigger class, 0.868 ± 0.019 for L1_J12-only against 1.030 ± 0.045 for the rest, 3.3σ). The gain from the peak fit, G_fit = [N_S/σ_N]_network / [N_S/σ_N]_tree, is 1.11 / 1.03 in run 427929 (pre-set threshold 1.15 not met) and 1.24 / 1.17 in run 428770 (met).
- (iii) **The sideband proxy overstates the gain.** For the same D⁺ cells the proxy on the far sidebands gives 1.36 / 1.26 (427929) and 1.77 / 1.58 (428770). In the minimum-bias run the gain realised in the yield error is 3–11 %; it is 17–24 % in the jet-triggered run, where R depends on the trigger. Gains quoted from sidebands elsewhere in this section are upper estimates in this sense.
- (iv) Pair features in the attention (Stage 5): significance proxy relative to the same network without them 1.007 ± 0.003 / 1.002 ± 0.012 / 1.030 ± 0.032 on MC; dropped. Cascade of a surroundings-only tagger after the cut on H: gain on the 427929 sidebands 1.22 / 1.38 / 1.31 for D⁰ and 1.12 / 1.39 / 2.04 for D⁺, against 2.03 / 1.51 / 1.00 and 1.50 / 1.35 / 1.23 from simply tightening the cut on H (Sec. 5.12); the pre-set criterion is met in one bin of three for each species; closed. Learning curve: the proxy with 100 % over 50 % of the training sample is 1.023 ± 0.003 / 1.028 ± 0.011 / 1.014 ± 0.024 on MC, i.e. 1–3 % per doubling (formally undecided by the pre-set criterion); pretraining and a common encoder are not pursued.
- (v) **State:** the tree H is the nominal classifier for both species. The variant without the track compatible with the soft pion in the network input has been read (Stage 6, below): it is not closed as a calibrated alternative. The 2–3 GeV part of Stage 4 was not available at the time of reading; it is now in Sec. 6.12 (last paragraph).
<!-- src: charm_work/DNET/RAPORT.md §14, §15; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus4/WERDYKT_etap4.md §1 (P1), §3 (D⁺: R, G_fit, far-sideband control rows), §6; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus5/WERDYKT_etap5.md §1–§4; definitions of R and G_fit: charm_work/DNET/KRYTERIA_etap4.md §1; D⁰ with M8 in item (ii) (20.11): charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §3, tabele/R_m8.md, tabele/gfit_m8.md -->

**Stage 6 (19–20.11): network without the soft-pion track; not closed as a calibrated alternative.** The transformer of Stage 2 was retrained (3 seeds) with one change: a surrounding track is removed from the input when its charge and its mass difference with the candidate, Δm < 0.155 GeV, are compatible with the soft pion of D*⁺→D⁰π⁺. The mask uses no truth information and is applied identically to data and MC; it removes 99.6 % of the visible soft pions and 2 % of the background tracks. Criteria and thresholds were written before the jobs were submitted (`DNET/KRYTERIA_etap6.md`); the reading is by a script that only compares with them.

| criterion | result | status |
|---|---|---|
| P1: the mask removes the soft-pion effect. Double ratio of the WP70 efficiencies on MC, D⁰ from D* over other D⁰, network over tree; required within 0.03 of one in all three pT bins | 1.014 ± 0.006 / 1.016 ± 0.008 / 1.017 ± 0.012 (3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV), all three seeds; without the mask 1.37 / 1.45 / 1.44 | met |
| S: D*-tag scale factor at WP70 determined for the three seeds, with a seed range of at most 0.04, in both runs at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV | seed range 0.049 in run 427929 at 5–8 GeV (1.06 / 1.11 / 1.08, each ± 0.13); the other three cells 0.006–0.021 (427929, 8–30 GeV: 0.89 ± 0.08; 428770: 0.83 ± 0.11 and 0.95 ± 0.04; tree: 0.97 ± 0.10, 0.88 ± 0.12, 0.97 ± 0.04) | not met |
| K2: background-mass sculpting not larger than for the tree | R_sculpt 0.96–0.98 ± 0.01, tree 0.94 ± 0.01 | met |

Reading. (i) With the mask the D* tag again calibrates the inclusive sample: the residual double ratio is 1.5 % (2.4σ from one at 3–5 GeV), against 37–45 % for the full network. (ii) The verdict "not closed" comes from one cell, in which the seed range exceeds the threshold by 0.009 while the statistical error of each scale factor is 0.13; the criterion is applied as written, and the test has little power to tell an unstable network from a fluctuation. (iii) Gain, **sideband proxy only**: 1.04 / 1.05 / 1.17 on the sidebands of run 427929, against 1.29 / 1.33 / 1.40 for the full network, so 13–42 % of the proxy gain is kept (run 428770: 1.25 / 1.40 / 1.42 against 1.56 / 1.76 / 1.61). The proxy overstates the gain (Stage 4: 1.26–1.36 became 1.03–1.11 in the peak fit for D⁺); with the proxy below 1.1 in two of three bins, the pre-set descriptive label is "no gain in minimum bias even in the proxy". **Real gain, from the fit of the inclusive D⁰ peak (20.11; one seed):** G_fit, the ratio of N_S/σ_N of the network to that of the tree at WP70, is 0.99 / 1.10 / 1.09 in run 427929, so the pre-set threshold (at least 1.15 in at least two of the three bins) is not met; in run 428770 it is 1.13 / 1.25 / 1.17 and the threshold is met. G_fit has no error (it is a ratio of fit errors of one fit), so differences of about 0.05 between bins are not significant. In run 428770 the gain sits in the L1_J12-only class (1.25 / 1.32 / 1.19, against 0.79 / 1.00 / 1.03 for the other events) and comes together with a yield ratio network over tree, after the MC efficiency, of R = 0.86 ± 0.09 / 0.86 ± 0.05 / 0.91 ± 0.03, i.e. 9–14 % below one, in line with the D*-tag scale factors of the table (0.83 and 0.95); in run 427929 R = 0.93 ± 0.12 / 0.98 ± 0.07 / 0.96 ± 0.06. The closure gate of R on simulation is failed in all three bins (R_fit − R_true = +0.19 / +0.04 / +0.07 with errors of 0.15 / 0.06 / 0.08, for lack of precision as in Stage 4), so R on data was obtained with the pre-declared override and is descriptive, not a calibration. The verdict is unchanged. (iv) The tree H stays the nominal classifier.
<!-- src: charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus6/WERDYKT_etap6.md §0–§6; charm_work/DNET/KRYTERIA_etap6.md §1, §3–§5; real gain: charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus6/ANAR_naprawa.md (Wynik), charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus6/dnet6_R.json -->

### 5.12 Working point of the tree: scan with the peak fit (WPSCAN, WPTIGHT, 19.11)

Post hoc with respect to Stages 4 and 5 of Sec. 5.11. Tree H, inclusive D⁰ and D⁺ without proton tag, both runs, signal efficiency ε from 0.10 to 0.95, the same peak-fit model at every point. The D⁰ numbers were recomputed on 20.11 with the model M8 of Sec. 5.13 (post hoc; with the template normalisation of 19.11, κ × 1.364, withdrawn: the scale alone moves the WP70 yields by −0.5 % / +1.4 to +1.8 % / +2.6 to +3.1 % at 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV in run 427929, see the scale caveat in Sec. 5.11 (ii) and Sec. 5.13; the error ratios below were not redone. The relative HESSE error of the final WP70 fit in run 427929 is 8.8 / 8.1 / 7.7 %, against the 8.2 % at 5–8 GeV used here, so the statement that the gain at 5–8 GeV disappears does not depend on the scale); the D⁺ numbers are unchanged. <!-- src: charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §4; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/out/final.json (hesse, N) -->

- **Tightening the cut reduces the statistical error of the yield at 3–5 GeV by 20–42 %** (D⁰ recomputed with the M8 fit model of Sec. 5.13 on 20.11; D⁺ unchanged): relative to WP70 the error is × 0.76 / 0.61 at ε = 0.50 / 0.35 for D⁰ in 427929 (0.68 / 0.52 with the old model), × 0.70 / 0.58 for D⁰ in 428770 (0.75 / 0.60), × 0.66 / 0.80 for D⁺ in 427929 and × 0.74 / 0.64 for D⁺ in 428770. The D⁰ factors changed by at most 1.4σ. **At 5–8 GeV the D⁰ gain does not survive the new model in the minimum-bias run:** × 1.10 ± 0.07 / 0.99 ± 0.08 in 427929 (old model 0.83 / 0.85; the new model improved WP70 from 11.3 % to 8.2 % and left the tight points where they were), × 0.79 / 0.88 in 428770 (0.77 / 0.70); for D⁺ the factors are 1.01 / 1.11 in 427929 (no gain) and 0.80 / 0.88 in 428770. At 8–30 GeV they are 0.91–1.34, so WP70 is at the optimum there or tightening hurts. These are ratios of HESSE errors; with the larger of HESSE and event bootstrap the D⁰ factors are 0.61 / 0.48 and 0.79 / 0.64 at 3–5 GeV and 1.10 / 0.99 and 0.94 / 0.88 at 5–8 GeV. In run 428770 at 8–30 GeV the data reject the M8 template at every working point between ε = 0.30 and 0.85 (p ≤ 5·10⁻⁴), so the old model is kept for that cell.
- **The gain exists only with a scale factor from an independent sample.** If the efficiency of the tight point relative to WP70 is measured on the same inclusive peak (pass/fail fit inside WP70), the corrected yield is algebraically the WP70 yield from a two-category fit: the ratio of the errors, joint fit over WP70, is 0.81–0.90 only for D⁺ in 427929 at 3–5 GeV and 0.87–0.91 for D⁺ in 428770 at 5–8 GeV, 0.90–1.08 in the other four D⁺ cells, and 0.92–1.09 for D⁰ with the M8 model (0.80–1.10 with the old model, where the low values came from the instability of the single-category fit at WP70). An independent sample exists for D⁰ above 5 GeV only (D* tag, not yet measured at ε = 0.4–0.5).
- **The pass/fail fit overestimates the signal in the loose category by 5–8 % on MC (a statement about the pass/fail ratio, established for D⁺).** It is not a statement about absolute yields: the absolute D⁰ yield at WP70 from the fit model used here was too low on MC, by 14–27 %, an effect of the opposite sign that is common to both categories and invisible in the ratio (Sec. 5.13). The scan shows N/ε_MC falling with tighter cuts (D⁺, average over the pT bins: −4.5 ± 2.8 % and −5.3 ± 2.5 % at ε = 0.50 in 427929 and 428770). This is not a dependence of the scale factor on the working point: the same pass/fail fit on MC with full background, where the truth is 1.00, gives 0.949 ± 0.023 / 0.935 ± 0.027 / 0.931 ± 0.028 at ε = 0.50 / 0.40 / 0.35 for D⁺ (average of the three pT bins), and the data give 0.949 ± 0.025 (427929) and 0.951 ± 0.016 (428770) at ε = 0.50. The double ratio data/MC is 1.00 ± 0.04. The apparent drift is a bias of the fit in the loose category, where S/B is small; a real change of the D⁺ scale factor between WP70 and ε = 0.35–0.50 is consistent with zero at the level of ±4–5 %. For D⁰, recomputed with M8, the drift of N/ε_MC averaged over the pT bins is +0.5 ± 4.7 % at ε = 0.50 and −6.8 ± 5.0 % at ε = 0.35 in 427929 (old model −5.1 ± 6.2 % and −12.5 ± 7.7 %), and −6.3 ± 4.0 % and −12.6 ± 3.2 % in 428770 (old model −11.1 ± 4.3 % and −9.0 ± 4.0 %; M8 at 3–8 GeV, old model at 8–30 GeV). The relative scale factor still does not close on MC with the required precision (error 0.05–0.17 with M8, 0.06–0.20 before), so no conclusion on a working-point dependence of the D⁰ scale factor is drawn.

The nominal working point stays WP70. This agrees with Sec. 6.8: the cut scan on the inclusive peak reaches a precision of 5 % but not that accuracy.
<!-- src: charm_work/WPSCAN_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (Wnioski 1, 3; T5, T6; "Korekta" at the end); charm_work/WPTIGHT_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (Odpowiedź 1–3, M1, D3, Rozstrzygnięcia, Rekomendacja); wording of the third item (19.11 night): charm_work/FITBIAS_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (Odpowiedź 1, §1) -->
