<!-- src (20.11, M8 recomputation of the D⁰ numbers): charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1; charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/tabele/wpscan_m8.md (W0–W3) -->

**Cross-check: simultaneous mass fit in classifier-score categories (post hoc).**
As an alternative to the single working point (WP70), the candidates of run 427929 were split into four disjoint categories of the BDT score, defined before any fit as intervals of the MC signal efficiency, [100, 70), [70, 50), [50, 30) and [30, 0) %, and the invariant-mass distributions were fitted simultaneously with a common signal shift and width scale, MC signal templates per category and free signal yields and backgrounds in each category. The sum of the four yields is the yield without any classifier requirement, so the classifier data-to-MC scale factor drops out of the cross section. The nominal fit models (M8 for D⁰, M0 for D⁺) were used unchanged.

The method does not reduce the total uncertainty. The loosest category holds 30 % of the signal and 94–99 % of all candidates; its yield is known to no better than 40 % (worse than 100 % in three of the six bins), and it dominates the summed yield. The relative uncertainty of the total yield is 30 / 60 / 26 % (stat.) for D⁰ and 46 / 25 / 25 % for D⁺ in the 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV bins, with background-model variations of 7–147 %, to be compared with 5–12 % for WP70. The closure on minimum-bias MC with full background is unbiased only within 19–44 %. The total cross-section uncertainty would increase from 29 / 20 / 23 % to 61 / 79 / 35 % (D⁰) and from 16 / 17 / 17 % to 161 / 51 / 40 % (D⁺). The resulting cross sections agree with the nominal ones within these uncertainties (ratios 1.37 / 0.56 / 0.96 and 0.97 / 0.90 / 0.75).

Constraining the cumulative signal fraction above WP70 to the MC value times the independently measured scale factor recovers the nominal measurement: the fit-plus-scale-factor part of the uncertainty improves by 4–10 %, which is invisible in the total (ratio of total uncertainties 0.97–1.02 in the favourable limit). A larger reduction (21–41 %), as suggested by an earlier pseudo-experiment study, is obtained only if the efficiencies at tighter working points are assumed to be independently known with the same precision; no such calibration exists for D⁺, and for D⁰ the D*-tag precision at WP50 in this run is 12–14 %. The fitted fractions agree with MC within 0.2–0.5 per category above 5 GeV; in the 3–5 GeV D⁰ bin the fit moves signal into the loosest category in data and in MC alike, i.e. it is a fit bias rather than a data–MC difference. The single-working-point measurement at WP70 is therefore retained.

A scan of single tighter working points (WP60–WP30) with the same models shows no working point with a smaller total uncertainty once an independent scale-factor uncertainty is included. In the 3–5 GeV D⁰ bin the uncertainty excluding the scale factor would decrease from 30 % to 15–17 % for WP ≤ 50, but the decrease comes entirely from the MC closure term (−22 ± 13 % at WP70 versus −5 ± 9 % at WP50), measured on a single MC realisation, and no independent scale factor is available at these working points; this is recorded as a conditional indication, not a result.
<!-- src: charm_work/CATFIT_2026-11-20/NOTA_tekst_EN.md; charm_work/CATFIT_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md; charm_work/CATFIT_2026-11-20/tabele/catfit.md -->

### 5.13 D⁰ fit model (FITBIAS, 19.11)

Post hoc with respect to Stages 4 and 5 of Sec. 5.11 and to Sec. 5.12. Tree H, inclusive D⁰ and D⁺ without proton tag, WP70, pT 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV. Criteria were written before the fits (in three parts: before all fits, after the diagnosis on MC and before the data fits, and a branching-fraction correction before the data fits, which was withdrawn on 20.11, see "Withdrawn" below). The old model ("M0": signal template from forced-decay MC with free shift and width scale, K↔π reflection with fixed fraction, exp(pol2) background on 1.75–2.00 GeV) is the one used everywhere else in this note.

- **The old D⁰ fit underestimated the yield at WP70 on MC.** On minimum-bias MC with its full background, b = N_fit/N_true − 1 = −26.5 ± 11.6 / −16.2 ± 7.3 / −13.6 ± 8.8 %. This is an absolute bias common to the pass and fail categories; it is a different effect, with the other sign, from the D⁺ pass/fail bias of Sec. 5.12.
- **Causes.** (a) The model had no component for true D mesons reconstructed in another decay mode under the peak (about 20 % of the signal in the window for D⁰): −5.4 / −6.0 / +3.2 % with a fixed signal shape (WP80). (b) The free width amplifies it: −9 / −16 / −6 % with a free shape, and the fitted width scale at WP70 is 0.94 / 0.82 / 0.92. Together they give −10 to −17 %; the rest is compatible with a fluctuation of the single MC background realisation. The K↔π reflection and the difference between the forced-decay signal template and the minimum-bias signal are not causes (reflection fraction 0.744 against 0.743).
- **New model M8 (nominal for D⁰ from 19.11, decision of 19.11):** a Gaussian constraint on the signal-shape parameters, a template for the other-decay-mode and charm-mismatch components, and the wider range 1.70–2.05 GeV. Among 10 settings × 2 modes (single-category and joint fit) it has the smallest figure of merit Q (0.126 against 0.184 for M0), a toy spread of the yield of 0.116 against 0.143, and a toy bias of −0.3 % against −3.9 %. On the MC realisation the closure at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV becomes −1.5 ± 8 % and −1 ± 10 %. With the template taken directly from the tree H (20.11, the nominal of Sec. 6.13) the closure on the minimum-bias MC at WP70 is −22.0 ± 9.7 / −4.6 ± 6.5 / +7.2 ± 7.8 % at 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV; these are the values behind the fit-bias row of the cross-section budget. **Two minima (20.11, found after the fits):** the M8 fit to data has two minima in the width scale of the signal, with yields of 1468 instead of 1536 at 5–8 GeV and 4245 instead of 4059 at 3–5 GeV and −2 ln L differing by 0.06 and 0.14 (one minimum at 8–30 GeV); the difference is carried as a systematic uncertainty of 4.4 % and 4.6 % (Sec. 6.13).
- **Departure from the criteria.** No setting met the pre-set coverage criterion (at least 10 of 12 cells with |⟨pull⟩| ≤ 0.25 and pull rms ≤ 1.25); this case was not foreseen, and the choice was made on Q (bias) alone, which is an extension of the criteria after the fact. **The HESSE error is too small by 20–30 %** also for M8 (pull rms 1.28; 68 % coverage becomes 58–62 %): the error has to be scaled by about 1.3 or taken from the bootstrap.
- **The 3–5 GeV bin stays unexplained:** −23 ± 10 % remains in every variant (the non-peaking background there has a dip of −22 ± 10 % of the signal relative to the fitted form; a 2σ fluctuation or a structure, not decidable with this MC sample). Update 20.11 (truth composition, below): the dip sits in the combinatorial background without charm, not in a charm category, and is read as a local 2.4σ fluctuation.
- **D⁺: no change in this study.** The best setting differs from M0 by less than 5 % in Q, and the simplicity rule keeps the old model. (State of 19.11. A dedicated study of 20.11 with the template taken directly from the tree H replaces M0 as the nominal D⁺ model: Sec. 5.14.)

D⁰ yields at WP70 in data (20.11, final: M8 with the template of the peaking components taken directly from the tree H and normalised to the signal as in the MC, scale 1; the error is the larger of the event bootstrap and 1.3 × HESSE in both runs; for run 428770 no bootstrap of the final fit exists and the error is 1.3 × HESSE (audit of 20.11: the toys behind the factor 1.3 cover both runs, pull rms 1.28 for M8 and 1.46 for M0, so for the M0 cell the factor is if anything too small); old model: errors as on 19.11):

| run | pT [GeV] | old model M0 | **M8 (nominal; final template)** | change | p(χ²), M8 | M8 as fitted on 19.11 (template scale 1.364, withdrawn) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 427929 | 3–5 | 4527 ± 970 | **4059 ± 468** | −10.3 % | 0.24 | 4105 ± 463 |
| 427929 | 5–8 | 1579 ± 178 | **1536 ± 161** | −2.7 % | 0.22 | 1530 ± 126 |
| 427929 | 8–30 | 764 ± 79 | **700 ± 70** | −8.4 % | 0.065 | 681 ± 55 |
| 428770 | 3–5 | 6425 ± 869 | **6322 ± 797** | −1.6 % | 0.22 | 6403 ± 639 |
| 428770 | 5–8 | 4548 ± 380 | **4829 ± 393** | +6.2 % | 0.71 | 4846 ± 302 |
| 428770 | 8–30 | **7716 ± 432 (M0 kept)** | (6987 ± 238 HESSE, rejected) | (−9.4 %) | 3·10⁻⁵ | (6829 ± 261, rejected, p = 1·10⁻⁷) |

All changes are within the old error (at most 0.8σ for the cells where M8 is used). In run 428770 at 8–30 GeV the data reject the template (p = 3·10⁻⁵ with the direct template, 1·10⁻⁷ with the withdrawn scale 1.364; M0: 0.43), so the old model stays there: the composition from minimum-bias MC does not carry over to the jet-triggered sample at high pT. The D⁺ yields of this study were unchanged; a dedicated study of 20.11 then fixed a new nominal D⁺ fit model with its own yields (Sec. 5.14).
<!-- src (final yields, 20.11): charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/tabele/final.md F5; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1; run 428770 and p-values: charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/tabele/m8.md (HESSE 613, 302, M0 332; × 1.3: charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §7; toys: charm_work/FITBIAS_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md); D⁺ study: charm_work/FITBIAS_Dplus_2026-11-20/KRYTERIA.md -->

**Calibration of D⁰ on the inclusive peak stays blocked.** The error of the relative scale factor on MC with M8 is 0.127 / 0.057 / 0.053 at ε = 0.50 (M0: 0.150 / 0.070 / 0.075), above the pre-set 0.05 in every bin; it is limited by the statistics of the MC background in the loose category, not by the signal shape. The D⁺ results of Sec. 5.12 are unchanged.

**What has been recomputed with M8 (20.11) and what still uses the old model.** Recomputed: the yields above and Sec. 6.13; the D⁰ rows of the working-point scan and of the pass/fail fit (Sec. 5.12: the gain from tightening survives at 3–5 GeV and disappears at 5–8 GeV in run 427929); the network-over-tree closure for D⁰ (Sec. 5.11, Stages 4 and 5: verdict unchanged); the D⁰ peak at 2–3 GeV (Sec. 6.12: yields unchanged within 0.8σ, without the template, which does not exist for that bin). Still with the fit models of their time: the tests of the MC background components (Sec. 6.6) and the D*-tag scale factors (Secs. 5.3, 5.6, 5.10 and 5.11, which are fits in Δm). The predicted tagged yields (Sec. 6.11) were recomputed from the M8 yields on 20.11. In run 428770 at 8–30 GeV the old model is kept everywhere, because the data reject the template at every working point from ε = 0.30 to 0.85. A cross-check of the template normalisation: the ratio of the other-decay-mode and charm-mismatch components to the signal from MC truth with the tree H itself at WP70 is 1.23 / 1.11 / 0.90, against 1.22 / 1.09 / 0.92 interpolated from the related classifier and used in M8. Scale caveat (uniform for Secs. 5.11, 5.12, 6.11 and 6.12; audit of 20.11, nothing refitted): these D⁰ fits to data used the template normalisation of 19.11, κ × 1.364, which is withdrawn (Sec. 5.13). From the stored fits the scale alone moves the WP70 yield of run 427929 by −0.5 % / +1.4 to +1.8 % / +2.6 to +3.1 % at 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV (run 428770: −1.0 % and within ±0.2 % at 3–5 and 5–8 GeV; the 8–30 GeV cell uses the old model without template), at most 0.3σ of the statistical error. Together with the change to the direct template the yields move by −1.3 to +2.8 % (table above). The quantities of Secs. 5.11, 5.12 and 6.12 and the fits by trigger region of Sec. 6.11 were not redone; no conclusion of those sections depends on a shift of this size (the affected quantities have errors of 5–70 %; the closure of Sec. 5.11 is on MC without the scale, and the nominal of Sec. 6.12 has no template). Sec. 6.13 uses the final template.
<!-- src (20.11): charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1–§4; charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/NOTA_teksty_EN.md (F); scale caveat: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1, charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/tabele/m8.md, charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §4 -->

**Truth composition of the MC background for the tree H (BKGCAT, 20.11; post hoc; the same minimum-bias MC realisation as above, so it localises effects and does not confirm them independently).** Signal window, WP70, in % of the background (signal excluded):

| component | D⁰ 3–5 GeV | D⁰ 5–8 | D⁰ 8–30 | D⁺ 3–5 | D⁺ 5–8 | D⁺ 8–30 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| combinatorial without charm | 83.4 | 60.4 | 38.0 | 51.9 | 19.9 | 7.6 |
| partially combinatorial | 10.4 | 17.1 | 19.6 | 41.3 | 64.5 | 64.4 |
| K↔π reflection | 2.4 | 10.8 | 21.4 | – | – | – |
| other decay mode of the same D | 1.3 | 5.3 | 11.0 | 0.4 | 1.6 | 2.7 |
| charm mismatch (another charm hadron) | 0.5 | 1.8 | 2.8 | 1.5 | 6.5 | 14.2 |
| combinatorial with tracks from b | 2.0 | 4.6 | 7.2 | 4.9 | 7.5 | 11.0 |
| background / signal in the window | 14.8 | 3.2 | 1.5 | 5.7 | 1.4 | 0.7 |

- **The D⁰ dip at 3–5 GeV is a fluctuation of the non-charm combinatorial background.** An Asimov signal was added to the histogram of one component at a time and the spurious signal of the nominal fit was read in % of the true signal (error from 60 Poisson replicas). All non-signal, non-reflection components together give −22.1 ± 9.1 % (2.4σ; the closure of M8 with the template is −22.0 ± 9.7 %). Of this, the combinatorial background without charm carries −16.7 ± 10.9 %, the partially combinatorial one −1.3 ± 3.5 % and the other-decay-mode plus charm-mismatch components −3.1 ± 1.4 %. The sign is not constant over disjoint score intervals (p ≈ 0.012), the pattern is absent at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV (+0.4 ± 6.9 % and −11.8 ± 6.7 %), and of 86 tests of non-peaking components 4 have |Z| > 2 (3.9 expected). Reading: a local 2.4σ fluctuation with no physical mechanism, not the structure of a charm category. Caveat: the change of sign with the tightness of the cut could also be a sculpting of the mass by the score; this cannot be decided on one MC realisation and needs an independent sample.
- **Real structures.** The other-decay-mode component of D⁰ has a negative spurious signal, −4.6 ± 1.0 % averaged over the pT bins (4.8σ): K⁻K⁺ and π⁺π⁻ raise the sidebands and K⁻π⁺π⁰ feeds the window; this is what the peaking template of M8 describes. For D⁺ the charm-mismatch component (mostly D_s) peaks (+1.4 ± 0.6 % at WP70, +2.5 ± 0.6 % at WP90), and the partially combinatorial component has an excess of the same sign in all pT bins (+4.0 ± 1.6 % at WP70, 2.5σ); the latter is an indication, not an established effect, and it is not part of any peaking template.
- **Withdrawn: the branching-fraction scale of the peaking template.** In the M8 fits to data of 19.11 the normalisation κ of the peaking template relative to the signal was multiplied by the ratio of the signal branching fraction "in the MC" to the PDG value. That ratio was an artefact of the truth decay flag, which is topological (K∓π±[π±] and no other charged pion, charged kaon or π⁰) and therefore includes decays with an unseen K⁰, η or other neutral particles (fractions in Sec. 6.13). The exclusive branching fractions of the signal modes in the MC agree with the PDG: in the fiducial region (pT ≥ 3 GeV, |η| < 2.2; radiative decays included, doubly Cabibbo-suppressed mode not included) they are 3.955 ± 0.025 % for K⁻π⁺ against 3.936 % and 9.336 ± 0.053 % for K⁻π⁺π⁺ against 9.31 %, i.e. MC/PDG = 1.005 ± 0.006 and 1.003 ± 0.006 (MC statistics; the PDG errors are 0.8 % and 1.5 %). This is the one definition used in this note. Over |η| < 2.5 and all pT the same counting gives 3.974 % and 9.404 % (ratio 1.010, a 1.0σ and 1.6σ statistical difference from the fiducial subset), and dividing the D⁰ value by the PDG fraction with the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed mode (3.951 %) gives 1.001; both variants appeared in earlier versions. The correct scale is 1. With κ and the template shape taken directly from the tree H instead of the related classifier (κ at WP70: 1.231 ± 0.018 / 1.108 ± 0.024 / 0.903 ± 0.033 against the interpolated 1.215 / 1.089 / 0.922, i.e. the interpolation was correct within 1σ) and with the scale removed, the D⁰ yields at WP70 change by −1.1 / +0.4 / +2.8 % in run 427929 and by −1.3 / −0.3 % at 3–5 / 5–8 GeV in run 428770, all 0.1–0.7σ of the statistical error. Run 428770 at 8–30 GeV still rejects the model (p = 3·10⁻⁵ against 1·10⁻⁷), so the direct template does not repair that cell. These are the final yields of the table above and of Sec. 6.13. Reweighting the whole MC decay table to the PDG changes κ by −1.3 to −1.8 % and the yields in data by at most 0.3 %; the largest deviations MC/PDG are in modes that do not matter for the template (D⁺→K⁺K⁻π⁺π⁰ 4.53, D⁺→π⁺π⁺π⁻ 1.47, D⁰→K⁺K⁻π⁰ 1.41). D_s and Λ_c are not stored in the truth record of this production, so their decay tables cannot be checked.
<!-- src: charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §2 (composition), §3 (spurious signal per component), §4 (direct template, yields), §5 (decay table; topological decayType); charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/tabele/{sklad,spur,m8,br}.md; exclusive BR in the fiducial region: charm_work/CVER_2026-11-20/out/cver.json and BKGCAT tabele/br.md (control column), ratios: charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §1 -->

**Data-driven K↔π reflection normalisation from twin candidates (KONSTR, 20.11; post hoc; negative result).** Each D⁰ candidate row carries the mass of its K↔π-swapped twin. A reflection row is the wrong-assignment row of a true D⁰, so its twin mass peaks at the D⁰ mass irrespective of the row's own mass. Counting this peak in slices of the candidate mass outside the region where the signal and reflection bands cross (|m − m_D⁰| > 50 MeV) measures the number of reflection rows directly from data. A linear sideband subtraction of the twin-mass peak sculpts (in simulation, background alone fakes 2725 ± 424 reflections at 3–5 GeV); a per-slice fit closes only at 8–30 GeV (0.89 ± 0.14 of the true number) and is unusable at 3–5 GeV. In data at 8–30 GeV the reflection-to-signal ratio relative to simulation is 1.14 ± 0.09 (run 428770, WP70), 1.02 ± 0.05 (WP50) and 1.16 ± 0.18 (run 427929), i.e. consistent with the simulated fraction and not more precise than the D*-tagged determination (0.957 ± 0.041). The simulated reflection fraction is kept.

**Origin of the M8 rejection in run 428770 at 8–30 GeV (diagnostic outside the pre-set criteria).** Replacing the simulated reflection fraction by the measured one leaves the fit unchanged (N = 6812 ± 235 against 6829 ± 236, p = 1.1·10⁻⁷ in both). Removing the peaking template of the other-decay-mode and charm-mismatch components from M8 restores an acceptable fit (N = 7504 ± 235, p = 0.27), whereas a free signal shape or a higher background order do not (p ~ 10⁻⁷). The rejection is therefore caused by the simulated peaking template (shape and normalisation from minimum-bias MC, applied to a jet-triggered sample), not by the reflection, the shape constraint or the fit range. The yields of the table above are not changed by this.

| model (428770, 8–30 GeV, WP70) | N | σ (HESSE) | χ²/ndf | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M0 | 7716 | 332 | 44.9/44 | 0.43 |
| M8 (template scale of 19.11) | 6829 | 236 | 140.7/64 | 1.1·10⁻⁷ |
| M8, reflection normalised to data | 6812 | 235 | 140.6/64 | 1.1·10⁻⁷ |
| M8 without the peaking template | 7504 | 235 | 70.5/64 | 0.27 |
<!-- src: charm_work/KONSTR_2026-11-20/NOTA_tekst_EN.md (part for Sec. 5); charm_work/KONSTR_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §A; charm_work/KONSTR_2026-11-20/tabele/{A_mc,A_mc2,A_dane_428770,A_dane_427929_pomiar}.md -->

Caveats: 20 variants were judged on one MC realisation of about the size of run 427929, so the closure has an error of 5–12 % and does not separate the variants; the ranking rests on Asimov fits and toys, and the toys draw the background from the family of the model. The template shape and normalisation come from the same MC sample on which the closure is computed, with a related classifier.
<!-- src: charm_work/FITBIAS_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (Odpowiedź 1–5, §1, §2, §3, Zastrzeżenia); charm_work/FITBIAS_2026-11-19/tabele/data.md, select.md -->

### 5.14 D⁺ fit model (FITBIAS_Dplus, 20.11)

**Status: post hoc; the model is chosen by ranking, because no setting meets the closure criterion.** Tree H, inclusive D⁺ without proton tag, WP70. The criteria were written before any fit of this study and the ranking was fixed before the signal region of the data was fitted with the candidate models; the yields of the previous model, its fit probability at 3–5 GeV and its closure were known beforehand.

**Model (A2, nominal for D⁺ from 20.11).** Binned likelihood fit to the K⁻π⁺π⁺ mass in 1.70–2.20 GeV (5 MeV bins). The signal is the simulated line shape with a common mass shift and a width scale, both constrained by Gaussian terms (0 ± 0.5 MeV and 1.00 ± 0.10). A second component, tied to the signal normalisation, describes charm decays in which all three tracks come from one charm hadron but the decay is not D⁺→K⁻π⁺π⁺: other D⁺ modes (K⁻K⁺π⁺, π⁺π⁺π⁻(π⁰), K⁻π⁺π⁺π⁰) and other hadrons (mainly D_s⁺→K⁺K⁻π⁺ and π⁺π⁺π⁻ with a wrong mass hypothesis, D⁰ and D*→K3π). Its shape and its ratio to the signal (0.51–0.53) are taken from the minimum-bias MC with the same classifier requirement, directly from the tree H. The remaining background (partially reconstructed charm and combinatorial candidates) is the exponential of a third-order Chebyshev polynomial. This is the D⁺ counterpart of M8 without the reflection. It was chosen among nine predefined settings (free or constrained signal shape, with and without tied templates, two other mass ranges, two other polynomial orders) using simulation and pseudo-experiments built from the data sidebands.

**Closure on simulation: the criterion is not met by any setting.** With the full background the fitted yield of A2 exceeds the true one by +4.6 ± 7.7 %, +9.2 ± 6.2 % and −0.9 ± 7.3 % at 3–5, 5–8 and 8–30 GeV (previous model: +4.8 ± 8.0 / +11.4 ± 6.4 / −0.8 ± 7.2 %). The pre-set requirement (|b| ≤ 5 % or within one standard deviation) fails at 5–8 GeV for all nine settings (+9 to +16 % for the eight without a template for partially reconstructed charm). For this foreseen case the choice was made on the figure of merit Q (bias of Asimov fits out of sample and spread of the pseudo-experiments): 0.074 for A2 against 0.090 for the previous model. The excess is attributed mostly to partially reconstructed charm (+4.2 ± 1.8 % averaged over pT, with no single parent hadron responsible), which a simulated sample of the size of the data can neither establish nor exclude as a real structure; a template for it cannot be used, because its statistical noise biases the yield by −14 to −38 % on an independent half of the sample. The yield is therefore not corrected, and |b| ⊕ σ_b = 9.0 / 11.1 / 7.3 % is carried as a separate uncertainty, in the same way as for D⁰ (Sec. 5.13). It is a bound from one MC realisation, not a measured bias. Pseudo-experiments at the purity of the data show that the HESSE error underestimates the spread of the yield by a factor 1.3; the statistical error is the larger of 1.3 × HESSE and the event bootstrap, as for D⁰.

D⁺ yields at WP70 in data (statistical and model uncertainty; the model uncertainty is the largest difference to the previous model and to the setting with a free signal shape and the tied template):

| run | pT [GeV] | previous model (M0) | **A2 (nominal): N ± stat ± model** | change | p(χ²) | closure on MC, \|b\| ⊕ σ_b |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 427929 | 3–5 | 3547 ± 276 | **3404 ± 292 ± 142** | −4.0 % | 0.034 | 9.0 % |
| 427929 | 5–8 | 1651 ± 76 | **1630 ± 102 ± 21** | −1.3 % | 0.89 | 11.1 % |
| 427929 | 8–30 | 687 ± 59 | **651 ± 53 ± 36** | −5.2 % | 0.23 | 7.3 % |
| 428770 | 3–5 | 4118 ± 315 | **4098 ± 424 ± 20** | −0.5 % | 0.85 | 9.0 % |
| 428770 | 5–8 | 3451 ± 199 | **3313 ± 206 ± 139** | −4.0 % | 0.15 | 11.1 % |
| 428770 | 8–30 | 6458 ± 216 | **6508 ± 254 ± 91** (fourth-order polynomial) | +0.8 % | 0.016 | 7.6 % |

The relative model uncertainty in run 427929 is 4.2 / 1.3 / 5.5 %. All changes are within the statistical error. The cross-sections and the D⁺/D⁰ ratio of Sec. 6.13 use these yields.

- **Low fit probability at 3–5 GeV in run 427929 (0.034).** It does not originate in the signal region (χ² = 20 for 18 bins) but in the sidebands: a 3.1σ deficit at 1.800–1.825 GeV, just below the peak, and scattered 2–2.7σ deviations above 2.0 GeV. A higher polynomial order or the tied template do not remove it; it is absent in run 428770 (p = 0.85 with three times more candidates) and in the simulation, and is treated as a fluctuation. It makes the fitted width scale large (1.11 ± 0.04 with the constraint, 1.16 ± 0.07 without), and the yield in this bin depends on the width by up to 13 %.
- **No D*⁺→D⁰π⁺ peak at 2.010 GeV; an unexplained local deficit instead.** Three of the six run and pT cells prefer a local deficit there at 2.4–3.2σ, which is not present in the simulation and is not understood. Its effect on the yield is below 2.8 % and inside the model uncertainty. A hypothesis, **not tested**: the deficit may be a trace of the D* veto on the D⁺ candidates (a candidate is rejected when min(m − m_Kπ) < 160 MeV; the veto is part of our single-D⁺ selection and is applied by the same code to data and simulation) acting differently in data and in simulation; whatever its origin, the effect of at most 2.8 % is already in the budget.
- **Run 428770 at 8–30 GeV rejects A2** (p = 0.004); the same model with a fourth-order polynomial is used in that cell (p = 0.016). As for D⁰, the composition taken from minimum-bias MC carries over poorly to the jet-triggered sample at high pT.

Caveats: one MC realisation of about the size of run 427929, so the closure has an error of 6–8 % and does not separate eight of the nine settings; the advantage of A2 in Q is 18 % over the previous model. The tied template and its normalisation come from the same MC sample as the closure; the template noise at full statistics is estimated at −0.4 to −2.5 % of the yield, inside the model uncertainty. The pseudo-experiments draw the background from the family of the model. No independent measurement of the D⁺ width scale in data exists. Not established: whether the excess from partially reconstructed charm is a structure (an MC sample at least four times larger is needed), and the origin of the deficit at 2.01 GeV.

## 6 Recommendations for the AFP single-D analysis

### 6.1 Scope and summary

Caveat: the numbers in Secs. 3–5 use our selection (h5 v1.1/our v1.2 conversion, run 428770, XGB H, no AFP tag, pT 3–30 GeV; exceptions: Secs. 6.3 and 6.11 use the proton tag, Sec. 6.12 pT 2–3 GeV); results that name run 427929 or a trigger class
(parts of Secs. 5.2 and 5.6–5.13, Secs. 6.2–6.13) use the v2.0 files of runs 428770 and 427929 (Sec. 2). The AFP analysis uses production
v2.1, CharmNet (MLP + DisCo trained on MC), the `tag` region and pT 2–30 GeV. **Methods and code transfer; numbers must
be recomputed on that selection.** Naming of "v1.2" must be aligned between the two analyses.

Caveat (19.11): the soft pion of the D* is inside the cone; on MC the tagged SF carries over to inclusive D⁰ in 427929 (within 0.5 %), in 428770 the transfer is model dependent, D⁺ not tested | SF(ptcone40 < 2 GeV), pT 3–30: 0.98 ± 0.04 in 427929 (MBTS); 0.51 ± 0.02 in 428770 (81 % L1_J12-only pairs); no MC reweighting without the cone tracks closes 428770 per pT bin (event-level: 0.76 ± 0.04 for L1_J12-only; data-signal weights: 0.90 ± 0.09 pT-inclusive, Sec. 5.8) | medium |
| 9 | Accidental AFP tag (background from unrelated protons) | proton mixing with a ξ_cal-vetoed donor pool, second estimators and half their difference as systematic (Sec. 6.3); **preliminary**, non-AFP triggers only | f_acc = 0.0087 ± 0.0044 (427929), 0.048 ± 0.014 (428770); covers 4.4 % of the tags in 428770 | medium |
Reference numbers in rows 1–7 are for run 428770 (our h5 conversion) unless a run is named; rows 8 and 9 name the run. The items added on 18.11 (Secs. 6.7–6.12: vertex veto, low-pT calibration, external inputs, L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm, tagged region and luminosity, 2–3 GeV bin) and on 19.11 (Sec. 6.13: inclusive cross-section) are not in this table; of the results of 20.11 only the absolute scale factor (row 2, Sec. 6.8) is.

Results of the inclusive measurement without proton tag (run 427929, |η(D)| < 2.2, particle + antiparticle, prompt and non-prompt; preliminary and post hoc; details, budget and comparisons in Sec. 6.13):

| quantity | 3–5 GeV | 5–8 GeV | 8–30 GeV | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| σ(D⁰) [µb] ± stat ± syst ± lumi | 1028 ± 119 ± 274 ± 23 | **278 ± 29 ± 49 ± 6** | **100 ± 10 ± 21 ± 2** | SF from the D* tag above 5 GeV (its error includes the spread of the Δm estimators, Sec. 6.8); at 3–5 GeV transferred SF and an uncorrected fit bias of −22 ± 10 % on MC |
| σ(D⁺) [µb] | **515 ± 44 ± 82 ± 11** | **165 ± 10 ± 25 ± 4** | **49.2 ± 4.0 ± 6.9 ± 1.1** | measurement with the SF from data (Sec. 6.8, conditional on the transfer; ± 0.08 at 3–5 GeV), not a scenario; fit model of Sec. 5.14, whose closure criterion on MC is not met at 5–8 GeV (carried as an uncertainty) |
| σ(D*±) [µb] | – | 134 ± 18 ± 23 ± 3 | 60.6 ± 7.1 ± 6.8 ± 1.3 | Δm fit without the classifier |
| D⁺/D⁰ | 0.50 ± 0.07 ± 0.15 | 0.59 ± 0.07 ± 0.12 | 0.49 ± 0.06 ± 0.12 | within 1.4σ of Pythia, ALICE, LHCb, e⁺e⁻; does not separate 0.40 from 0.47 |
| D*⁺/D⁰ | – | 0.48 ± 0.08 ± 0.11 | 0.61 ± 0.09 ± 0.13 | MC truth 0.42 / 0.44 |
| D⁰ data / CMS 13 TeV (definitions matched) | – | 0.53 ± 0.13 (3.0σ below) | 0.75 ± 0.20 | tension, not explained |
| D⁺ data / CMS 13 TeV | – | 0.87 ± 0.21 | 0.81 ± 0.18 | |
| D⁰ data / Pythia 8 A3 | 0.84 ± 0.24 | 0.58 ± 0.12 | 0.63 ± 0.15 | about 0.6 above 5 GeV, also for D⁺ (0.65, 0.58); D*±: 0.67 ± 0.15, 0.87 ± 0.14 |

### 6.2 Isolation

Summary: the isolation efficiency of true D⁰ is described by the MC in minimum-bias data (run 427929) and is not
described in the jet-trigger-dominated run 428770. The difference follows the trigger class, not the run or pile-up, and
no MC reweighting that we tried without the tracks of the cone itself removes it in every pT bin (Sec. 5.8). [Fig. F14](figures/F14_iso_sf_trigger.pdf) shows the SF per trigger class,
run and pT bin.
<!-- src: summary of 6.2.1–6.2.4; F14 input: B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/btrig_iso.json (points in figures/F14_points.json) -->

**Caveat on the D* tag (19.11).** Every scale factor in Sec. 6.2, for the isolation cuts and for the CharmNet clone (Sec. 6.2.4c), is measured on D⁰ tagged by a D*. Our track-set network separates D⁰ from D* from all other D⁰ through the soft pion among the surrounding tracks (ε at WP70 0.87–0.89 against 0.60–0.63 on MC; Sec. 5.11, Stages 4 and 5), so for that network the D*-tag scale factor does not calibrate the inclusive sample. The same test (efficiency for D⁰ from D* against other D⁰, and with the soft pion removed from the cone) has not been made for the isolation cuts or for the clone with ptcone inputs. Until it is, the numbers of Secs. 6.2.1–6.2.4d are scale factors for D⁰ from D*; their transfer to inclusive D⁰, and to D⁺, is an assumption. No number in Sec. 6.2 is changed.
<!-- src: charm_work/DNET/RAPORT.md §15 pkt 1; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus4/WERDYKT_etap4.md §1 (P1), §6 -->

**Soft pion in the isolation cone, quantified on MC (19.11).** The ptcone branches exclude only the D⁰ daughters, so the soft pion of the D* lies inside the R = 0.4 cone for 95–100 % of the tagged D⁰ and contributes a median of 0.31 / 0.47 / 0.80 GeV (pT 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV). On MC it lowers ε(ptcone40 < 2 GeV) of the tagged sample by 5 / 10 / 24 %; because tagged D⁰ are otherwise more isolated than the rest, the tagged efficiency differs from the inclusive one by +0.6 / −1.6 / −9.7 % (forced-decay MC), and by less than 1 % for ptcone40/pT < 0.5. For the scale factor the pion cancels to first order: shifting the non-pion activity in MC until the tagged SF equals 0.98 changes the inclusive SF by less than 0.5 %, so the 427929 scale factors apply to inclusive D⁰. For 428770 the transfer is model dependent (−2 to +8 % at SF = 0.76, −10 to +27 % at SF = 0.51, scale versus pedestal model; an estimate of size, not a measurement), so those numbers remain scale factors for D⁰ from D*; the conclusion that the MC does not describe isolation in that run is unaffected. D⁺ was not tested.
<!-- src: charm_work/ISO_pis_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md -->

#### 6.2.1 Result on run 428770 (ISO_sf, full statistics, 17.11)

D*-tagged D⁰ in data 428770, all 39 files of the h5 v1.2 D*-tag sample (S = 4152 ± 196 with score_H > 0.6). ε in data
comes from a simultaneous Δm pass/fail fit and ε in MC from truth. "rw" means that the MC is reweighted to the
signal multiplicity in data, with the candidate-based proxy n_trkid.

| requirement | pT 3–30 | 3–30 rw | 3–5 rw | 5–8 rw | 8–30 rw |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ptcone40 < 2 GeV | 0.51 ± 0.02 | 0.64 ± 0.03 | 0.89 ± 0.07 | 0.76 ± 0.05 | 0.50 ± 0.03 |
| ptcone40/pT < 0.5 | 0.73 ± 0.02 | 0.86 ± 0.03 | 0.85 ± 0.07 | 0.92 ± 0.05 | 0.81 ± 0.02 |
| ptcone40/pT < 1.0 | 0.88 ± 0.02 | 0.95 ± 0.02 | 0.96 ± 0.06 | 0.92 ± 0.04 | 0.94 ± 0.02 |
| ptcone100 < 8 GeV | 0.41 ± 0.02 | 0.61 ± 0.03 | 0.69 ± 0.07 | 0.71 ± 0.05 | 0.61 ± 0.03 |
| ptcone100/pT < 1.0 | 0.66 ± 0.03 | 1.03 ± 0.04 | 0.68 ± 0.09 | 0.64 ± 0.06 | 0.98 ± 0.03 |

The earlier 2-file result (0.60 ± 0.10 for ptcone40 < 2 GeV) is superseded: the error is 5× smaller and the deficit
is significant. Multiplicity reweighting recovers only part of it. Absolute cuts stay at SF 0.50–0.89 and the loose
relative ptcone40 cuts at 0.81–0.96. The pT-inclusive closure of relative ptcone100 (1.03 ± 0.04) does not hold in the
3–5 and 5–8 GeV bins (0.68, 0.64). In this run an MC-derived isolation efficiency is therefore too high for all the
cuts listed (SF 0.41–0.88 without reweighting).
<!-- src: charm_work/ISO_sf/runs/lxplus/out/sf_table.md (17.11; A_score0.6, columns SF and SF rw(n_trkid), all pT blocks);
     39 files and the 2-file sample: ISO_sf/RAPORT.md (Krótko); 2-file value: ISO_sf/out/sf_table.md.
     v1 → v2: the third row had empty pT-bin cells (now filled from the same table); "overestimates ε_S by 10–50 %" replaced
     by the SF range, because SF = 0.41 corresponds to more than 50 %. -->

#### 6.2.2 Trigger dependence (B_trig, 17.11)

The same measurement was repeated on the v2.0 files per trigger class of run 428770 and on the minimum-bias run 427929
(MBTS, μ ≈ 0.005). SF for pT 3–30 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV:

| requirement | 428770 all | 428770 L1_J12 only* | 428770 MBTS-based* | 427929 (MBTS) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ptcone40 < 2 GeV | 0.51 ± 0.02 / 0.58 ± 0.05 / 0.37 ± 0.03 | 0.80 ± 0.05 / 0.77 ± 0.08 / 0.49 ± 0.04 | 0.76 ± 0.08 / 0.64 ± 0.10 / 0.80 ± 0.10 | **0.98 ± 0.04 / 1.06 ± 0.06 / 0.88 ± 0.10** |
| ptcone40 < 4 GeV | 0.64 ± 0.02 / 0.81 ± 0.05 / 0.50 ± 0.02 | 0.85 ± 0.04 / 0.98 ± 0.07 / 0.60 ± 0.03 | 0.90 ± 0.06 / 0.77 ± 0.08 / 0.83 ± 0.07 | **0.98 ± 0.02 / 0.99 ± 0.03 / 0.90 ± 0.07** |
| ptcone40/pT < 0.5 | 0.74 ± 0.03 / 0.77 ± 0.05 / 0.72 ± 0.02 | 0.83 ± 0.03 / 0.94 ± 0.07 / 0.76 ± 0.03 | 0.84 ± 0.07 / 0.78 ± 0.09 / 0.86 ± 0.06 | **0.95 ± 0.04 / 0.98 ± 0.04 / 0.96 ± 0.05** |

*The MC efficiency for the 428770 classes uses trigger-emulation weights ε(n_sel^BL, leading-track pT) derived from
427929. These weights do **not** close on 428770 (χ²/ndf ≈ 1.5·10⁴), so the two starred columns are model dependent.
The 427929 column and the "428770 all" column use no emulation.

In minimum-bias data the isolation efficiency of true D⁰ agrees with the MC: SF = 0.95–0.98 ± 0.02–0.04 for pT 3–30 GeV
in the three cuts above, and 0.88–0.96 ± 0.05–0.10 in 8–30 GeV. Control with other estimators (20.11, ptcone40 < 2 GeV, pT 3–30 GeV): the pass/fail fit, the same fit with an added exponential factor in the background and the sideband subtraction with a threshold fit agree (ε in data 0.649–0.655), while sideband counting gives SF = 1.05–1.12 against 0.98 ± 0.04. The difference comes from the 3–5 GeV bin (S/B = 0.57; the 5–8 and 8–30 GeV bins are stable) and grows with the width of the counting window; it is a spread of the background model that the ±0.04 does not contain, and which estimator is biased was not established (per bin and in relation to the estimator dependence of the classifier scale factor: Sec. 6.8, "Estimator dependence"). For 428770 four estimators give 0.49–0.51. In 428770 it does not agree in any class. Of the 15
cut × pT sets tested, 12 have a class-to-class pull above 2σ (maximum 4.1σ). The "MBTS-based" class of 428770 is 2.5σ
below 427929 for ptcone40 < 2 GeV; it is not a minimum-bias class: 93 % of it is HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 and the rest passed another chain of the skim set (Secs. 2, 6.11). The 428770
values of the two chains (ISO_sf on h5 v1.2, B_trig on v2.0) agree within 0.01 for pT 3–30 GeV (5–8 GeV: 0.61 ± 0.04 against 0.58 ± 0.05).
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/RAPORT_DANE.md (table "SF izolacji per klasa", gates G5, G8), btrig_iso.md;
     93 % HLT_j20 and no minimum-bias class: B_trig/ISO_origin/RAPORT.md §0 pkt 2. The 2.5σ is (0.98 − 0.76)/√(0.04² + 0.08²).
     control with other estimators (20.11): charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §3, §8 pkt 3. -->

#### 6.2.3 Origin of the deficit (ISO_origin, 17.11)

The deficit follows the jet triggers, not pile-up and not the run.

- **Same trigger, same isolation in both runs.** For ε(ptcone40 < 2 GeV) in events with HLT_j20 and without L1_J12 the
  signal gives 0.38 ± 0.05 (427929) vs 0.40 ± 0.04 (428770) and the background 0.3088 ± 0.0027 vs 0.3083 ± 0.0021. The
  L1_J12 background gives 0.160 ± 0.005 vs 0.158 ± 0.001. Run 427929 split by the j20 bit gives SF = 0.58 ± 0.08 (fired)
  and 1.13 ± 0.04 (neither j20 nor J12 fired), so the deficit is reproduced inside the good run. HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 belongs to the skim set of 428770, so its sample is complete there and the comparison is like for like (inclusive ⟨n_sel^BL⟩ 34.8 against 35.1, median leading-track pT 4.26 against 4.37 GeV in 428770 and 427929).
- **Pile-up is excluded.** The isolated background fraction is flat in μ in both runs, and events with an additional
  vertex change the result by less than 0.5 %.
- **No single variable is universal.** ε_iso as a function of n_sel^BL differs between 427929 and the L1_J12-only class
  (χ²/ndf = 204/5), and likewise for the leading-track pT (23/5) and pT(D⁰) (88/6). In 427929 the data agree with the
  MC in each of these variables.
- **Reweighting does not close 428770.** Reweighting the MC in pT × leading-track pT × n_sel^BL to the signal in data
  closes 427929 (0.985 ± 0.034) but leaves the L1_J12-only class at 0.76 ± 0.04, falling with pT: 0.97 ± 0.08 /
  0.72 ± 0.06 / 0.50 ± 0.05 in 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV. The emulation weights of Sec. 6.2.2 leave the same residual
  (0.80 ± 0.05), so the residual is not an artefact of one weighting model.

The hypothesis left by this study was that the jet trigger selects D⁰ inside a jet much harder than the D⁰ itself; it
is tested in Sec. 6.2.4.
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/ISO_origin/RAPORT.md §0 (pkt 1, 3–6), §4a, §4b (tables), out/tables.md (17.11).
     v1 → v2: "0.97 / 0.72 / 0.50 ± 0.05" corrected to the per-bin errors 0.08 / 0.06 / 0.05 (§4b table T8). -->

#### 6.2.4 Track-jet test (TRKJET, 17.11)

Anti-kT R = 0.4 track jets (own implementation; tracks with pT > 0.5 GeV from the `Tracks` tree of the v2.0 files) were
built for every D⁰←D* pair. In L1_J12-only events the D⁰ is much more often inside a hard jet. A jet with pT > 10 GeV
contains the D⁰ in 0.61 ± 0.02 of the signal, against 0.16 ± 0.02 in 427929 and 0.18 in MC. A jet with pT > 5 GeV built
only from tracks outside the isolation cone lies within ΔR < 0.8 in 0.17 ± 0.02 of the signal, against 0.06 ± 0.01 (5.4σ).

This does **not** explain the deficit, for three reasons.

1. For D⁰ with no such nearby jet the SF is still 0.47 ± 0.03 in L1_J12-only events (1.00 ± 0.04 in 427929).
2. ε_iso in data is not a common function of the distance to the nearby jet (χ²/ndf = 157/8 between 427929 and
   L1_J12-only) or of its pT (190/4).
3. Reweighting the MC in jet variables that do not use cone tracks closes 427929 (0.99 ± 0.03) but leaves L1_J12-only
   at 0.70 ± 0.04, worse than the event-level reweighting of Sec. 6.2.3. Even a reweighting in the pT of the jet
   containing the D⁰, which uses the cone tracks themselves, reaches only 0.86 ± 0.04.

The jet-triggered sample therefore differs from the minimum-bias MC in the content of the cone itself at fixed jet
topology. No reweighting in event-level or track-jet variables that we tried makes the MC usable for isolation in
428770. Caveats: track jets contain charged particles only and are not the calorimeter jets of the trigger, and the MC
has few D⁰ near hard jets. ([Fig. F16](figures/F16_trkjet.pdf).)
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/TRKJET/RAPORT.md §0 (K1–K4), §3–§6, §7; out/trkjet.json (F16 input; points in figures/F16_points.json) -->

#### 6.2.4a Perpendicular cones, away-side-jet topology, neutral-energy proxy (TRKJET2, 17.11)
Three tests taken from the literature, criteria fixed before the full extraction. (1) Perpendicular cones (same R = 0.4, axis at
φ_D ± π/2): the pedestal is raised in L1_J12-only events (SF of "perpendicular cone < 2 GeV" 0.906 ± 0.021; control in 427929
1.017 ± 0.024), but the data − MC excess is 4.9 ± 1.8 GeV in the D⁰ cone against 0.74 ± 0.15 GeV in the perpendicular cone, and
neither pedestal subtraction (L1_J12-only 0.73 ± 0.03) nor reweighting in pT × perpendicular activity × leading-track pT
(0.69 ± 0.04) closes the isolation SF. (2) Sub-sample in which the trigger is explained by a track jet on the away side
(pT > 15 GeV, no jet above 5 GeV in the D⁰ hemisphere): SF = 0.40 ± 0.13 (0.60 ± 0.15 after pT reweighting) against 0.455 ± 0.027
inclusive, i.e. the deficit stays. The cells defined by minimum-bias trigger bits are not a test in 428770: such events are in the ntuple only if a chain of the skim set fired as well (Sec. 6.11), and they hold fewer than 50 D*. (3) A
neutral-energy proxy r_n = ξ_trk/ξ_cal differs between L1_J12-only and the rest of the run (5.7σ), but reweighting in it does not
change the SF (0.53 ± 0.03 against 0.57 ± 0.03); r_n in MC is on a different scale (ratio 21.9), so only a quantile scheme was
usable. Conclusion: the hypothesis that the deficit is an event-level bias removable by an event variable is rejected; the excess
sits in the D⁰ cone itself. What remains is an efficiency from data per trigger stream, or embedding (done, Sec. 5.8: not a calibration).
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/TRKJET2/RAPORT.md §0–§1, out/trkjet2.json (17.11) -->

#### 6.2.4b Events written by AFP chains (AFPSTREAM, 17.11; qualitative)
Most AFP tags in 428770 sit in events recorded by proton triggers (trigger composition of the tagged region: Sec. 6.11). That sample is not one stream but a mix of chains, and it is **not** minimum-bias-like: the events with a D candidate are selected mainly by AFP chains with a jet or a track requirement (and the 428770 ntuples are a
skim that keeps track-triggered events only if a jet chain fired as well, Sec. 6.11), and in background and
activity the sample is closest to the HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 class without L1_J12 ("MBTS ∧ j20") (two-sample AUC 0.70; 0.80 against 427929 and against J12-only). Only the
HLT_noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C part (prescale 60–120) has a minimum-bias-like background (isolated fraction 0.51 ± 0.02 against 0.48 in 427929).
The D* statistics of this sample are too small for a scale-factor measurement, so the
isolation and classifier scale factors measured in the inclusive sample are **not** validated there; that they carry over is a
hypothesis.
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/AFPSTREAM/RAPORT.md (17.11) -->

#### 6.2.4c Clone of the D⁰ CharmNet with and without isolation (CN_clone, 17.11)
A network with the 14 D⁰ inputs, architecture and loss of the CharmNet recipe (our own weights; trained on fd_nd + mb_nd only,
5 folds by event) was trained twice, with and without ptcone40/ptcone100, and measured with the D* tag. On MC, isolation raises the
AUC from 0.873/0.888/0.908 to 0.897/0.911/0.928 (3–5/5–8/8–30 GeV) and ε_S/√ε_B at WP80 by a factor 1.17/1.16/1.14. In data
(428770, all triggers, WP80): SF with isolation 0.98 ± 0.10 (5–8 GeV) and 0.95 ± 0.04 (8–30 GeV), without isolation 1.07 ± 0.10 and
1.01 ± 0.04; ratio R_SF = 0.91 ± 0.12 and 0.94 ± 0.05 (L1_J12-only: 0.89 ± 0.16 and 0.93 ± 0.05; 427929: 0.97 ± 0.11 and
1.05 ± 0.11). The gain from isolation in data is 1.55 ± 0.21 and 1.36 ± 0.07 in 428770, larger than in MC; in 427929 it is 1.07 ± 0.12 and 1.20 ± 0.13, consistent with MC. By the criteria fixed in advance:
the clone is valid on MC; the cost of isolation in ε_S is unresolved in 428770 (a 6–9 % loss at about 1.2σ, with errors treated as
independent, which is conservative) and absent in 427929; the advantage of isolation survives in data. The hard-cut SF of 0.51
therefore does not translate into a network that uses isolation as one of many inputs. Limits: a clone, not CharmNet; D⁰ only;
our pair selection (L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm); no AFP tag (in the class of events fired only by AFP triggers the D* sample is too small for a measurement; trigger composition of the tagged region in Sec. 6.11).
<!-- src: charm_work/CN_clone/RAPORT.md, out/cn_results.json (17.11) -->

#### 6.2.4d Isolation with the underlying-event pedestal subtracted (ISO_ped, 18.11)

Criteria fixed beforehand. The pedestal is the mean of two cones of R = 0.4 at φ_D ± 90° (the full transverse region would need a new extraction); variants: cone − pedestal per event, cone − mean pedestal at the event multiplicity, cone / (pedestal + 1 GeV). Each is cut at the MC signal efficiency of ptcone40 < 2 GeV.

| class | pT [GeV] | SF raw | SF subtracted (three variants) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 427929, minimum bias | 5–8 | 1.07 ± 0.06 | 1.03–1.09 ± 0.06 |
| 428770, all | 5–8 | 0.60 ± 0.05 | 0.74–0.77 ± 0.05 |
| 428770, all | 8–30 | 0.51 ± 0.04 | 0.61–0.68 ± 0.05 |
| 428770, L1_J12 only | 8–30 | 0.43 ± 0.04 | 0.53–0.61 ± 0.05 |
| 428770, MBTS-based | 3–30 | 0.74 ± 0.08 | 0.85–0.96 ± 0.08 |

The subtraction moves the scale factor towards one by 4–6σ (paired) in run 428770, but does not close it, and the background efficiency on the data sidebands rises by 22–47 %; by the pre-set criteria it is not an improvement. Direct test on D*-tagged signal (data/MC of the pedestal | of the excess in the cone above the pedestal): 1.01 ± 0.07 | 1.00 ± 0.12 in run 427929; 1.64 ± 0.17 | 1.04 ± 0.22 in the MBTS-based class of 428770; 1.71 ± 0.08 | 1.78 ± 0.12 in the L1_J12-only class. So in the "MBTS-based" class (92 % HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2: a jet at the HLT, no jet at L1) the mismatch is the pedestal, while the L1 jet trigger also enhances the near-side activity itself; neither class is minimum bias, so this is a comparison of two jet selections, not a confirmation of the minimum-bias picture of the ALICE D–hadron correlations, which no pedestal subtraction removes. At fixed multiplicity the pedestal is the same in data and MC (1.48 / 1.53 / 1.52 GeV for 427929 / 428770 / MC at 60–80 tracks); the difference is the multiplicity distribution (mean 56 against 39 tracks), which is set by the trigger selection and not by the run: for the same chain the inclusive ⟨n_sel^BL⟩ agrees between the runs (HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 34.8 against 35.1, L1_J12 40.0 against 39.6), while all events give 32.5 (428770) against 11.9 (427929). Cells with fewer than 150 signal candidates (427929 at 8–30 GeV, MBTS-based class in bins) are indicative only.
<!-- src: charm_work/ISO_ped_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md, out/tables.md -->

#### 6.2.5 Recommendation

Roles of the runs in what follows: 428770 is the run of the target proton-tagged measurement (the base code configures the tagged fit on this run alone; it holds
the AFP tags and the statistics); 427929 is the clean reference run without trigger bias and, since 20.11, the reference for every absolute normalisation, because no trigger region of 428770 gives one (Sec. 6.11). Before 20.11 this assignment was a working assumption. Results are always quoted per run.

- Isolation with an MC-derived efficiency is acceptable only for the minimum-bias (MBTS) sample, where SF = 0.98 ± 0.04
  (statistical) for ptcone40 < 2 GeV.
- For 428770 a hard isolation cut should not be used with the minimum-bias MC as the signal reference. Isolation as a
  network input is a different case (Sec. 6.2.4c): a loss of 6–9 % in ε_S is not excluded (at most 1.2σ) and the background rejection improves, so it can be
  kept provided the SF is measured in data, per trigger class and pT bin, from D*-tagged D⁰ (which calibrates D⁰ from D*; see the caveat at the start of Sec. 6.2). The present precision of
  the cut-based SF is
  8–9 % per pT bin for L1_J12-only and 14–17 % for the "MBTS-based" class.
- Loose or relative requirements are less affected: after the event-level reweighting the L1_J12-only residual is
  0.80 ± 0.03 for ptcone40 < 4 GeV and 0.82 ± 0.03 for ptcone40/pT < 0.5, against 0.76 ± 0.04 for ptcone40 < 2 GeV.
- A closing correction would need an MC sample with the L1_J12/HLT_j20 decision. Track-jet variables alone do not
  provide it (Sec. 6.2.4).
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/ISO_origin/RAPORT.md §7 pkt 2–4, §4b (other cuts, T7); B_trig/TRKJET/RAPORT.md §0; roles of the runs (20.11): charm_work/INDICO_1709031/ (review of 20.11) §R.3, charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5 -->

### 6.3 Accidental AFP tag by proton mixing (preliminary)

**Status: preliminary.** This section gives only the fraction of accidental tags and its uncertainty. No diffractive
signal yields or cross-sections were computed.

#### 6.3.1 Method (v2)

A tagged event is either true diffraction or an event with an unrelated proton (pile-up without a reconstructed vertex,
beam halo). The tag is exactly one proton on a side with 0.030 ≤ ξ ≤ 0.100, in the region A ∨ C, with the requirement
ξ_cal ≤ ξ_AFP + 0.02 on each tagged side. The accidental fraction is f_acc = N_acc/N_tag.

- **Estimator A (nominal).** The AFP content of a donor event is overlaid on the D candidates of a recipient event of
  the same stratum. Donors must have ξ_cal > 0.14 on the side, so that a proton from the same interaction cannot pass
  the ξ_cal requirement and the donor carries accidental protons only. Strata are (run, luminosity block) × n_sel^BL
  class × "with/without a D candidate", with one-sided pools. The statistical error is a Poisson bootstrap over the
  tagged donors (200 replicas).
- **Extrapolation.** The pool has ξ_cal > 0.14 and the estimate is used at small ξ_cal. The tag rate in the pool is
  fitted linearly in ln ξ_cal and evaluated at the mean ln ξ_cal of the region of use (κ). No correction is applied; the
  full deviation √((κ − 1)² + σ_κ²) enters the uncertainty.
- **Second estimators.** B(i) is an absolute tag probability from a quasi-minimum-bias sample of full activity
  (L1MBTS chains, ξ_cal veto on both sides, n_sel^BL ≥ 20) in six μ intervals. In 428770 this sample is 92 % HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 and is not minimum-bias-like (Sec. 6.11). A random trigger is not available
  (HLT_noalg_L1RD0_FILLED has 0 events in both runs). B(ii) scales A by observed/predicted tags in a control region that
  is accidental by construction (ξ_cal ≤ 0.12, ξ_cal − ξ_AFP > 0.045). Half of the largest difference A − B enters the
  uncertainty.
- **Selection.** GRL, at least one stored chain without L1AFP, no additional reconstructed vertex. Candidates have the
  loose v2.0 selection without a classifier score. The two runs are not combined.
- **Gates.** The verdict requires pool homogeneity in ξ_cal above the threshold (G1), closure on disjoint trigger
  classes (G2), the shape in the control region (G5) and agreement of A with B(ii) and B(i) (G7, G8). G9 is the opposite-side test: the tag rate on one side in events with a true tag on the other side, compared with the mixing prediction.
<!-- src: charmbkg docs/afp_mixing.md §1–§5 (v2, 17.11); charm_work/AFP_mix/runs/lxplus/out_v2/RAPORT_DANE_v2.md (header, §0, §1) -->

#### 6.3.2 Result

| run | **f_acc** | statistical | donor pool | extrapolation | ½ Δ(methods) | total (relative) | κ | B(i)/A | B(ii)/A | gates |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 427929 | **0.0087 ± 0.0044** | 12.7 % | 2.9 % | 47.1 % | 9.9 % | 49.9 % | 0.62 ± 0.28 | 0.80 ± 0.16 | 0.97 ± 0.14 | pass (G9 not applicable) |
| 428770 | **0.048 ± 0.014** (0.0477 ± 0.0135) | 3.3 % | 4.8 % | 10.4 % | 25.8 % | 28.4 % | 1.05 ± 0.09 | 1.52 ± 0.29 | 1.12 ± 0.06 | pass (G9 not applicable) |

In 427929 the uncertainty is dominated by the extrapolation in ξ_cal (902 tagged donors). In 428770 it is dominated by
half the difference with B(i), which rests on 43 tagged donors. The extrapolation and the B(ii) component address the
same question, so their quadratic sum is conservative. Per side, with statistical errors only: 427929 A 0.0094 ± 0.0017,
C 0.0080 ± 0.0014; 428770 A 0.0455 ± 0.0022, C 0.0500 ± 0.0025. Under the D⁰ mass peak (all candidates in the window,
no fit) f_acc is 0.0091 ± 0.0010 ± 0.0044 and 0.0502 ± 0.0015 ± 0.0141, the same as in the mass sidebands within errors.
The v1 estimator gave 0.0087 and 0.0459. [Fig. F17](figures/F17_afp_mix_facc.pdf) shows f_acc per run and side and the
error budget.
<!-- src: charm_work/AFP_mix/runs/lxplus/out_v2/RAPORT_DANE_v2.md §1 (table), per-run "Estymatory", "f_acc inkluzywnie per region",
     "Gatunki × pT" (D0, pT 3–150); result_v2.json (F17 input; points in figures/F17_points.json). Event counts are deliberately not quoted. -->

#### 6.3.3 Range of validity and known limitations

- **Non-AFP triggers only.** Events selected only by L1AFP chains are excluded from recipients and donors, and the
  method does not describe them: they have a different activity and ξ_cal, and the tag is forced by the trigger. In
  428770 the method therefore covers 4.4 % of all tags after the GRL (about 96 % are outside its range); in 427929 it
  covers 100.0 %. Because the 428770 ntuples are a skim (Sec. 6.11), 3.4 % of the tagged events of this sample (1148 of 33 592; 30 % for n_sel^BL < 5, 0.6 % of those with a D candidate) carry a non-AFP bit of a chain outside the skim set and are present only through an AFP chain, i.e. their tag is forced by the trigger. Removing these events from targets and donors changes the nominal estimate from 0.0477 to 0.0473 (0.0476 if a stored non-AFP chain of the skim set is required for all events), i.e. by less than 0.03 of its uncertainty. The forced tags did bias the cross-check B(i): 9 of its 43 tagged donors are forced, and without them B(i)/A goes from 1.52 ± 0.29 to 1.13 ± 0.23. The quoted uncertainty, dominated by half of this difference, is therefore conservative. A sample triggered by L1AFP needs recipients representative of that trigger class. This is an open
  decision; a first attempt for those events is described at the end of Sec. 6.3.4.
  <!-- src: charm_work/AFP_mix/FORCED_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md -->
- **Blind spot: a step in the accidental rate at small ξ_cal.** Below ξ_cal ≈ 0.075 true and accidental tags cannot be
  separated, so the rate there is not testable. In the toy, a rate doubled below ξ_cal = 0.07 biases the estimate by
  −36 % with no gate failing and a 2σ coverage of 33 %. A step at 0.12 is detected by G7 only when it is a factor of 2
  (17 % power for a factor of 1.5). The linear extrapolation in ln ξ_cal is an assumption, not a measurement.
- **B(i) is not fully independent.** It uses the same trigger sample and the same ξ_cal veto as A.
- **G9 is not applicable in either run** (fewer than 5 expected). In 428770 it has 10 observed against 4.1 predicted.
- **Rate rise at low n_sel^BL in 428770, partly explained.** Donors without a D candidate have a tag rate up to 31 times
  higher there. The nominal estimate avoids this with the "with D" class; using donors with and without D changes it by
  ×1.15 ± 0.03. Forced tags (see "Non-AFP triggers only" above) are 30 % of the tags for n_sel^BL < 5; without them the rise relative to n_sel^BL 40–60 is a factor of 22 instead of 31.
- **No classifier score.** f_acc must be recomputed for the sample after the classifier cut.
<!-- src: RAPORT_DANE_v2.md §0, per-run "zakres (W2)" (33 592 tagged selected vs 737 591 tagged L1AFP-only in 428770), "Ograniczenia";
     charmbkg docs/afp_mixing.md §6 (power table: xical_step_0.07_x2, xical_step_0.12_x1.5/x2), §8; charm_work/REV_afp_mix/RAPORT.md K1, W2 -->

#### 6.3.4 Precedents

The construction follows published forward-proton analyses. CMS-TOTEM single-diffractive dijets [2002.12146] use two
background methods, protons from zero-bias events and protons from events with large ξ_CMS normalised in a
background-dominated region, and take half their difference as a systematic. This is the template for the pool of
estimator A, for B(ii) and for the half-difference rule. ATLAS ALFA [1911.00453] overlays an absolute tag probability
from a full-activity control sample, the template for B(i). The ATLAS AFP analyses pair ξ_AFP from a control sample at
random and vary the pool definition as a systematic [2009.14537], and replace the AFP data by those of other events in
intervals of instantaneous luminosity [2304.10953].
<!-- src: charm_work/RECON_lit_B/RAPORT.md §1 (table rows 1, 3, 4, 5; all marked [Ź] = read in full text); docs/afp_mixing.md §9 -->

**Template and test of the low-ξ_cal extrapolation (17.11, proposal).** The accidental component is tabulated per run, side,
species and pT bin (`charm_work/AFP_mix/TEMPLATE/template.{json,csv}`, schema in its README); f_acc per bin is 0.008–0.011 in
427929 and 0.043–0.056 in 428770. Its mass shape is **not** that of the untagged sample for D⁺ (relative slope −0.29 ± 0.01 /GeV
in 428770, χ²/ndf = 579/29; −0.21 ± 0.04 in 427929) and D⁰ (−0.12 ± 0.02 /GeV); D* is compatible. The template must therefore be
the p_acc-weighted histogram, not the untagged shape scaled by f_acc. Two signal-free tests bound a change of the accidental
rate below the donor threshold: the opposite-side ("mirror") estimator gives 0.79 ± 0.04 (428770) and 0.83 ± 0.12 (427929) of the
nominal, and kinematically impossible protons (ξ_AFP + δ < ξ_cal) at ξ_cal 0.09–0.14 give 1.00 ± 0.05 and 1.14 ± 0.12. No upward
step is seen. Proposed replacement of the extrapolation systematic: +18/−21 % (427929) and +5/−21 % (428770), i.e.
f_acc = 0.0087 +0.0021/−0.0024 and 0.048 +0.013/−0.016. Not adopted yet: below ξ_cal ≈ 0.06 (55 % of the weights in the region of
use) a tag-side-specific mechanism is not constrained by data, and the side symmetry assumed by the mirror test is disfavoured at
2.4σ in 428770.
<!-- src: charm_work/AFP_mix/TEMPLATE/RAPORT.md, jump_summary.json (17.11) -->

**Events written only by AFP chains (validation not passed in 428770).** The rate method above does not apply to events
recorded only by proton triggers (about 96 % of the tags in 428770, counted with the 11 chains of our converted files; they are a mix of chains, composition by all stored HLT chains in Sec. 6.11). There the accidental component was estimated from the
shape of Δ = ξ_cal − ξ_AFP: a mixed template (proton from the AFP stream overlaid on ξ_cal of non-AFP events of the same lumi
block, stratified in n_sel^BL) normalised in the control region Δ > δ and extrapolated into the signal region, as in the
CMS-TOTEM and ALFA analyses. For events with a D candidate (sides A + C): f_acc = 0.0513 ± 0.0007 (stat) +0.0047/−0.0069 (syst)
in 428770 and 0.0100 ± 0.0011 ± 0.0006 in 427929; per species × pT × peak/sideband in 428770 it is 0.05–0.06, the same under
the peak and in the sidebands. Validation with criteria fixed in advance: the closure inside the control region fails in 428770
(observed/predicted 1.16 ± 0.02; passes in 427929 and in the non-AFP stream), and the result depends monotonically on the
normalisation margin (0.0448 ± 0.0018 when normalised in Δ > 0.10). The excess runs along ξ_cal ≈ ξ_AFP + const and grows with
μ, i.e. true protons leak into the control region (about 2 % of true tags have Δ between 0.02 and 0.10) and inflate the
estimate; the cause (pile-up without a reconstructed vertex) is a hypothesis. Agreement with the rate method where both apply
(0.0087 and 0.0495 against 0.0087 and 0.048) is largely by construction. Not validated: HLT_noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C alone (closure 1.53;
0.043 indicative only). Shuffling protons inside the AFP stream is useless (overestimates by × 10–20). The region ξ_cal ≲ 0.075
remains untestable (toy bias −35 % with a correct closure).
<!-- src: charm_work/AFP_mix/AFPTRIG/RAPORT.md, facc_afptrig.json, KRYTERIA_WALIDACJI.txt (17.11) -->

### 6.4 Not offered; general rules

Not offered: background generators as a better training background (ceiling shown); the K/π arbiter (≈ hash; `keep_both`
better for single D⁰). General rules: never accept a background model on C2ST alone — require ε_B(SR) agreement with a
sideband fit to data after the cut; apply reflection/D* veto and SB_min ≥ 70 MeV before any SB-based fit; same classifier
on SR and SB with a signal-free MC test before every fit.
<!-- src: charm_work/WK2_wklad_AFP/WKLAD.md (Czego NIE oferujemy); docs/signal_like_background.md §3a, §5 -->

### 6.5 Background model: how much of the data/MC difference is the trigger (17.11)

R = ε_B(data, pol2 interpolation from sidebands) / ε_B(MC in the signal region, truth ≥ 2), averaged over pT (dominated by 3–5 GeV):

| WP | 428770 | 427929 | 427929, MC reweighted (incl. score) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 95 | 0.812 ± 0.012 | 0.883 ± 0.013 | 0.988 ± 0.015 |
| 80 | 0.767 ± 0.017 | 0.859 ± 0.033 | 0.998 ± 0.038 |
| 50 | 0.709 ± 0.034 | 0.879 ± 0.073 | 1.061 ± 0.088 |

The jet triggers account for about one third of the data/MC difference in ε_B at 3–5 GeV and about one half at 5–8 GeV; a
12–14 % deficit remains in clean minimum-bias data (χ² against 1: 133/15 in 427929, 833/15 in 428770; run-to-run 72/15).
At 8–30 GeV the 427929 measurement is not decisive (error 0.08–0.30). A two-sample classifier on six event/candidate variables
plus the score separates 428770 from MC with AUC 0.876 and from 427929 with 0.870, but 427929 from MC only with 0.596.
Reweighting the MC in sidebands with a classifier that includes the score closes ε_B under the peak in 427929
(χ²/ndf = 13.2/15, n_eff/N = 0.84, no mass leakage: R² = 0.013); reweighting without the score brings 428770 to the 427929 level
only (n_eff/N = 0.13). Recommendation: use 427929 as the control run in which the background-model method is validated; the ±5/10/25 % budget
(the D⁺ numbers, as quoted in the source; the D⁰ budget of Sec. 5.2 is ±5/10/15–20 %; defined at WP80/70/50; the comparison here was made at WP95/80/50, so only WP80 and WP50 are directly checked) is consistent
with it for 3–8 GeV. In 428770, which is the run of the proton-tagged measurement, the same budget
does not cover the trigger-induced part of the difference (ε_B data/MC 0.81/0.77/0.71), which has to be taken from data
per trigger class or added as a separate uncertainty. Caveats: the inclusive sample used here lacks the 18 classifier inputs and η, so SALAD v1.0 itself
was not rerun; the source of the residual (vertex-variable modelling) is a hypothesis.
<!-- src: charm_work/B_trig/BKG_427929/RAPORT.md (17.11) -->

**Spread of estimators (EST_spread, 17.11).** Following the ALICE practice of comparing several combinatorial-background
estimators, the estimates of the background under the peak were collected in one table (1290 rows with sources) and ten D⁰
estimators were recomputed on the same sample for both runs. RMS spread of the four data-based estimators (exp(pol2) fit,
sideband counting, linear interpolation, reweighted MC), WP95→WP50: D⁰ in 427929 1.0–3.5 % (3–5 GeV), 2.2–4.4 % (5–8 GeV),
1.7–8.1 % (8–30 GeV); 428770 similar up to 8 GeV and 5–15 % at 8–30 GeV; everywhere below the ±5/10/15–20 % budget. Raw MC is off
by 13–16 %; after reweighting it agrees to 1.3–1.6 %. D⁺ (428770 sub-sample only): 3.2/4.0/7.7 % at WP80/70/50, up to 9/12/27 % in
single pT bins. In the MC closure the spread alone covers the deviation of the nominal from truth in 4 of 15 cells only (χ² = 12.8/15
once the statistical error of the nominal is included), and it does not cover a change of the background composition at all
(adversarial closure: all estimators give 0.65–0.85 of truth). Recommendation: half-range of the four estimators per run, added in
quadrature to the composition term; this gives ±4–5/6–7/16–18 % for D⁰ (budget not contradicted) and about ±7/8/15 % for D⁺, i.e. ±7 % instead of ±5 % for D⁺
at WP80 (a proposal, not adopted: the budget quoted elsewhere in this note is unchanged). Missing: D⁺ on 427929, like-sign and rotation/mixing with refit.
<!-- src: charm_work/EST_spread/RAPORT.md, out/tabela_estymatory.csv, out/rozrzut.csv (17.11) -->

### 6.6 Background components taken from MC: test on data (MC_templates, 17.11)
In the AFP single-D fit only the combinatorial component is free; the reflection and the other components (charm mismatch,
decay-mode mismatch, partially combinatorial) have their normalisation and shape from MC. They were tested with our selection
and classifier, outside the tagged signal region, with criteria fixed in advance; the closure on MC passes in 18 of 18 cells.
- **Reflection K↔π (D⁰), from the D* tag:** the ratio of reflected to correctly assigned candidates agrees with MC in every
  sample: data/MC = 0.98 ± 0.06, 0.97 ± 0.05, 0.99 ± 0.04 (428770, WP95/80/50), 0.93 ± 0.09, 0.98 ± 0.07, 1.01 ± 0.08 (427929),
  0.97 ± 0.07 to 0.99 ± 0.05 in L1_J12-only. The normalisation from MC is therefore confirmed to about 5 % in 428770 and to 7–9 % in 427929. The shape is not:
  χ²/ndf up to 71/34 in 428770, and replacing the MC reflection shape by the data one moves N_S by 4–10 % in 427929 and by up
  to 14–17 % in the "MBTS-based" class at 8–30 GeV, more than one third of the statistical error in most cells.
- **Stability of the MC composition under reweighting to the event activity of a trigger class:** stable in 427929 (changes of
  0–12 %, n_eff/N ≥ 0.90); in 428770 the combinatorial and partially combinatorial fractions change by +80 to +140 % at
  3–8 GeV with n_eff/N of 0.11–0.26, i.e. the MC composition cannot be transferred to the jet-triggered sample by reweighting.
- **D⁺ reflections:** the D*⁺ → D⁰π⁺ peak relative to the D⁺ yield is 1.34–1.37 ± 0.15–0.19 of MC at WP95 and 1.10–1.12 ± 0.14–0.18 at WP80 in
  427929 (20.11: peak counted above a threshold-shaped background, the range being two fit ranges of that background). The values quoted before, 1.35 ± 0.16 and 1.11 ± 0.14, counted the peak above a linear background, which underestimates the background in the window by 13–21 %; with the threshold shape the peak yields are lower by 12–14 % in data and MC alike, so the ratio and the reading (inconsistent at WP95, consistent at WP80) do not change. This was the only number of the note with a linearly interpolated Δm background outside the withdrawn input calibration (Sec. 7). φ, D_s and Λ_c peaks are not measurable with this statistics.
- **Fraction from b** (fit of the impact-parameter distribution): consistent with MC at 5–8 GeV (0.14 ± 0.09 against 0.15 in
  427929); inconsistent in single cells (D⁰ 3–5 GeV in 427929, D⁺ 8–30 GeV in 428770), where the fit is at a boundary — not a
  usable measurement.
- **Proposed systematic on the MC components (in % of N_S):** D⁰ 4–11 % in 427929 (dominated by the reflection shape), 4–17 % in
  the "MBTS-based" class of 428770; D⁺ 2–13 % (dominated by the partially combinatorial component, with an assumed 25 %
  normalisation uncertainty). For comparison, changing the functional form of the combinatorial background moves N_S by −2 to
  −25 % (D⁰) and up to −50 % (D⁺ at 3–8 GeV in 428770).
Transferable to the AFP analysis: the category definitions, the reflection ratio and shape at a given L_xy cut, the sensitivities.
Not transferable one to one: fractions after the classifier cut (CharmNet has other inputs), our L_xy ≥ 0.25 mm and D* veto, the
2–3 GeV bin, and the tagged region, where the composition was not looked at.
<!-- src: charm_work/MC_templates/runs/lxplus/ana/WYNIKI.md §1–§5, results.json; KRYTERIA.md (17.11); D* peak in D⁺ candidates with a threshold background (20.11): charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0, §2, §8 pkt 1; truth composition of the MC background after the tree H: Sec. 5.13 -->

### 6.7 Efficiency of the pile-up vertex veto (inclusive sample, 18.11)

The event selection of the AFP analysis requires `nPileupVertices == 0`. Measured on the inclusive sample (no proton tag) with our D⁰ selection (pT ≥ 3 GeV, L_xy ≥ 0.25 mm); the MC has no pile-up (μ = 0).

| sample | ⟨μ⟩ | D* signal (Δm sidebands subtracted, background normalised from a threshold-function fit) | events with a D⁰ candidate | data/MC |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 428770, all | 0.054 | 93.95 ± 0.73 % | 94.08 ± 0.02 % | 0.949 |
| 428770, L1_J12 only | 0.054 | 94.72 ± 0.82 % | 94.78 ± 0.02 % | 0.956 |
| 428770, MBTS-based | 0.054 | 93.92 ± 2.13 % | 94.45 ± 0.05 % | 0.952 |
| 428770, other triggers (events with a D⁰ only) | 0.055 | – | 88.24 ± 0.09 % | – |
| 427929 | 0.0085 | 98.60 ± 0.69 % | 98.48 ± 0.02 % | 0.993 |
| MC | 0 | 99.25 ± 0.04 % | 99.0–99.2 % | – |

⟨μ⟩ in this table is the mean over the selected events of the per-bunch value (`actual_mu`); the official run averages (`average_mu`) are 0.0452 for 428770 and 0.00469 for 427929, and the values of Sec. 2 (0.051 and about 0.005) are medians over events.

The veto efficiency follows P = (1 − f) · exp(−ε_v μ): f = 1.27 ± 0.05 %, ε_v = 0.770 ± 0.011 in 428770 (L1_J12-only and "MBTS-based" classes together; pile-up loss 4.05 %) and f = 0.97 ± 0.02 %, ε_v = 0.656 ± 0.025 in 427929 (loss 0.56 %); ε_v differs between the runs by 3.9σ (cause not established), so the correction has to be taken per run. The term f (a second reconstructed vertex without pile-up) depends on the track multiplicity and is modelled to 1.0–1.25 in data/MC. The class "other triggers" (neither L1_J12 nor an MBTS chain; 10 % of events with a D⁰, 24.6 % of them with a recorded L1AFP chain), i.e. events written by chains of the skim set that are not among the 11 stored bits (AFP, EM, MU and forward-jet chains, Sec. 6.11), is given only as an event fraction by trigger bits; its efficiency falls with μ twice as fast as a pure Poisson term allows (slope 1.98 ± 0.05), i.e. the selection is correlated with an extra collision, and it is excluded from the scale factor: the 0.955 below therefore does not cover events written by AFP chains, which make up almost all of the tagged region (Sec. 6.11). No dependence on the candidate is seen: signal minus all events with a D⁰ is +0.01 ± 0.50 %. Proposed scale factor for the normalisation: **0.955 ± 0.005 (428770), 0.993 ± 0.003 (427929)**; the uncertainty is a budget of assumed components (background-to-signal transfer, modelling of f, pT and L_xy dependence, trigger-class mix), not a fit result; the direct measurement on signal gives 0.954 ± 0.008 and 0.993 ± 0.007. In the ratio of tagged to inclusive yields the pile-up term cancels only for true tags, under the assumption that the extra collision is independent; f does not cancel if the multiplicities differ, and accidental tags, where the proton comes from an extra collision, have a different veto efficiency by construction. Not checked: D⁺, the 2–3 GeV bin, CharmNet selection.
<!-- src: charm_work/VETO_pv_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md; definition of ⟨μ⟩ (20.11): charm_work/LUMI_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md §6; sideband wording: charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1 row 12 -->

### 6.8 Calibrating the classifier efficiency below 5 GeV with a cut scan on inclusive D⁺ (feasibility, 18.11)

The D* tag gives no measurement of ε_S below 5 GeV. Tested alternative: stability of N_fit(WP)/ε_S^MC(WP) on inclusive D⁺ (classifier H, common exp(pol3) background, paired event bootstrap), criteria fixed beforehand. ρ(a/b) is the ratio of that quantity at two working points.

| run | pT [GeV] | ρ(70/90) | ρ(70/80) | 2σ sensitivity, 70/90 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 427929 | 3–5 | 0.92 ± 0.10 | 1.00 ± 0.06 | 21 % |
| 427929 | 5–8 | 1.17 ± 0.10 | 0.91 ± 0.05 | 17 % |
| 428770 | 3–5 | 1.10 ± 0.16 | 1.04 ± 0.08 | 30 % |
| 428770 | 8–30 | 0.94 ± 0.04 | 0.93 ± 0.03 | 9 % |

By the pre-set criterion (2σ sensitivity ≤ 15 % at 3–5 GeV in 427929) the scan is **not usable as a calibration**. An injected relative scale factor of 0.90 is recovered as 0.92 ± 0.12 at 3–5 GeV, but as 0.85 ± 0.09 and 0.76 ± 0.09 at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV, because the fit at the loosest working point overestimates the yield. The scan sees only the change of the scale factor between working points, not a common shift. Inclusive D⁰ is worse (the reflection-to-signal ratio depends on the working point). In the 2–3 GeV bin (Sec. 6.12) the scan gives only "no dependence of the scale factor on the working point larger than about 20 %" in 427929. Recommendation: use the scan as a stability test and as a systematic of about 10 % (427929) to 15 % (428770) at 3–5 GeV, with the loosest point not looser than ε_S ≈ 80 %, and transfer the absolute scale factor from the D* tag at 5–8 GeV with an extrapolation uncertainty. Numbers are for our classifier H and indicate the order of magnitude for CharmNet.
<!-- src: charm_work/LOWPT_calib_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md -->

**Absolute scale factor at WP70 for D⁺ and for D⁰ at 3–5 GeV (SFABS, 20.11; conditional).** Criteria and closure gates were written before the data fits of this study; it is post hoc with respect to Secs. 5.11–5.13. Three methods were used.
- (i) Reference: the D* tag for D⁰ at 5–30 GeV, with a joint two-dimensional fit in (m(Kπ), Δm) of the passing and failing candidates, a threshold background in Δm and the efficiency as a fit parameter. The four cells (two runs, 5–8 and 8–30 GeV) agree (χ²/ndf = 1.45/3): **SF_ref = 0.968 ± 0.029 (stat) ± 0.012 (fit model)**. The cell of run 427929 at 5–8 GeV is taken from the Δm pass/fail fit of Sec. 5.11, because the two-dimensional fit failed its purity gate there; the cell of run 428770 at 8–30 GeV has a weight of 0.80.
- (ii) Transfer to D⁺ and to D⁰ at 3–5 GeV. The systematic uncertainty is the difference in the sensitivity of the classifiers to the resolution scale of the impact parameters and of the vertex (MC smearing with scales 1.05–1.10, two smearing models) and to the scale and smearing of dE/dx: 4.0 / 4.7 / 4.9 % for D⁺ at 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV and 1.2 % for D⁰ at 3–5 GeV.
- (iii) Direct checks in the target bins. For D⁺ a "ladder" SF(WP70) = SF(WP90) × SF_rel(70 | 90) from a joint fit of disjoint score categories of the inclusive peak, corrected for the fit bias measured on MC with full background: 0.74 ± 0.17 / 1.17 ± 0.15 / 1.09 ± 0.12 (3–5 GeV from run 427929 only; average of the three bins 1.03 ± 0.08). For D⁰ at 3–5 GeV the D* tag in the same bin, run 427929: 0.96 ± 0.16 (stat) ± 0.08 (model); run 428770 fails the gates there.

All checks agree with the transferred value within 1.3σ, and the methods were combined in a weighted mean:

| species | pT [GeV] | ladder (direct) | D* tag, 2D fit (direct) | transfer from D⁰ ≥ 5 GeV (± stat ± model ± transfer) | **SF(WP70), combined** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D⁺ | 3–5 | 0.74 ± 0.17 | – | 0.968 ± 0.029 ± 0.012 ± 0.039 | **0.95 ± 0.05** |
| D⁺ | 5–8 | 1.17 ± 0.15 | – | 0.968 ± 0.029 ± 0.012 ± 0.045 | **0.99 ± 0.05** |
| D⁺ | 8–30 | 1.09 ± 0.12 | – | 0.968 ± 0.029 ± 0.012 ± 0.048 | **0.99 ± 0.05** |
| D⁰ | 3–5 | gates not passed | 0.96 ± 0.16 ± 0.08 | 0.968 ± 0.029 ± 0.012 ± 0.012 | **0.97 ± 0.03** |

These values replace the earlier assumption of a scale factor of one with a 10 % uncertainty for D⁺ and for D⁰ at 3–5 GeV; the cross-sections that use them belong to Sec. 6.13. What the ±0.05 rests on: the combined value is dominated by the transfer (weights 0.81–0.97), which is **conditional** on the data/MC differences being of the resolution or dE/dx type. The direct methods alone have errors of 0.12–0.18. An effect of another kind, specific to D⁺, is bounded directly only at ±0.08 (the ladder averaged over the bins, 1.03 ± 0.08 against SF_ref); **cautious variant: ±0.08 for D⁺**. The ratio at 3–5 GeV is SF⁺/SF⁰ = 0.981 ± 0.042 (the common SF_ref cancels). Caveats: SF_ref is dominated by the jet-triggered run and is applied to run 427929 (the two runs agree at 8–30 GeV within 1.0σ: 1.074 ± 0.106 against 0.960 ± 0.033); in run 427929 the two-dimensional fit gives a scale factor about 0.10 higher than the Δm pass/fail fit on the same data (5–8 GeV: 1.10 against 1.01; 8–30 GeV: 1.07 against 0.97), an order of magnitude for the method dependence at low statistics; the ladder fit for D⁺ at 3–5 GeV has p(χ²) = 0.014 and its bias correction comes from one MC realisation; one training seed of the tree; the 2–3 GeV bin is not covered. This qualifies the statement of Sec. 7 that no pass/fail scheme gives the absolute scale factor to better than about 0.15: that remains true for the direct methods.
<!-- src: charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0–§5; charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/KRYTERIA.md; charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/tabele/{a_tab,b_tab,c_tab,d_tab}.md; charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/akapit_do_noty.md (the cross-sections of that paragraph are deliberately not copied) -->

**Estimator dependence of the D*-tag efficiencies in run 427929 (audit of 20.11; numbers of the reports, no new fit).** Two differences between estimators on the same data are quoted in this note: for the isolation efficiency (Sec. 6.2.2; sideband counting against the threshold fits, SF 1.05–1.12 against 0.98 ± 0.04) and for the classifier scale factor (two-dimensional fit against the Δm pass/fail fit, about +0.10, above). They are not shown to be one effect. The isolation difference sits at 3–5 GeV only (S/B = 0.57: +0.11 to +0.24 in the scale factor; within ±0.04 at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV), grows with the width of the counting window, and the three threshold-fit estimators agree within 0.02 in every bin. The classifier difference is at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV, where the isolation estimators are stable; toys give a bias of the two-dimensional fit of +0.05 to +0.07 at 5–8 GeV (where its purity gate failed) and +0.03 at 8–30 GeV, which leaves 1.03–1.05 and 1.04 against 1.01 and 0.97; and the four Δm estimators of the classifier efficiency at WP70 scatter on both sides of the nominal (in units of the scale factor 0.94–1.03 at 5–8 GeV and 0.91–1.01 at 8–30 GeV, half-range 0.05). What the two have in common: with the statistics of run 427929 the choice of the background estimator in Δm moves a D*-tag efficiency by 0.05–0.10, and the statistical error of a single fit does not contain this. Treatment (decision of 20.11): for the classifier scale factors that enter σ(D⁰) the half-range of the four Δm estimators (0.048 and 0.049) is added in quadrature to the statistical error of the pass/fail fit (0.126 and 0.097), which gives 1.005 ± 0.135 and 0.965 ± 0.109 and raises the systematic total of σ(D⁰) from 16.8 to 17.5 % at 5–8 GeV and from 20.5 to 21.1 % at 8–30 GeV (applied in Sec. 6.13). The isolation value 0.98 ± 0.04 is to be read with a one-sided estimator spread of +0.07 to +0.14, which comes from the 3–5 GeV bin.
<!-- src: charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §3 (both tables); charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §2 (two-dimensional fit, toys); charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/out/final.json (SF, budgets); arithmetic: charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §6 -->
