**Independent cross-check with a cut-based selection (XSEC_atlas, 20.11; post hoc; cut values fixed before the data fits).** The D⁰ and D⁺ measurements of run 427929 were repeated without the classifier, with a rectangular selection following the published ATLAS analyses (arXiv:2302.00336 and arXiv:1512.02913: track pT, vertex χ², L_xy, impact parameter of the D, three-dimensional impact significance, cos θ*, D* and φ vetoes for D⁺), on the same candidate tables and with the same fit code; the statistical correlation comes from a common event bootstrap. R is the ratio of the result with cuts to the result with the classifier at WP70, so 1/R is a scale factor of the classifier efficiency under the assumption that the MC describes the efficiency of the cuts:

| species | pT [GeV] | R ± stat ± MC stat | 1/R |
|---|---|---|---|
| D⁰ | 3–5 | 0.92 ± 0.13 ± 0.02 | 1.09 ± 0.16 |
| D⁰ | 5–8 | 0.99 ± 0.09 ± 0.02 | 1.01 ± 0.10 |
| D⁰ | 8–30 | 1.11 ± 0.09 ± 0.02 | 0.90 ± 0.08 |
| D⁺ | 3–5 | 0.77 ± 0.11 ± 0.03 | 1.29 ± 0.19 |
| D⁺ | 5–8 | 0.96 ± 0.07 ± 0.03 | 1.04 ± 0.08 |
| D⁺ | 8–30 | 1.12 ± 0.08 ± 0.03 | 0.89 ± 0.07 |
| D⁰ | 5–30 | 1.05 ± 0.07 | **0.95 ± 0.06 ± 0.08** |
| D⁺ | 5–30 | 1.04 ± 0.06 | **0.97 ± 0.05 ± 0.05** |

The second uncertainty of the last two rows is the spread over pre-defined variations of the cuts (5 % for D⁺, 8 % for D⁰), not a measurement. For D⁺ this is a constraint on the classifier scale factor that is independent of the D* tag and of the transfer, and it agrees with the combined value above; for D⁰ it agrees with the D*-tag scale factor. The level of the D⁰ result is therefore not caused by the classifier. At 3–5 GeV the D⁺ value (1.9σ from one) is a warning about the WP70 yield there (that fit has p = 0.04 and a width scale of 1.16), not a scale-factor measurement; it points the other way than the ladder value of the same bin (0.74 ± 0.17); their compatibility is quantified in the next paragraph. R does not see errors common to both selections (tracking, vertexing, preselection).
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §B, Zastrzeżenia; charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/KRYTERIA.md §B; charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/tabele/wyniki.md; the cross-sections of that report are deliberately not copied -->

**Compatibility of the D⁺ numbers at 3–5 GeV (audit of 20.11; arithmetic on the quoted values, no new fit).** There are three inputs: the ladder (0.741 ± 0.165), the transfer (0.968 ± 0.050) and the cut-based 1/R (1.29 ± 0.19). Their weighted mean is 0.97 ± 0.05 with χ²/ndf = 4.8/2 (p = 0.09); the two direct numbers differ by 2.2σ (p = 0.03). Both direct numbers rise with the WP70 yield of the classifier, so their correlation makes the tension larger, not smaller. The nominal 0.95 ± 0.05 combines the ladder and the transfer only (χ²/ndf = 1.7/1, p = 0.19; the ladder has a weight of 0.08); 1/R is 1.7σ above it. Scaling the error of the three-input mean by √(χ²/ndf) = 1.54 gives ± 0.07. The ± 0.05 is thus the error of the transfer under its assumption; it does not cover the disagreement of the direct checks in this bin. The defensible size for D⁺ at 3–5 GeV is the cautious variant ± 0.08. Decision of 20.11: ± 0.08 is used for D⁺ at 3–5 GeV in Sec. 6.13 (central value unchanged; ± 0.05 is kept at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV). With the yields of Sec. 5.14 the scale-factor row of σ(D⁺, 3–5 GeV) is then 8.4 % instead of 5.0 % and the systematic total 16.0 % instead of 14.5 % (82 instead of 75 µb); in D⁺/D⁰ at 3–5 GeV the scale-factor term rises from 4.2 to 8.0 %.
<!-- src: charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0, §1, §3; charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §B; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/out/final.json (variant SFABS_pm008); arithmetic: charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §5 -->

### 6.9 External inputs: branching fractions and charm fragmentation fractions (18.11)

Compiled from public sources read in full on 18.11.2026 (table with all values and sources in `charm_work/BR_frag_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md`).

| nuisance parameter | value | ±1σ | source | cancels in tagged/inclusive ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B(D⁰→K⁻π⁺), shared by D⁰ and D*⁺ | 0.03936 | 0.00030 (0.76 %) | PDG 2026 | yes |
| B(D⁺→K⁻π⁺π⁺) | 0.0931 | 0.0014 (1.50 %) | PDG 2026 | yes |
| B(D*⁺→D⁰π⁺) | 0.677 | 0.005 (0.74 %) | PDG 2026 | yes |
| B(D*⁺→D⁰π⁺) × B(D⁰→K⁻π⁺), D* chain (derived) | 0.02665 | 0.00028 (1.06 %) | product of the two above | yes |
| f(c→D⁰) / f(c→D⁺) / f(c→D*⁺) in pp at 13 TeV | 0.382 / 0.191 / 0.156 | +0.026−0.045 / +0.021−0.027 / +0.026−0.023 | ALICE, arXiv:2308.04877 | to first order |
| same, e⁺e⁻/ep average (default combination; proposed as a two-point model variation) | 0.609 / 0.240 / 0.243 | 0.008 / 0.007 / 0.005 | arXiv:1509.01061 | to first order |
| non-prompt fraction (b→D) | from MC, 5–15 % rising with pT | ±30 % relative (our proposal) | – | **no** |

A fiducial cross-section at the D-meson level needs only the branching fractions (0.8–1.5 %). The fragmentation fractions enter through backgrounds normalised from MC and in any extrapolation to σ(cc̄); the pp values differ strongly from the e⁺e⁻ ones (f(c→D⁰) 0.38 against 0.61, because of the baryon enhancement), while the ratios D⁺/D⁰ and D*⁺/D⁰ agree. The ALICE fractions are integrated over pT > 0 at |y| < 0.5, not over our fiducial region. The non-prompt fraction does not cancel in the tagged-to-inclusive ratio: the signal definition (prompt only, or prompt plus non-prompt) has to be fixed and identical in numerator and denominator. Checked on 19–20.11: the exclusive branching fractions of the signal modes in the minimum-bias MC agree with the PDG values (MC/PDG = 1.005 ± 0.006 for K⁻π⁺ and 1.003 ± 0.006 for K⁻π⁺π⁺ in the fiducial region; definition in Sec. 5.13); the difference reported on 19.11 was an artefact of the topological truth decay flag and is withdrawn (Secs. 5.13 and 6.13). Not verified: the PHOTOS settings actually used in the MC samples, and whether radiative decays are counted as signal in the truth matching.
<!-- src: charm_work/BR_frag_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md; MC decay table (20.11): charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5; ratios: charm_work/CVER_2026-11-20/out/cver.json, charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §1 -->

**Published ATLAS practice for the selection systematics and the fit model (arXiv:1512.02913, arXiv:2302.00336; read in full on 20.11).**
- *Selection efficiency.* In both papers the efficiency of the D selection is taken from simulation and its uncertainty from variations of the simulation; neither applies a scale factor of the selection measured in data. arXiv:1512.02913 (D*±, D±, D_s± at 7 TeV, Table 2): tracking ±7.7 % for D± and ±7.8 % for D*± (three tracks, dominated by the material description, strongly correlated between bins), D selection +1.6/−1.0 % (D±) and +2.8/−1.6 % (D*±) from varying the resolution of the selection variables in MC, fit ±1.3 %, model +2.3/−2.6 % and +1.0/−1.7 %. arXiv:2302.00336 (W + D(*) at 13 TeV, Sec. 9 and Table 8): samples simulated with more material (+5 % in the inner detector, +10 % in the IBL, +25 % in the pixel services) change the D(*) reconstruction efficiency by 1–4 %, most at low pT and high |η|; smearing the track impact parameters and refitting the vertex changes it by about 1.5 % below 40 GeV; secondary-vertex reconstruction in total 3.0 % (D⁺) and 2.3 % (D*). The published numbers hold for cut-based selections at pT ≥ 3.5 GeV (7 TeV) and ≥ 8 GeV (W + D); they do not cover a classifier working point at 3–5 GeV.
- *Fit model.* arXiv:1512.02913 fits a modified Gaussian with free position and width, an exponential of a second-order polynomial for the D⁺ background and a threshold function for Δm, and subtracts reflections from other decays with MC templates normalised to the measured yields. arXiv:2302.00336 uses MC templates with constrained shape parameters (peak shift up to 0.2 MeV for D⁺ and 0.05 MeV for D*, resolution smearing up to 4.0 MeV and 0.2 MeV) and a separate category for candidates built from another charm species or another decay mode of the signal meson, with a correlated variation of the branching fractions (up to 5 % of that component) and of the fragmentation fractions (up to 3 %). These are the precedents for the M8 model of Sec. 5.13: template with constrained shape parameters, peaking template for other decay modes, exp(pol2) background.
- *Definition and scope.* arXiv:1512.02913 measures pp → D X for the sum of charges, charm and beauty together, in 3.5 < pT < 100 GeV and |η| < 2.1; our definition (Sec. 6.13) is of the same kind. Neither paper measures inclusive D⁰ → Kπ without a D* tag, so the D⁰ result has no ATLAS template and D*± and D± are the quantities with a direct published reference. Jet triggers are used in arXiv:1512.02913 only above pT = 20 GeV; below, the sample comes from minimum-bias triggers (Sec. 2, roles of the runs).
<!-- src: charm_work/INDICO_1709031/ (review of 20.11) §A rows 1a–1c, 2a–2d, 3a, 7 (entries marked [Ź], read in arXiv:1512.02913 and arXiv:2302.00336), §C.1, §R.5; material from the internal ATLAS note reviewed in the same file is deliberately not used -->

### 6.10 Peak position, width and K↔π reflection at L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm (18.11)

The AFP analysis uses L_xy ≥ 0.10 mm for D⁰ and a 2–3 GeV bin; our numbers in Sec. 6.1 are for L_xy ≥ 0.25 mm and pT ≥ 3 GeV. D⁰ tagged by D* were re-extracted from the v2.0 files for both MC samples and both runs (complete file sets), so data and MC now have the same L_xy cut. D⁺ is unchanged (its threshold is 0.5 mm). Below 3 GeV only the D⁺ peak is measured (Sec. 6.12); nothing exists there for Δm, and for D⁰ only the peak seen with a tight classifier cut (19.11, Sec. 6.12; position −2.6 to +2.8 MeV from MC, width 0.97–1.00 of MC, efficiency uncalibrated).

| observable (WP70, both runs averaged; data − forced-decay MC) | pT [GeV] | ≥ 0.25 mm | ≥ 0.10 mm | width data/MC at ≥ 0.10 | effect of the threshold, data − MC |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Δm position [keV] | 3–5 | −105 ± 47 | −89 ± 49 | 1.01 ± 0.08 | −6 ± 26 |
| Δm position [keV] | 5–8 | −70 ± 36 | −71 ± 36 | 1.04 ± 0.05 | +1 ± 8 |
| Δm position [keV] | 8–30 | +19 ± 30 | +19 ± 30 | 1.08 ± 0.03 | +1 ± 2 |
| m(Kπ) position from the tag [MeV] | 3–30 | −0.11 ± 0.65 | +0.00 ± 0.68 | 1.02 ± 0.06 | +0.17 ± 0.28 |
| reflection/signal, data over minimum-bias MC (428770 / 427929) | 3–30 | 1.03 ± 0.06 / 1.17 ± 0.08 | 0.97 ± 0.07 / 1.11 ± 0.08 | – | – |

Lowering the threshold to 0.10 mm changes none of the data − MC differences beyond their errors (truth-level MC: 0 ± 3 keV). The reflection-to-signal ratio is larger in the 0.10–0.25 mm slice than above it, in data (×1.6–1.8) and in the minimum-bias MC (×2.5 ± 0.7) but hardly in the forced-decay MC (×1.29); against the forced-decay MC the ratio in data is 1.22 ± 0.05 and 1.40 ± 0.07, so the forced-decay sample should not be used for the reflection normalisation. Here the reflection ratio counts all reflected candidates in the 1.75–2.00 GeV range relative to the correctly assigned ones in the peak window; the 0.96 ± 0.04 of Sec. 5.4 counts both inside the window and is relative to the forced-decay MC at L_xy ≥ 0.25 mm, so the two numbers are not the same quantity. The pT-integrated value for 427929 depends on the pT spectrum; per bin it is 1.09 / 1.17 / 0.88 (± 0.11–0.13) at ≥ 0.10 mm.

Proposed sizes for the fit (3–30 GeV): peak position ±0.5 MeV for m(Kππ) and ±0.8 MeV for m(Kπ) (±1.3 MeV if the minimum-bias MC shape is used: the two MC samples differ by 1.3 ± 0.9 MeV); Δm as a separate parameter with ±0.2 MeV at 3–5 GeV (or a free mean), ±0.15 MeV at 5–8 GeV and ±0.10 MeV at 8–30 GeV; peak width ±10 % for Δm and m(Kπ) (427929 at 8–30 GeV has 1.24 ± 0.09, so no narrowing); reflection normalisation ±15 % with the ratio taken from minimum-bias MC at the same cut, and the minimum-bias shape as the nominal or as a variation (χ²/24 of 13–26 against 34–59 for the forced-decay sample in 428770). Caveats: the two runs differ by 2.5σ in the Δm position at 3–5 GeV (−211 ± 63 against +21 ± 69 keV at ≥ 0.25 mm); our classifier keeps only 3–4 % of the signal in the slice at WP70, CharmNet may keep more, and without a classifier the slice is not measurable below 8 GeV; no number for Δm at 2–3 GeV and for D⁰ only the tight-cut peak of Sec. 6.12 (D⁺: position −0.5 ± 1.3 MeV, width ratio 1.00 ± 0.08, Sec. 6.12, so ±0.5 MeV for m(Kππ) is not demonstrated in that bin). In the forced-decay MC the cross-validation fold is assigned by event number, which repeats between events (Sec. 7), so "out-of-fold" there means out of the event-number group.
<!-- src: charm_work/PEAK_lxy010_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md §7 -->

### 6.11 Tagged region: trigger composition, effective luminosity, expected yield in 427929, ξ_cal cut efficiency (18.11)

No D signal was fitted and no cross-section or tagged-to-inclusive ratio was extracted in the tagged region; the numbers below are event counts by trigger bits, prescales, a prediction, and the ξ_cal distribution. Selection: GRL, exactly one proton tag (A or C), `nPileupVertices == 0`, ξ_cal cut passed, no trigger requirement (as in the AFP analysis); our D⁰ candidates, pT ≥ 3 GeV.

All 75 stored HLT chains and 31 L1 items were read from the ntuples; our converted files used elsewhere in this note hold 11 of them.

| family (exclusive, in this order) | 428770, events | 428770, events with a D⁰ | 427929, events | 427929, events with a D⁰ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C` | 48.8 % | 10.9 % | 67.9 % | 68.4 % |
| other AFP `noalg` chains (`A_AND_C`, bunch-group variants) | 31.4 % | 2.9 % | 0 | 0 |
| AFP + track (`mb_sptrk_*_L1AFP`) | 9.8 % | 62.3 % | 0 | 0 |
| AFP + jet (`j20_L1AFP_A_OR_C`, `j20f_L1AFP_A_OR_C` and other jet chains on L1 AFP) | 8.9 % | 18.2 % | 0 | 0 |
| jet without AFP | 0.85 % | 5.2 % | 0.24 % | 4.5 % |
| MBTS | 0.00 % | 0.00 % | 31.9 % | 27.1 % |
| total | 1,134,024 | 76,030 | 701,183 | 10,438 |

In 427929 the `HLT_noalg_mb_L1MBTS_1` bit is set in 99.99 % of the events, so the run is one MBTS region with prescale 1. The events without any of those 11 chains are identified to 99.9 %: for events with a D⁰ it is 80 % `j20_L1AFP_A_OR_C`.

**The 428770 ntuples are a skim.** An event is present only if it passed a chain from a set S (jet chains, AFP `noalg`, EM, MU, J); `mb_sptrk_*` and MBTS chains are not in S. Only about 24 % of the `mb_sptrk_pt4_L1AFP_A_OR_C` events are therefore in the tree, those that also fired a jet chain: of the events with a D⁰, 82 % have the bit of an AFP + jet chain (78 % `j20_L1AFP_A_OR_C`). Consequences: (i) `mb_sptrk_pt4_L1AFP_A_OR_C` cannot define a measurement region in these ntuples, although its prescale is 1; (ii) the apparent inconsistency of the `noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C` prescale is explained: with the condition "also passed another chain from S" all reference chains give 50–59 (stored value 60), with no multiplicity dependence; (iii) classes of 428770 defined by MBTS or sptrk bits elsewhere in this note ("MBTS-based", "sptrk4", the minimum-bias-bit cells of Sec. 6.2.4a, the L1MBTS pool of Sec. 6.3) contain only events that also passed a chain from S and are not unbiased. Checked on the whole ntuple (Sec. 2): HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 is itself in S and makes up 92 % of the "MBTS-based" class.

Prescales from the ntuple agree with L1 × HLT from the conditions database in every luminosity block of both runs: `noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C` is L1 1 (2 in blocks 4–16) × HLT 60. Relative effective luminosity (fraction of the delivered one, weighted by events per block) and proposed disjoint regions in 428770:

| region | chain | prescale | relative luminosity | events | events with a D⁰ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | `noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C` | 60 (120 in blocks 4–16) | 0.0159 | 48.8 % | 10.9 % |
| R2 | `j20_L1AFP_A_OR_C`, not R1 | 1 (2) | 0.956 | 17.3 % | 77.0 % |
| R3 | `noalg_L1J12`, not R1, not R2 | 1 | 1.000 | 0.6 % | 4.5 % |

Together 66.8 % of the events and 92.4 % of the events with a D⁰. R1 is the only unbiased region and has 63 times less luminosity than the 29.41 nb⁻¹ used in the AFP analysis; R2 carries most candidates but is strongly biased by the jet requirement (in R1 the `j20` bit is set for 13 % of the events with a D⁰). A single luminosity cannot describe the region without a trigger requirement. The luminosity per block, missing on 18.11, was determined by us on 19.11 (next paragraph) and is to be confirmed by the authors of the ntuples. Still missing: the name of the stream or skim of the 428770 ntuples, and whether a dataset exists in which `mb_sptrk_pt4_L1AFP_A_OR_C` is complete.

**Absolute luminosity per run and trigger region (LUMI, 19.11; read-only access to the conditions database and cvmfs; in the tagged region only event counts were used).** L = Σ over blocks of L_delivered × live fraction / prescale, luminosity tag `OflLumi-Run3-003`.

| run | chain / region | prescale | L [nb⁻¹] | blocks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 427929 | `HLT_mb_mbts_L1MBTS_1` (≡ `HLT_noalg_mb_L1MBTS_1`) | 1 | **0.4737 ± 0.0105** | 119–324, 189 blocks |
| 427929 | the same, our copy of the files (files missing in 16 blocks) | 1 | 0.4648 | the same |
| 428770 | R3 `HLT_noalg_L1J12` (whole run in the GRL) | 1 | **29.41 ± 0.68** | 4–25 and 210–323, 136 blocks |
| 428770 | R1 `HLT_noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C` | 60 (120 in blocks 4–16) | **0.4687 ± 0.0104** | the same |
| 428770 | `HLT_j20_L1AFP_A_OR_C`, the chain alone | 1 (2 in blocks 4–16) | 28.12 ± 0.62 | the same |
| 428770 | R2 `j20_L1AFP_A_OR_C`, not R1 | the same × (1 − 1/60) | **27.65 ± 0.61** | the same |

- Inputs: delivered luminosity per block from the official iLumiCalc files on cvmfs for the GRL `PHYS_StandardGRL_All_Good_25ns_AFP_SIT` (our own iLumiCalc run on the analysis GRL gives the same sum); live fraction from the L1 counters of the conditions database (`L1_RD0_FILLED`): 0.99625 in 427929 and 0.9665 in 428770; blocks = `GRL_MinBiasAFP_lowmu2022.xml` of the base code ∩ blocks present in our files. Prescales as in the table above.
- **Origin of 29.41 nb⁻¹.** The number is `lumi_nb: 29.41` in `config/trex/minbias_trex_example.yaml` of the base code, described there as a placeholder ("recorded GRL luminosity without trigger/prescale"). We reproduce it: 30.4297 × 0.96650 = 29.4105 nb⁻¹. It is the recorded luminosity of run 428770 in the GRL without prescales, a temporary value valid only for chains with prescale 1 (`noalg_L1J12`); it does not apply to run 427929 or to R1. The relative luminosity 0.956 quoted for R2 in the table above is that of the chain alone (28.12 nb⁻¹); with R1 excluded it is 27.65 nb⁻¹.
- **Assumptions.** Calibration uncertainty 2.2 % (the preliminary value for 2022 pp physics data at high μ, ATL-DAPR-PUB-2023-001; no separate number for the low-μ runs was found). The luminosity tags 001 / 002 / 003 give 0.461 / 0.507 / 0.4755 nb⁻¹ for the same blocks of 427929 and 32.27 / 32.71 / 30.66 nb⁻¹ for 428770, so ±7 % is the conservative choice until the luminosity group confirms the tag. Live fraction: ±0.3 % (427929), ±0.8 % (428770).
- **Completeness of our files.** 427929: events in the tree over L1-accepted MBTS_1 events is 0.828, a flat 0.84 in 173 of the 189 blocks and lower in 16. We read the plateau as an event-level requirement of the ntuple production (a primary vertex), i.e. an efficiency, and the dips as missing files: file completeness inside the GRL 0.981, L = 0.4648 nb⁻¹. If the plateau were a loss of files, L would be 0.392 nb⁻¹. The 125 missing input files (of 583) lie mostly outside the GRL blocks. Settled on 19.11 (night): `nPrimaryVertices` = 1 in 100 % of the 23.84 M MBTS events of the GRL; the shares of the files in the events of one block are multiples of 1/6, so a lost file removes at least 1/6 of a block and cannot produce a flat 0.84 in 173 blocks; and in the same blocks the ratio rises with the hardness of the trigger (0.840 / 0.872 / 0.945 for L1_MBTS_1 / L1_MBTS_2 / L1_J12). The plateau is the primary-vertex requirement of the production, L = 0.4648 ± 0.0103 nb⁻¹ stands, and the 0.392 nb⁻¹ variant is closed (the file list of the container itself was not checked). 428770: the same ratio for `noalg_L1J12` is 0.966 and flat, file completeness 0.998. For R1 the ratio is 0.44: 56 % of the events of this chain are not in the tree, which is an event-selection efficiency to be kept under control in a cross-section for R1.
- **Cross-check with the MBTS counter (427929).** The visible cross-section of L1_MBTS_1 from our luminosity is 60.8 mb against an expected 69–72 mb, so a luminosity from the MBTS counter would be 0.40–0.42 nb⁻¹, **12–16 % lower**. Either the MBTS_1 efficiency is lower than in 2015 or the calibration of the tag is too high for this run; not resolved on 19.11. Update (19.11, night): the check is not an indication of the luminosity. With the random-trigger sample the efficiency of L1_MBTS_1 for events with a primary vertex is 99.45 ± 0.45 %, so the deficit concerns events without a vertex. For events with a D candidate the inefficiency of L1_MBTS_1 is at most 0.03 % (below 0.08 % at 95 % CL), and event selection × trigger = 1.000 +0/−0.002. What stays open is the absolute scale of the luminosity tag for low-μ runs. The check does not work in 428770 (out-of-time signals in bunch trains).
- R3 as defined above (not R1, not R2) is not uniform in the blocks, because the L1_AFP prescale is 2 in blocks 4–16; without these blocks L = 26.83 nb⁻¹. The region without a trigger requirement still has no single luminosity (spread 63 times).
<!-- src: charm_work/LUMI_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md §0–§5; charm_work/LUMI_2026-11-19/lumi.json; plateau, MBTS efficiency (19.11 night): charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/WYNIKI.md §8.1, §8.2 -->

**Run 428770 by trigger region (REG428, 20.11; post hoc; trigger bits only).** The yields at WP70 of the tree H were refitted in the disjoint regions defined above, with the trigger bits taken per event from the ntuples (the `HLT_j20_L1AFP_A_OR_C` bit is not in our converted files). R3 (`HLT_noalg_L1J12`, not R1, not R2) carries 70–87 % of the yield of the run, the events of none of the three chains 12–24 % (no luminosity can be assigned to them), R2 2–7 % and R1 below 1 %. The D⁰ template of Sec. 5.13 is rejected at 8–30 GeV in the whole run and in R3 (p = 1·10⁻⁷ and 3·10⁻⁴), so the old model is used in that bin. These fits used the template normalisation κ × 1.364, since withdrawn (scale caveat in Secs. 5.11 (ii) and 5.13: −0.5 % / +1.4 to +1.8 % / +2.6 to +3.1 % on the WP70 yield of run 427929, −1.0 % and ±0.2 % in run 428770 at 3–5 and 5–8 GeV); the shares of the regions and ε_J12 below are ratios of yields fitted with the same model, with errors of 14–34 % (16–71 % per species), and were not refitted. The D*-tag scale factor of the tree at WP70 and 8–30 GeV is 0.990 ± 0.050 in R3, 0.90 ± 0.15 in R2 and 0.830 ± 0.076 in the remaining events (χ²/ndf = 3.2/2, p = 0.20); at 5–8 GeV 0.87 ± 0.15, 0.60 ± 0.21 and 0.89 ± 0.17 (p = 0.50); it is undetermined at 3–5 GeV and in R1.

A cross-section from R2 or R3 needs the efficiency of the jet trigger for events with a D. The orthogonal-trigger method with R1 does not work: R1 holds 20 to 1100 candidates per cell at WP70, 2 to 21 of them with the L1_J12 bit, and no measurable signal among those; it would need at least 30 to 150 times more events. The efficiency was therefore taken from the MBTS-triggered run 427929, where the same chain runs unprescaled: the fraction of the D signal (peak fit at WP70) in events with the `HLT_noalg_L1J12` bit is **ε_J12(D) = 2.5 ± 0.8 % at 3–5 GeV, 2.7 ± 0.9 % at 5–8 GeV and 12.0 ± 1.7 % at 8–30 GeV** (D⁰ and D⁺ combined; 16–71 % relative per species), rising to 25–38 % above 12 GeV. The same fraction for all candidates, which are mostly background, is about twice as large. With this efficiency a cross-section can be formed in R3 (L = 29.41 nb⁻¹), with an error of 17–72 % per cell and at most 20 % only for D⁺ at 8–30 GeV; the values are not quoted here. It is not an independent measurement: the ratio of the two cross-sections equals the ratio of the rates of D with the L1_J12 bit per unit luminosity in the two runs, 1.07 ± 0.15 for pT ≥ 5 GeV (four cells, p = 0.93), while the rate of all L1_J12 events per unit luminosity is 16 % higher in 428770. The efficiency cannot be transferred between the runs to better than that, and the test cannot tell 1.00 from 1.16.

**Verdict: relative quantities yes, absolute ones no.** No region of run 428770 gives a second absolute point. R1 measures the cross-section times the probability of the L1_AFP bit for an event with a D (0.41 ± 0.22 % from the trigger bit alone); R2 needs that probability and a jet-trigger efficiency that cannot be measured (65–120 % error); R3 is a consistency check of 15–30 % at pT ≥ 8 GeV. The run remains useful for relative quantities inside one trigger class (shapes, the D*-tag scale factor, tests of the fit model), with 3 to 10 times the statistics of run 427929. A sample of 1 nb⁻¹ of minimum-bias data would give the L1_J12 efficiency to 20 % for D⁰ at 8–30 GeV, 6 nb⁻¹ at 5–8 GeV.
<!-- src: charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0–§5; charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/tabele/tabele.md (T0–T5); charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/out/summary.json; charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/AKAPIT_DO_NOTY.md (the cross-section values of that paragraph are deliberately not copied); probability of the L1_AFP bit: charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/WYNIKI.md §8.4 -->

**Expected number of tagged D (a prediction; recomputed on 20.11 after the closure of the generator variation).** Both scenarios have the form N_tag = Σ over pT bins of N_incl × fraction, with N_incl the inclusive yield fitted in data (our copy of run 427929, classifier H, pT 3–30 GeV; D⁰ with fit model M8 and the peaking component at its MC normalisation, 4077 / 1548 / 705 at WP70, 0.6 % above the final yields of Sec. 6.13; D⁺ with the fit model of the working-point scan, 3547 / 1651 / 687 at WP70, 3.5 % above the final model of Sec. 5.14; both differences are far inside the ranges). **The nominal working point is WP70** (decision of 20.11), the working point of the inclusive results; WP80 is given in the table. In the sideband scenario the fraction is the share of sideband candidates in the tagged region (0.21–0.47 % per species and pT bin) times (1 − f_acc), with an assigned factor 0.5–2. In the generator scenario it is R(pT) × A·ε_AFP × ε_ξcal, with R from the official A3 configuration (D⁰: 0.375 / 0.324 / 0.268 % at 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV) and the envelope of the diffraction-model variation of the next paragraph (D⁰ × 0.40 to × 3.98, D⁺ × 0.38 to × 3.79, D* × 0.39 to × 3.98). The detector factor is the measured A·ε_AFP = 0.92 ± 0.05 of the acceptance study of 20.11 (geometric acceptance 0.97 ± 0.03 in 0.030 ≤ ξ ≤ 0.100, reconstruction efficiency 0.95 ± 0.02, which includes the requirement of exactly one proton; alignment and optics ± 3.4 %), with ε_ξcal = 1.00 in run 427929 (last paragraph of this section); the earlier 0.55 (0.35–0.80) with ε_ξcal = 0.99 was an assumption and is superseded, which raises the generator scenario by a factor 1.69. The pile-up-vertex veto is not part of the fraction: it cancels in N_tag/N_incl when applied to both, and the yields below refer to N_incl without the veto (× 0.985 with it). Neither a Monte Carlo efficiency nor a branching fraction enters, so the correction of the efficiency denominator of Sec. 6.13 (0.734 for D⁰, 0.939 for D⁺) does not apply.

| species | run, region | working point | N_incl | sideband scenario (× 0.5–2) | generator scenario, measured factor 0.92 × 1.00 (model envelope) | generator scenario, superseded factor 0.55 × 0.99 | accidental tags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D⁰ | 427929, MBTS | **WP70** | 6330 ± 496 | **23.5 (12–47)** | **20.4 (8.2–81)** | 12.1 (4.8–48) | 0.2 |
| D⁺ | 427929, MBTS | **WP70** | 5885 ± 353 | **14.2 (7.1–28)** | **18.6 (7.1–71)** | 11.0 (4.2–42) | 0.12 |
| D* | 427929, MBTS | (WP80, D* tag) | 885 ± 43 | **2.1 (1.0–4)** | **2.8 (1.1–11)** | 1.7 (0.6–7) | 0.02 |
| D⁰ | 427929, MBTS | WP80 | 8041 ± 753 | 30 (15–60) | 26 (10–104) | 15.5 (6.2–61) | 0.3 |
| D⁺ | 427929, MBTS | WP80 | 6583 ± 516 | 16 (8–32) | 21 (8–79) | 12.3 (4.7–47) | 0.14 |
| D⁰ | 428770, R1 | WP70 | – | 15 (7–30) | 13 (5–52) | 7.7 (3.1–31) | 0.5–0.6 |
| D⁺ | 428770, R1 | WP70 | – | 9 (5–18) | 12 (5–45) | 7.0 (2.7–27) | 0.4–0.5 |
| D* | 428770, R1 | (WP80, D* tag) | – | 1.3 (0.7–3) | 1.8 (0.7–7) | 1.1 (0.4–4) | 0.05–0.07 |

The corresponding tagged-to-inclusive fractions, which do not depend on the working point, are 0.37 % (D⁰), 0.24 % (D⁺) and 0.23 % (D*) in the sideband scenario and 0.32 % (0.13–1.28 %), 0.32 % (0.12–1.20 %) and 0.32 % (0.12–1.28 %) in the generator scenario (0.19 % with the superseded factor). History: the numbers of 18–19.11 at WP80 were D⁰ 35 (18–71) from the sidebands or 18.6 (7.4–74) from the model, D⁺ 18 or 31, D* 2 or 4; with respect to them the inclusive yields are lower (D⁰ 9596 → 8041 with model M8, D⁺ 7599 → 6583), the model values of D⁺ and D* use R = 0.384 % with its pT dependence instead of 0.75 %, and the detector factor is the measured one. The values entered in the audit of 20.11 (D⁰ 31 (12–123) at WP80, with its two re-normalisations 19 (8–77) and 27 (11–105); D⁺ 24 (9–92)) were those earlier predictions multiplied by 0.92/0.55 and by ratios of yields; the present values are computed anew per pT bin from the M8 yields and replace them. The ranges are a spread of assumptions and models, not confidence intervals; they do not include the uncertainty of N_incl (about 20 % for D⁰ from the fit closure, 6–8 % for D⁺) or a possible difference of the classifier efficiency between diffractive and non-diffractive events. For region R1 of run 428770 (`HLT_noalg_L1AFP_A_OR_C`; luminosity in the table above) the prediction is that of 427929 times 0.635 (range 0.60–0.77): luminosity ratio 1.008, L1 AFP efficiency for an offline tag 0.684 (0.91 on side A, 0.46 on side C), ratio of the pile-up-veto efficiencies 0.955, of the ξ_cal-cut efficiencies 0.974 and of the AFP efficiencies 0.989; there is no inclusive yield in R1, because the trigger requires a proton. For R2 (27.65 nb⁻¹, jet trigger on L1 AFP) no model number is given: the efficiency of the jet requirement for diffractive events with a D is not known, and R falls with pT; the sideband anchor on the whole run gives of the order of 110 D⁰ and 60 D⁺ (× 0.5–2) with 5–9 % accidental tags. A differential measurement in 427929 is not feasible; one pT-integrated point with an uncertainty of roughly 30–50 % is. Run 428770 has 6–7 times more tagged events with a candidate, but with a trigger bias and an accidental fraction of 5–6 %. These are predictions; no yield measured in events with a proton is quoted in this note.
<!-- src: charm_work/SD_var_2026-11-19/RAPORT_domkniecie.md §2–§5; charm_work/SD_var_2026-11-19/out/pred_tables.md §1–§3, out/pred.json; charm_work/AFP_akceptancja_2026-11-20/PLAN.md §0, §5; history: charm_work/TAGREG_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md §3, charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §2 -->

**Model uncertainty of the generator scenario (SD_var, 19.11; particle level, Pythia 8.317, no ATLAS data).** R = σ(D + proton with 0.030 ≤ ξ ≤ 0.100 on either side) / σ(D), D with 2 ≤ pT < 30 GeV and |η| < 2.2. The nominal is now the official ATLAS A3 configuration (Donnachie–Landshoff flux with ε = 0.07, `SigmaDiffractive:mode = 0`): **R(D⁰) = 0.384 ± 0.005 %** (MC statistics) against the 0.75 % used before, which was the default Schuler–Sjöstrand shape. The envelope of the pre-defined set of variations is **0.154–1.53 %** (× 0.40 to × 3.98; × 3.97 until the missing parts were added on 20.11, see the end of this paragraph): upwards it is set by the ABMST model (1.217 %), downwards by the Pomeron–proton reference cross-section (25 mb) and the σ_SD normalisation. It is a spread of models, not a confidence interval. After the detector the predicted tagged-to-inclusive ratio is 0.384 % × 0.92 × 0.99 = **0.35 % (0.14–1.39 %)** (audit of 20.11, with the AFP acceptance and efficiency 0.92 ± 0.05 of the previous paragraph; as computed on 19.11 with the superseded 0.55: 0.21 % (0.08–0.83 %); before 19.11: 0.41 % (0.07–1.6 %)); the ± 0.05 of the AFP factor is 5 % and is not part of the envelope. This ratio refers to 2–30 GeV; for the yields of the table above R is weighted with the measured inclusive yields per pT bin in 3–30 GeV and ε_ξcal = 1.00 is used, which gives 0.32 % (0.13–1.28 %) for D⁰. The predicted D⁰ yield in 427929 in the generator scenario is **20.4 (8.2–81)** at WP70; the sideband scenario, 23.5 (12–47), does not depend on the generator and lies close to the nominal of the model range. The species ratio is insensitive to the model: R(D⁺)/R(D⁰) = 0.98–1.05 in all 44 scenarios. Missing parts (closed on 20.11): the variation with QCD colour reconnection had 9 of 10 parts for the diffractive sample and 16 of 20 for the non-diffractive one; the missing parts were produced with new seeds (now 10 of 10 and 22), and the envelope is unchanged (R(D⁰) = 0.384 ± 0.005 %, 0.154–1.53 %; 0.512 % before and after for the reconnection scenario). The failed jobs were caused by two defects of Pythia 8.317 in the configuration with QCD colour reconnection (`ColourReconnection:mode = 1`, `BeamRemnants:remnantMode = 1`), a segmentation fault in `ColourReconnection::getJunctionIndices` reproducible for one seed (about one event in 10⁷) and a non-terminating loop in `JunctionSplitting::splitJunGluons` (four seeds in 2.6·10⁷ non-diffractive events; one stack sample of one seed), and not by memory or the batch nodes; the lost events are too few to bias R. Not included: Reggeon exchange, another generator, the AFP acceptance in t and φ.
<!-- src: charm_work/SD_var_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md §1, §2, §4, §6; charm_work/SD_var_2026-11-19/out/tables.md §1 (rows A3off, ABMST, sigPp25), §2; AFP factor: charm_work/AFP_akceptancja_2026-11-20/PLAN.md, charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §2; closure and causes of the failed jobs: charm_work/SD_var_2026-11-19/RAPORT_domkniecie.md §1; yields: out/pred_tables.md §1 -->

Efficiency of the ξ_cal cut for true tags, from the ξ_cal distribution of tagged events with the accidental component subtracted using the mixing shape: 1.000 +0/−0.005 in 427929 and 0.99 +0.01/−0.03 in 428770 (to be read as the interval 0.96–1.00; the method fails at high multiplicity). Raw pass fractions are 96.6 % and 77.1 %; the difference is the accidental component. Assumptions: the accidental shape at small ξ_cal, and the transfer from tagged events with a background candidate to D signal. Not checked: CharmNet selection, 2–3 GeV, ξ_cal resolution in MC.
<!-- src: charm_work/TAGREG_2026-11-18/v2/RAPORT.md (trigger part); -->
<!-- src: charm_work/TAGREG_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md -->

### 6.12 The 2–3 GeV bin: D⁺ peak measured; D⁰ visible only with a tight classifier cut (18–19.11)

The v2.0 ntuples of the 2–3 GeV production were downloaded (428770: 96 of 97 files, one file unavailable; 427929: 450 files; forced-decay MC: 15 files; no minimum-bias MC exists for this bin). Our classifier H, trained for pT ≥ 3 GeV, is applied outside its training range; its MC signal efficiency at the working-point thresholds of 3–5 GeV drops to 0.87 / 0.77 / 0.62 / 0.50 / 0.30 (WP95 to WP50). The working point was chosen on MC signal and data sidebands with the signal window blinded: score ≥ 0.992, ε_S^MC = 0.205. Criteria were fixed before the data were opened (peak measured if N_S/σ ≥ 5).

| run | N_S | N_S/σ | S/B (±45 MeV) | position, data − MC [MeV] | width, data/MC | N/ε_MC relative to 3–5 GeV |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 427929 | 705 ± 53 | 13.2 | 0.48 | −0.9 ± 1.4 | 0.99 ± 0.09 | 0.79 ± 0.08 |
| 428770 | 626 ± 74 | 8.4 | 0.19 | +0.9 ± 2.6 | 1.01 ± 0.21 | 0.52 ± 0.07 |

Background exp(pol3); pol2 and pol4 move N_S by −3 % and 0 % (427929), +7 % and −3 % (428770). N/ε_MC is stable against the neighbouring working points within 2σ. Run average: position −0.5 ± 1.3 MeV, width ratio 1.00 ± 0.08; the data do not support a ±0.2 MeV position uncertainty in this bin, ±10 % on the width is adequate. S/B for D⁺ falls steeply with looser cuts: 0.077 / 0.017 at ε ≈ 0.5 and 0.019 / 0.008 at ε ≈ 0.7 (427929 / 428770); the significance is maximal at ε ≈ 0.2–0.3, and at loose cuts a pol2 background shifts the yield by 12–40 %.

Not measurable in this bin: inclusive D⁰ without a classifier cut (N_S/σ ≤ 3.1; the D⁰ test of 18.11 was made without the classifier only, and with a tight cut the peak is seen, last paragraph) and D* (no stable peak; the soft pion is mostly below threshold), hence no tag-based classifier efficiency, no reflection ratio, and no veto efficiency on signal. The cut scan gives only "no dependence of the scale factor on the working point larger than about 20 %" in 427929. Retraining the classifier below 3 GeV gives nothing significant (background at fixed ε_S lower by 3–10 % at best; maximum significance 11.6 against 11.5): the loss of efficiency at a fixed threshold is a shift of the score distribution, not a loss of separation. Caveats: no injection test with full background was possible (closure on forced-decay MC: 0.99 ± 0.02); significance is N/σ, not a likelihood ratio; the ratio to 3–5 GeV is an order-of-magnitude statement (different file sets).
<!-- src: charm_work/D23_plan_2026-11-18/ANALIZA/RAPORT.md -->

**D⁰ at 2–3 GeV with a tight classifier cut (19.11, stage 4; independently re-checked).** With a tight classifier cut (ε_S^MC = 0.10–0.20, working point chosen on sidebands with the window blinded) an inclusive D⁰→Kπ peak is seen at 2–3 GeV in both runs and with two independently trained classifiers (427929: N_S = 448 ± 54 for the tree and 860 ± 95 for the network; 428770: 501 ± 66 and 391 ± 55; likelihood-ratio significance 6.8–9.1 with the nominal exp(pol2) background, falling to 3.2–5.0 with exp(pol4) on the 1.75–2.00 GeV range). Fitted with free shape, the position (−2.6 to +2.8 MeV, ±2.2) and width (0.97–1.00 ± 0.05 of MC) agree with simulation, the K↔π reflection is included with its normalisation fixed from MC, and N/ε_MC is stable over disjoint score slices (4.0–4.9 × 10³). Background sculpting as the sole origin is excluded (truth-matched minimum-bias background at 3–5 GeV after the same cut: fake/background = 0.09 ± 0.09 against the required 0.68; the peak does not scale with the background between runs), but a non-signal contribution of up to about 20–30 % of the yield cannot be ruled out: no background MC exists for this bin, the classifier efficiency is uncalibrated outside its training range, and N/ε_MC is 1.3–1.5 times the value expected from the D⁺ yield. The result establishes visibility of the peak, not a cross-section; without a classifier the peak remains unmeasurable (N_S/σ ≤ 3.1), which is consistent with this yield. This qualifies the statement above that D⁰ is not visible in this bin, which holds only without a classifier.
<!-- src: charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus4/WERDYKT_etap4.md §5; charm_work/D23_D0_weryfikacja_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md -->

**Recomputed with the D⁰ fit model M8 (20.11, post hoc).** No minimum-bias MC exists for this bin, so the template of the other-decay-mode and charm-mismatch components of M8 does not exist here; the nominal fit for this bin is M8 without that component (Gaussian constraint on the signal shape, range 1.70–2.05 GeV, exp(pol2) background), and the fit with the 3–5 GeV template is quoted as a variant only. The variant used the template normalisation κ × 1.364, since withdrawn (scale caveat in Secs. 5.11 (ii) and 5.13); without the scale the template normalisation is lower by the factor 1/1.364, so the 2–5 % rise of the variant is an upper value (not refitted); the nominal column has no template and is not affected.

| run | classifier, working point | N_S, stage-4 model | N_S, M8 without template | N_S, variant with the 3–5 GeV template | Z_LR: stage 4 → M8 | N/ε_MC relative to D⁺: stage 4 → M8 (variant) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 427929 | tree, ε = 0.10 | 448 ± 54 | 452 ± 38 | 472 ± 41 | 8.6 → 12.1 | 1.31 ± 0.16 → 1.32 ± 0.14 (1.38 ± 0.15) |
| 427929 | network, ε = 0.20 | 860 ± 95 | 867 ± 84 | 886 ± 88 | 9.1 → 11.8 | 1.23 ± 0.15 → 1.29 ± 0.14 (1.32 ± 0.15) |
| 428770 | tree, ε = 0.10 | 501 ± 66 | 459 ± 51 | 482 ± 54 | 7.8 → 9.4 | 1.53 ± 0.26 → 1.58 ± 0.24 (1.63 ± 0.25) |
| 428770 | network, ε = 0.10 | 391 ± 55 | 438 ± 54 | 449 ± 50 | 6.8 → 10.0 | 1.28 ± 0.22 → 1.33 ± 0.21 (1.39 ± 0.21) |

The yields move by at most 0.8σ and their errors fall by 13–22 %. The larger likelihood-ratio significance comes from the wider fit range, which fixes the background better, and not from the signal model; the dependence on the background order remains (with exp(pol4) on the same wide range and the stage-4 signal shape: 7.9 / 8.1 / 7.0 / 6.4). The data do not reject the model in any of the four fits (p(χ²) = 0.06–0.78; 0.06 for the tree in 427929 with every model, the old one included; the variant with the template does not converge formally for that point, with a stable yield). **The excess of N/ε_MC over the value expected from the D⁺ yield does not decrease:** the ratio to D⁺, for which 0.82–1.03 is expected, is 1.29–1.58 with M8 and 1.32–1.63 with the 3–5 GeV template, against 1.23–1.53 before. Adding the template raises the yield by 2–5 %, because that component lies mostly below the peak and is otherwise absorbed by the background, not by the signal; a missing peaking component in the fit model is therefore not the explanation of the excess. The statements of the previous paragraph stand: the peak is visible, the yield is not a cross-section, and a non-signal contribution of 20–30 % is not excluded.
<!-- src (20.11, M8 recomputation): charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §2; charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/tabele/d23_m8.md (D1, D3); charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/NOTA_teksty_EN.md (E) -->

**Inverted-cut background template (KONSTR, 20.11; post hoc; negative result).** Using the mass distribution of candidates failing the classifier cut, multiplied by exp(c₁m), as the background model does not remove the dependence of the significance on the background model. With a first-order transfer the local significance is 11–15, but simulated background at 3–5 GeV then yields a negative spurious signal of 2–22 % of the background under the peak (10 of 16 tests fail), and the sidebands of run 427929 (tree) are not described (p = 0.004–0.011). A second-order transfer reproduces the exp(pol2) results. The spread of Z over the constructed variants (4.9–6.8) is not smaller than over the functional ones (5.8–7.0). The smallest Z over all constructed variants is 6.8–9.1, so a peak is present in every variant. The functional background model is kept.
<!-- src: charm_work/KONSTR_2026-11-20/NOTA_tekst_EN.md (part for Sec. 6.12); charm_work/KONSTR_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §C; charm_work/KONSTR_2026-11-20/tabele/{C_prep,C_wyniki}.md -->

### 6.13 Inclusive cross-sections of D⁰ and D⁺ and the D⁺/D⁰ ratio (run 427929; preliminary)

**Status: preliminary and post hoc.** Single D mesons from the inclusive mass peaks, no proton tag, run 427929. The WP70 yields and the level of the comparison with CMS were known before the definitions of this section were written down; nothing was tuned to the comparisons. This section supersedes the version of 19.11, whose cross-sections were too high by 1/0.734 (D⁰) and 1/0.939 (D⁺) because of the efficiency denominator (see "Efficiency denominator" below). History: the earlier values σ(D⁰) = 376 and 132 µb (5–8 and 8–30 GeV) are withdrawn, because the efficiency denominator counted true D mesons by the topological decay flag; the text of 19.11 and its correction notice of 20.11 are kept in the backup of the note, not here.
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/DEFINICJA.md (written before the fits); charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0; charm_work/CVER_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0 -->

**Fiducial definition.** D⁰ + anti-D⁰ and D⁺ + D⁻ (sums), meson at generator level; prompt and non-prompt together, D⁰ from D*⁺ included; pT(D) in 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV and |η(D)| < 2.2 (pseudorapidity). Events: `HLT_mb_mbts_L1MBTS_1` and the GRL; L = 0.4648 ± 0.0103 nb⁻¹ (Sec. 6.11; the 2.2 % calibration uncertainty is an assumption).
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/DEFINICJA.md §1; charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/WYNIKI.md §8.1; charm_work/LUMI_2026-11-19/lumi.json -->

**Formula.** σ = N / (C × SF × B × L).
- N: yield of the peak fit at WP70 of the tree H. D⁰: model M8 (Sec. 5.13) with the template of the peaking components (other decay modes and other charm hadrons) taken directly from the tree H on minimum-bias MC and normalised to the signal as in the MC (scale r = 1); D⁺: the model A2 of Sec. 5.14 (constrained signal shape, tied template of the other charm decays), which replaces the old model M0 since the version v2 of 20.11; its closure criterion on MC is not met at 5–8 GeV, and the closure is carried as an uncertainty without correction, as for D⁰. The statistical error of both yields is the larger of the event bootstrap and 1.3 × HESSE (Secs. 5.13 and 5.14).
- C: acceptance × efficiency from the minimum-bias MC without weights. Numerator: truth-matched signal candidates at WP70 in the reconstructed-pT bin. Denominator: true D mesons decaying **exactly** to K⁻π⁺ or K⁻π⁺π⁺ (with radiated photons), in the true-pT bin and |η| < 2.2. C = 22.3 / 30.0 / 39.6 % (D⁰) and 16.1 / 23.1 / 30.8 % (D⁺).
- B: 3.936 ± 0.030 % (D⁰→K⁻π⁺; the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed mode is part of the peaking template, not of the signal) and 9.31 ± 0.14 % (D⁺).
- SF: data/MC of the classifier efficiency at WP70. D⁰ at 5–8 and 8–30 GeV: D* tag in run 427929, 1.005 ± 0.135 and 0.965 ± 0.109 (statistical error of the pass/fail fit, 0.126 and 0.097, and the half-range of the Δm estimators, Sec. 6.8). D⁰ at 3–5 GeV: 0.967 ± 0.033; D⁺: 0.949 ± 0.080 / 0.993 ± 0.052 / 0.990 ± 0.051 (at 3–5 GeV the error of the combination, 0.048, is replaced by 0.08, Sec. 6.8). These last four are dominated (81–97 % of the weight) by a transfer of the D⁰ scale factor measured above 5 GeV, which is valid if the data/MC difference is of the resolution or dE/dx type; a direct measurement in the target bins has ± 0.12–0.18.
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/DEFINICJA.md §1, §3, §4; charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/out/truth_ngen.json and CVER RAPORT.md §2 (denominators agree exactly); charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0 and out/final.json; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus4/dnet4_results.json; v2 (D⁺ model, SF errors): charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/ZMIANY.md, charm_work/FITBIAS_Dplus_2026-11-20/out/dplus_yields.json, charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §5, §6 -->

**Efficiency denominator (correction of 20.11).** The truth decay flag of the ntuples is topological: it also marks decays with an unseen neutral (K⁰, η, …). Only 73.4 ± 0.2 % (D⁰) and 93.9 ± 0.1 % (D⁺) of the flagged decays are the exclusive mode, while the numerator and the fitted yield are 100 % exclusive. The exclusive branching fractions in the MC agree with the PDG values used in the formula: MC/PDG = 1.005 ± 0.006 (D⁰, 3.955 % against 3.936 %) and 1.003 ± 0.006 (D⁺, 9.336 % against 9.31 %), in the fiducial region, with radiative decays and without the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed mode, as in the denominator of C (with the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed mode in the PDG value, 3.951 %, the D⁰ ratio is 1.001; definition in Sec. 5.13). The earlier statement that the MC branching fractions differ from the PDG (× 1.364, × 1.068) is withdrawn, and with it the scaling of the peaking template by 1.364 in Sec. 5.13.
<!-- src: charm_work/CVER_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0, §2, §4; charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5 -->

**D⁰ yields.** With the template taken directly from the tree H and r = 1 the yields are 4059 ± 468 / 1536 ± 161 / 700 ± 70 (3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV), against 4105 / 1530 / 681 in Sec. 5.13 (−1.1 / +0.4 / +2.8 %). Removing the scale 1.364 alone changes them by −0.5 / +1.7 / +3.1 %; replacing the interpolated template by the direct one, by −0.5 / −0.8 / −0.7 %. A larger effect of the scale at 5–8 GeV (+6 %) seen in a cross-check on a slightly different candidate sample was a jump of the fit between two minima in the width scale of the signal, not an effect of the normalisation. The same two minima exist in the nominal fit: 1468 instead of 1536 at 5–8 GeV and 4245 instead of 4059 at 3–5 GeV, with −2 ln L differing by 0.06 and 0.14; the difference is carried as a systematic uncertainty (4.4 % and 4.6 %; added after the fits).
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §1; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/out/{kappa_A,kappa_B,kappa_altmin}.json; charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/tabele/m8.md -->

**Result, run 427929:**

| species | pT [GeV] | N | σ [µb] ± stat ± syst ± lumi | total | data / Pythia 8 A3 | prompt data / FONLL (central; band) | data / CMS 13 TeV |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D⁰ | 3–5 | 4059 ± 468 | **1028 ± 119 ± 274 ± 23** | 29 % | 0.84 ± 0.24 | 1.50; inside | – |
| D⁰ | 5–8 | 1536 ± 161 | **278 ± 29 ± 49 ± 6** | 21 % | 0.58 ± 0.12 | 1.13; inside | 0.53 ± 0.13 |
| D⁰ | 8–30 | 700 ± 70 | **100 ± 10 ± 21 ± 2** | 23 % | 0.63 ± 0.15 | 1.11; inside | 0.75 ± 0.20 |
| D⁺ | 3–5 | 3404 ± 292 | **515 ± 44 ± 82 ± 11** | 18 % | 0.80 ± 0.15 | 1.91; 0.3σ above | – |
| D⁺ | 5–8 | 1630 ± 102 | **165 ± 10 ± 25 ± 4** | 17 % | 0.65 ± 0.11 | 1.67; 0.4σ above | 0.87 ± 0.21 |
| D⁺ | 8–30 | 651 ± 53 | **49.2 ± 4.0 ± 6.9 ± 1.1** | 16 % | 0.58 ± 0.09 | 1.36; inside | 0.81 ± 0.18 |
<!-- src (v2): charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/tabele/final.md F1, F4, F6 -->

Relative systematic uncertainties (D⁰ 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30; D⁺ 3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30; in %): fit model 7.5 / 1.5 / 12.8; 4.2 / 1.3 / 5.5. Fit bias (closure on MC, |b| ⊕ σ_b, no correction applied, same definition for both species) 24.0 / 8.0 / 10.6; 9.0 / 11.1 / 7.3. Peaking template, shape and normalisation (D⁰) 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.8. Second minimum (D⁰) 4.6 / 4.4 / 0. Scale factor 3.4 / 13.4 / 11.3; 8.4 / 5.2 / 5.2. Track reconstruction, 2.6 % per track, correlated between tracks, 5.2 (D⁰) and 7.8 (D⁺). Impact-parameter resolution and material 1.5. Three-track vertex (D⁺) 3.0. Generator model (pT and η shape, multiplicity) 2.1–2.5. MC statistics 1.5–2.9. Branching fraction 0.8 and 1.5. Non-prompt fraction 0.4–3.1. Event selection and trigger 0.2. Reflection ≤ 0.1. Total 26.7 / 17.5 / 21.1 and 16.0 / 15.4 / 14.1; luminosity 2.2 (0.0103/0.4648; the first version of 20.11 used 2.24 %, which included 0.3 % for the completeness of the files). The statistical uncertainties are 11.5 / 10.5 / 10.1 and 8.6 / 6.2 / 8.1. The rows for tracking, impact-parameter resolution, vertex and multiplicity are taken from ATLAS publications (arXiv:1512.02913, arXiv:2302.00336), not measured on these data. An uncertainty for dE/dx is not listed separately because the scale factor measured on data contains it.
<!-- src (v2): charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/tabele/final.md F2; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/ZMIANY.md; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §4; charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §C -->

Cross-checks of the scale factor and of the level. A cut-based selection without the classifier gives σ(D⁰) = 286 ± 28 and 107 ± 11 µb and σ(D⁺) = 158 ± 11 and 57.8 ± 3.9 µb (5–8 and 8–30 GeV, statistical errors), i.e. a classifier scale factor of 0.95 ± 0.06 ± 0.08 (D⁰) and 0.97 ± 0.05 ± 0.05 (D⁺), independent of the values used above. With SF = 1 ± 0.10 for D⁺ (the earlier scenario) σ(D⁺) = 488 / 163 / 48.8 µb. History of the D⁺ values: with the old fit model M0 and ± 0.05 at 3–5 GeV (first version of 20.11) they were 536 ± 42 ± 73 ± 12, 167 ± 8 ± 28 ± 4 and 52.0 ± 4.5 ± 7.4 ± 1.2 µb, from yields of 3547 ± 276, 1651 ± 76 and 687 ± 59.
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §B; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/tabele/final.md F1 (variants); first version: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/tabele/final.md F1 -->

**Ratios** (the luminosity and the event selection cancel; of the tracking uncertainty one track remains; the scale factors cancel only partly):

| pT [GeV] | D⁺/D⁰ ± stat ± syst | Pythia 8 A3 | ALICE 13 TeV, \|y\| < 0.5, prompt | LHCb 13 TeV | e⁺e⁻ | D*⁺/D⁰ ± stat ± syst | minimum-bias MC truth |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3–5 | 0.50 ± 0.07 ± 0.15 | 0.521 | 0.458 ± 0.029 | 0.405 ± 0.017 | 0.395 ± 0.015 | – | – |
| 5–8 | 0.59 ± 0.07 ± 0.12 | 0.528 | 0.470 ± 0.029 | | | 0.48 ± 0.08 ± 0.11 | 0.416 |
| 8–30 | 0.49 ± 0.06 ± 0.12 | 0.534 | 0.430 ± 0.027 | | | 0.61 ± 0.09 ± 0.13 | 0.437 |

D⁺/D⁰ agrees with all references within 1.4σ and does not separate 0.40 from 0.47. The earlier, lower values (0.40 / 0.47 / 0.43) were an artefact of the efficiency denominator. σ(D*±) = 134 ± 18 ± 23 ± 3 µb (5–8 GeV) and 60.6 ± 7.1 ± 6.8 ± 1.3 µb (8–30 GeV) from a fit in Δm without the classifier.
<!-- src (v2): charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/tabele/final.md F3; charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §A; literature values: charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/WYNIKI.md §2 -->

**Comparisons.**
- *FONLL at 13.6 TeV* (prompt, 2 f(c→D) σ, f = 0.609 and 0.240; band from scales, masses and PDF): D⁰ lies inside the band, 1.1 times the central value above 5 GeV; D⁺ lies just above the upper edge at 3–5 and 5–8 GeV (0.3σ and 0.4σ) and inside the band at 8–30 GeV. CMS and ALICE report the same pattern.
- *Pythia 8 A3* (all D of the species in the fiducial region, σ_ND from stand-alone Pythia): data/Pythia is about 0.6 above 5 GeV for D⁰ and D⁺ (0.58–0.65; 2.5–4.5σ with the data error only, the largest for D⁺ at 8–30 GeV); for D*± it is 0.67 ± 0.15 (2.2σ) at 5–8 GeV and 0.87 ± 0.14 (0.9σ) at 8–30 GeV (corrected in the audit of 20.11: the earlier wording included D*± in "about 0.6").
- *CMS at 13 TeV* (arXiv:2107.01476). The definitions were checked one by one: both results are sums of particle and antiparticle; CMS uses |η| < 2.1 (pseudorapidity), we use |η| < 2.2 (factor 0.960 from the MC truth); CMS is prompt only, with the non-prompt part of 5–17 % removed with simulation, we multiply by 1 − f = 0.893 and 0.874 (D⁰; f from our MC, ± 50 %); the CMS table is dσ/dpT and is integrated over the bins 5–8 and 8–40 GeV (the 30–40 GeV part is 0.5 %); the energy is corrected with FONLL (÷ 1.036 and 1.046). No factor of two and no large non-prompt effect is hidden in the comparison. Converted data over CMS: D⁰ 0.53 ± 0.13 (3.0σ below) at 5–8 GeV and 0.75 ± 0.20 at 8–30 GeV; D⁺ 0.87 ± 0.21 and 0.81 ± 0.18; D*± 0.66 ± 0.17 and 1.08 ± 0.23.
- *ALICE at 13 TeV* (arXiv:2308.04877; prompt, |y| < 0.5, average of particle and antiparticle). **Post hoc, with unverified reference values:** this comparison was added after the tension with CMS was seen; the ALICE numbers are bin sums taken from HEPData on 19.11 and not re-verified, and an 8 % error is assumed for them. With the rapidity shape of Pythia the converted data over ALICE are 0.95 ± 0.29 / 0.82 ± 0.19 / 0.86 ± 0.22 (D⁰) and 1.05 ± 0.21 / 1.04 ± 0.20 / 1.01 ± 0.20 (D⁺). The same conversion applied to the CMS D⁰ value at 5–8 GeV gives 1.54 ± 0.20 times ALICE, so a large part of the tension with CMS in that bin would be a difference between the two published measurements; with the reference values unverified this is an indication, not a resolution of the tension.
- *ATLAS at 7 TeV* (arXiv:1512.02913; same definition as ours), scaled with FONLL by 1.84 and 2.08: D⁺ 0.83 ± 0.17 and 0.77 ± 0.16; D*± 0.76 ± 0.19 and 0.90 ± 0.18.
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5.1 (checklist with the source of every factor; CMS paper read on 20.11.2026 from arXiv HTML v2, HEPData not accessible), §5.2, §5.3; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/tabele/final.md F4; FONLL: charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/xsec2/fonll and charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/fonll; ALICE cross-section values: charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/WYNIKI.md §5 (bin sums from HEPData, not re-verified; 8 % error assumed) -->

**Open.**
- D⁰ at 5–8 GeV is 3.0σ below CMS; the comparison of definitions does not explain it. Data over Pythia 8 A3 is about 0.6 above 5 GeV for D⁰ and D⁺ (D*±: 0.67 and 0.87). The internal ratios pull the same way (D⁺/D⁰ and D*⁺/D⁰ each 0.2–1.4σ above the measured references), which would correspond to a D⁰ cross-section low by 15–30 %; the cut-based selection gives the same D⁰ level, so the classifier is not the cause. Candidates are the D⁰ fit model (two minima, closure bias 8–24 %) and the D⁰ preselection.
- At 3–5 GeV the D⁰ fit underestimates the yield on MC by 22 ± 10 %; this is carried as an uncertainty, not corrected (corrected value 1317 µb).
- Tracking, vertexing and multiplicity uncertainties are from the literature. The calibration of the luminosity (2.2 %) is an assumption; 7 % is the conservative variant.
- The scale factors of D⁺ and of D⁰ at 3–5 GeV rest on the transfer from D⁰ above 5 GeV; at 3–5 GeV the three D⁺ scale-factor inputs are compatible only at p = 0.09 and the two direct ones differ by 2.2σ (Sec. 6.8), so ± 0.05 does not cover their spread in that bin and ± 0.08 is used there (decision of 20.11; systematic 75 → 82 µb). At 5–8 and 8–30 GeV ± 0.05 is kept; with ± 0.08 there too the D⁺ systematic totals would be 16.6 and 15.4 % instead of 15.4 and 14.1 %.
- The D⁺ fit model (Sec. 5.14) was chosen by ranking and post hoc: no setting meets the closure criterion on MC at 5–8 GeV (+9.2 ± 6.2 % for the nominal). The closure (9.0 / 11.1 / 7.3 %) is in the budget, the yield is not corrected, and σ(D⁺) and D⁺/D⁰ inherit this. The yield at 3–5 GeV depends on the fitted width by up to 13 %.
- For run 428770 no absolute cross-section is given (Sec. 6.11). The D⁺/D⁰ column for run 428770 of the 19.11 version (0.296 / 0.308 / 0.358) is not restored (audit of 20.11); the correction of the denominator alone would multiply it by 1.279. A ratio of efficiency-corrected yields needs the same trigger efficiency for the two species, and this is not established: ε_J12 per species is 2.1 ± 1.5 / 2.8 ± 2.0 / 12.2 ± 3.6 % for D⁰ and 2.7 ± 1.0 / 2.7 ± 1.0 / 12.0 ± 1.9 % for D⁺, a ratio of 1.29 ± 1.03 / 0.96 ± 0.78 / 0.98 ± 0.33, compatible with one but tested only to 33–80 %; 12–24 % of the yield comes from events of none of the three chains, with unknown efficiency and shares that differ between the species (at 8–30 GeV the L1_J12-only region holds 79 % of the D⁰ and 87 % of the D⁺ yield); and D⁰ at 8–30 GeV is fitted with another model than in run 427929.
<!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5.4, §6; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/tabele/final.md F1 (variants), F6; charm_work/FITBIAS_Dplus_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §4; ε_J12 per species and region shares: charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/tabele/tabele.md T3, RAPORT.md §2; arithmetic: charm_work/AUDYT_noty_2026-11-20/out/przeliczenia.md §5, §7 -->

## 7 Limitations and outlook

**Limitations.**
- No intervals over multiple seeds/bootstrap for most metrics; ε_B ratio at WP50 has ±3–4 % statistical uncertainty;
  SIC at top-1 % background is meaningless for B < 20.
  <!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §5 pkt 4; charm_work/RECON_methods/RAPORT.md §3 (c) -->
- FETA and flows trained on CPU only (base-flow limit 0.618); full-statistics GPU training is not planned (generators are closed as a lever on Z, Sec. 5.1).
  <!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §2, §6; README.md Otwarte 3 -->
- B1 was run for D⁺ and D⁰ on subsamples only. Still open: the 8–30 GeV bin on full MC, and `mc_truth_min: 2` for D⁰ in
  the frozen SALAD v1.0 yaml.
  <!-- src: docs/signal_like_background.md §6; charm_work/B1_salad_v1/out_D0/charm-B1-salad-v1.md §6f.
       v0.2 said "D⁰ adversarial closure still open" — superseded by out_D0 (files dated 14.11). -->
- Pair closure with hash leg selection (r(m) effect in fit2d combinatorial background) unchecked — belongs to the pair analysis.
  <!-- src: README.md Otwarte 2; docs/hyp.md Otwarte -->
- No reference two-sample tests beyond C2ST (NPLM, low-FPR ROC, score-tail tests) were run.
  <!-- src: charm_work/RECON_methods/RAPORT.md §3 (b) -->
- f_MM cannot be measured in data from `hypo_swap` (correction, 17.11): it is an MC-truth flag (−1 in data), and MisMatch* is "one track from a charm hadron plus a random track", not a K↔π swap; the twin-hypothesis observable separates it from other background with AUC 0.52 only. Data give only an interval, and it is not useful: a template fit of a multivariate discriminant anchored in the sidebands bounds the local change of composition under the peak to |Δf| ≲ 44 / 18 / 11 pp (3–5 / 5–8 / 8–30 GeV, run 428770; 39 / 15 / 11 pp in 427929), dominated by the mismodelling of the features themselves (a stress test alone gives 25 / 8 / 6 pp). Through the B1 sensitivities this would be far above the assumed 5/10/15 % budget, so composition cannot be constrained this way; the direct check of ε_B under the peak against data (Sec. 6.5) remains the only test that covers it.
  <!-- src: charm_work/FMM_data/RAPORT.md §0, §5, §6 (17.11); supersedes docs/signal_like_background.md §6 -->

- **Background validation mostly on a jet-trigger-dominated sample.** Except for the last paragraph of Sec. 5.2, all closures,
  SALAD/data comparisons and ε_B numbers of Secs. 4–5 use run 428770, in which 81 % of the D*-tagged pairs come from L1_J12 alone, compared with MC
  without a trigger. The only comparison without weights and without a trigger difference is run 427929, where
  ε_B(data)/ε_B(MC) = 0.86–0.88 (Sec. 6.5; the earlier 0.87–0.97 lacked truth 2–7 in the MC denominator). SALAD and the composition test were repeated on 427929 (Sec. 5.2).
  <!-- src: B_trig/ISO_origin/RAPORT.md §5 (R = 0.87–0.97 for 427929), §7 pkt 1; B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/btrig_epsb.md (pol2/SALAD column empty) -->
- **The trigger emulation does not close.** MC has no trigger bits. Emulation weights ε(n_sel^BL, leading-track pT) from
  427929 fail on 428770 (χ²/ndf ≈ 1.5·10⁴); a model that accounts for the stream composition improves this 60-fold
  (χ²/ndf = 19 870/67) and still does not close. The cause is the skim (Sec. 6.11): the classes are defined by bits of chains that did not write the events; 7.8 % of the "MBTS-based" events fired neither HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 nor L1_J12 and are present through chains such as HLT_j10f_L1MBTS_2, which a map in (n_sel^BL, leading-track pT) cannot emulate. All per-class data/MC ratios for 428770 (classifier SF, isolation SF,
  ε_B) are therefore model dependent: for ε_B the two weighting models differ by about ±0.1 in 3–8 GeV and ±0.3 in
  8–30 GeV. The apparent agreement of ε_B for the "MBTS-based" class (ratio ≈ 1.0 at WP80/70) is produced by the weights
  (0.77–0.84 without them). Whether ε_B needs a per-trigger treatment is unresolved.
  <!-- src: B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/RAPORT_DANE.md (G5, G7); B_trig/ISO_origin/RAPORT.md §2a, §5 -->
- **Completeness of run 427929 is not confirmed** (458 of 583 input files in the container). According to the source
  this affects the statistics only, not the scale factors. (19.11, Sec. 6.11: from event counts per block the missing files lie
  mostly outside the GRL blocks, and inside them the file completeness is 0.981 under the assumption stated there. The assumption was verified on 19.11 (night), Sec. 6.11; the file list of the container itself remains unchecked.)
  <!-- src: B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/RAPORT_DANE.md (Uwagi); B_trig/PROCEDURA.md §6 -->
- **The 5–8 GeV deficit is unexplained** and the independent test is inconclusive (Sec. 5.6); in the score-category fit it is not significant in the score shape and sits in the rejected category (Sec. 5.7); the 3–5 GeV bin is not
  measurable in either run.
  <!-- src: B_trig/runs/lxplus/out/RAPORT_DANE.md (G6; 3–5 GeV rows "nieokr.") -->
- **Isolation in 428770 has no closing MC correction** (Sec. 6.2); the skim set of the ntuples was inferred from bit correlations and event counts (Secs. 2 and 6.11), not from the trigger menu or the derivation configuration.
  <!-- src: B_trig/ISO_origin/RAPORT.md §7 pkt 3, §8 -->
- **The accidental-tag estimate is preliminary** and limited to non-AFP triggers; a rate step below ξ_cal ≈ 0.075 would
  not be detected (Sec. 6.3.3).
  <!-- src: AFP_mix/runs/lxplus/out_v2/RAPORT_DANE_v2.md (Ograniczenia); docs/afp_mixing.md §6, §8 -->

- **Luminosity determined by us, not confirmed; no stream name for 428770** (18.11, updated 19.11). On 18.11 the luminosity per
  block was not available to us; on 19.11 we determined the absolute luminosity per run and trigger region (Sec. 6.11; tag
  `OflLumi-Run3-003`). It awaits confirmation by the authors of the ntuples and rests on assumptions: a calibration uncertainty of
  2.2 % (7 % from the spread of the tags) and the completeness of our files (verified inside the GRL blocks, Sec. 6.11). The cross-check with the MBTS counter,
  12–16 % lower, is understood as an inefficiency of L1_MBTS_1 for events without a primary vertex and is not an indication of the luminosity (Sec. 6.11); the absolute scale of the tag for low-μ runs stays unconfirmed. The 428770 ntuples are a trigger
  skim whose stream or derivation we could not identify; whether a dataset exists in which the track-triggered AFP chain is
  complete is an open question to the authors of the ntuples.
  <!-- src: charm_work/TAGREG_2026-11-18/v2/RAPORT.md §6; MBTS cross-check and completeness (19.11 night): charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/WYNIKI.md §8.1, §8.6 -->
- **Events written by AFP chains are not covered by the scale factors** (18.11). The isolation, classifier and vertex-veto
  scale factors are measured on events written by L1_J12 or HLT_j20_L1MBTS_2 and on run 427929. For events written by AFP
  chains, which make up almost all of the tagged region, they are not validated (Secs. 6.2.4b, 6.7 and 6.11).
  <!-- src: charm_work/VETO_pv_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md §4; charm_work/B_trig/AFPSTREAM/RAPORT.md -->
- **The 2–3 GeV bin** (Sec. 6.12): only the inclusive D⁺ peak is measured; a D⁰ peak is seen with a tight classifier cut only
  (19.11), uncalibrated and with a possible non-signal contribution of up to about 20–30 %. The classifier is applied outside its training range
  (pT ≥ 3 GeV), no minimum-bias MC exists for this bin, so neither the fit bias at the S/B of data nor a reflection ratio can
  be checked, and one of the 97 files of run 428770 could not be downloaded.
  <!-- src: charm_work/D23_plan_2026-11-18/ANALIZA/RAPORT.md §3, §6, §8 -->
- **Gain of the track-set network on data** (Sec. 5.11): the number to quote is 1.3–1.4, from the sidebands of run 427929; the
  1.54–1.79 from the 428770 sidebands is an upper estimate (jet-triggered skim, training sample of the reference tree). Stage 3
  has one training seed. Since 19.11 the sideband number itself is known to overstate the gain: from the peak fit, measured for D⁺
  only, it is 1.11 / 1.03 at 5–8 / 8–30 GeV in run 427929 (Sec. 5.11, Stages 4 and 5). With the soft-pion track removed from the input
  (Stage 6) the sideband number in run 427929 falls to 1.04 / 1.05 / 1.17; the gain from the peak fit was not measured for that network.
  <!-- src: charm_work/DNET/RAPORT.md §13, §15; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus6/WERDYKT_etap6.md §4, §5 -->
- **Event number in the forced-decay MC.** The generator restarts the event numbering inside a file, so (file, run, event) is
  not a unique event key in that sample. The D⁰–D* pairing of Sec. 6.10 does not use this key (0 mis-paired rows of 555 118); in
  the files of Secs. 5.6 and 6.2, which pair on it, 0.062 % of the pairs are joined across events and the MC efficiencies
  change by less than 0.01 %. The cross-validation fold is assigned by event number, consistently in training and application.
  Event-level tables of this sample are not used.
  <!-- src: charm_work/AUDYT_evjoin_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md §1, §3; charm_work/PEAK_lxy010_2026-11-18/RAPORT.md §7.0 -->
- **D*-tag scale factors and classifiers that see the surroundings** (19.11; Secs. 5.11 and 6.2). A network with the tracks around
  the candidate recognises the soft pion of the D* (ε at WP70 on MC 0.87–0.89 for D⁰ from D*, 0.60–0.63 for other D⁰), so its
  D*-tag scale factor calibrates D⁰ from D* only. For the isolation cuts and the CharmNet clone of Sec. 6.2 the corresponding test
  has not been made; their scale factors are to be read as valid for D⁰ from D*. Update 20.11 (Stage 6): removing the track compatible
  with the soft pion from the network input removes the effect (double ratio network over tree 1.014–1.017 instead of 1.37–1.45), but the
  network is not closed as a calibrated alternative: the D*-tag scale factor fails the pre-set seed-stability criterion in one cell (range
  0.049 against 0.04, with a statistical error of 0.13), and its real gain from the peak fit (one seed, no error) stays below the pre-set 1.15
  in run 427929 (0.99 / 1.10 / 1.09) and reaches it in run 428770 (1.13 / 1.25 / 1.17) only together with a yield 9–14 % below that of the tree.
  <!-- src: charm_work/DNET/RAPORT.md §15 pkt 1; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus4/WERDYKT_etap4.md §1; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus6/WERDYKT_etap6.md §1, §2, §5, §6; charm_work/DNET/runs/lxplus6/ANAR_naprawa.md -->
- **Absolute scale factor for D⁺ and for D⁰ at 3–5 GeV: conditional only** (Secs. 5.12 and 6.8; state of 19.11 below, update of 20.11 at the end). The D* tag covers D⁰ above 5 GeV only. The
  relative methods on the inclusive peak (cut scan, pass/fail, network over tree) give at best a ratio between working points or
  classifiers, for D⁺ from 5 GeV; for D⁰ they do not close on MC. The yield error at low pT is limited by this unknown, not by
  the working point. A study of 19.11 concludes that no pass/fail scheme on the existing data can give the absolute scale factor
  to better than about 0.15 for D⁺ and for D⁰ at 3–5 GeV: every denominator sample has S ≈ 10³ at S/B ≤ 0.01.
  Update 20.11 (Sec. 6.8): the direct methods confirm this (errors of 0.12–0.18), and a combined value now exists, 0.95–0.99 ± 0.05 for D⁺ and
  0.97 ± 0.03 for D⁰ at 3–5 GeV. It is dominated by the transfer of the D*-tag scale factor of D⁰ above 5 GeV and is conditional on the
  data/MC differences being of the resolution or dE/dx type; a D⁺-specific effect of another kind is bounded directly at ±0.08 only. A
  cut-based selection gives an independent 0.97 ± 0.05 ± 0.05 for D⁺ at 5–30 GeV. At 3–5 GeV the two direct D⁺ numbers, 0.74 ± 0.17 (ladder)
  and 1.29 ± 0.19 (cuts against classifier), point in opposite directions: they differ by 2.2σ, and together with the transfer the three inputs have χ²/ndf = 4.8/2 (p = 0.09); the ± 0.05 does not
  cover this spread in that bin, ± 0.08 does (Sec. 6.8; used for D⁺ at 3–5 GeV in the cross-sections of Sec. 6.13 since the decision of 20.11).
  <!-- src: charm_work/WPTIGHT_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (D5, Rekomendacja 2–3); charm_work/DNET/RAPORT.md §15 pkt 2; charm_work/SFABS_plan_2026-11-19/PLAN.md §0 pkt 1; update 20.11: charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0, §5; charm_work/XSEC_atlas_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §B -->
- **Calibration of the classifier inputs: attempted, stopped, no result** (INCAL, 19–20.11). The alternative route, fitting the
  resolution of the inputs to data on D⁰ from D* and propagating it through the tree, does not work on the present samples. A
  first reading suggested a data/MC difference in the primary-vertex resolution along the beam; this was an artefact and is
  withdrawn: the Δm background under the D* peak had been subtracted by linear interpolation of the sidebands, which
  underestimates a threshold-shaped background by 12–19 % and left a background admixture of 17–75 % in the "signal" sample
  (largest in 428770 at low pT). With the subtraction corrected the method closes on MC at the MC purity but not at the purity
  of the data (an injected 40 µm smearing is recovered as 16 ± 9 µm), so by its own stopping rule no scale factor is quoted.
  Only the 8–30 GeV D* sample is clean enough to be used, and a clean closure test would need a wrong-sign sample that is not
  in the present tables. All uses of the D* tag in this note were reviewed on 20.11: every scale factor from the D* tag (classifier H, isolation,
  CharmNet clone, networks, hypothesis arbiter, vertex veto, reflection ratio) uses a pass/fail fit with a threshold background, a sideband
  subtraction normalised from a threshold fit, or sWeights with a threshold background. The single exception was the D* peak in D⁺
  candidates (Sec. 6.6), whose data/MC ratio does not change. In the 40 refitted and 334 stored fits the nominal threshold × pol2 model was
  chosen every time; the fallback model with an exponential factor was never used, and for the cells that do not store the model name
  (trigger classes other than "all", CharmNet clone, networks, pedestal and track-jet studies) this was not checked.
  <!-- src: charm_work/INCAL_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md §9; charm_work/Q_lxplus/dnet1/src/charmtag/dstar_refit.py (fit_passfail, threshold_pol2); review of all uses (20.11): charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0, §1, §5, §6 -->
- **Fit bias at small S/B** (Secs. 5.12 and 5.13). On MC with full background the pass/fail fit overestimates the signal in loose
  score categories by 5–8 % (D⁺), known to ±4–5 % only; the MC pseudo-data have the composition of minimum bias and are not
  representative of run 428770. Separately, the absolute D⁰ yield from the old fit model was too low on MC by 14–27 %; the model
  M8, nominal for D⁰ from 19.11, removes this above 5 GeV, while −23 ± 10 % remains at 3–5 GeV (20.11: located in the combinatorial
  background without charm and read as a local 2.4σ fluctuation of the single MC realisation; an independent MC sample is needed to settle
  it), its HESSE error is too small by 20–30 %, and in run 428770 at 8–30 GeV the data reject it. The D⁰ quantities of Secs. 5.11, 5.12 and
  6.12 were recomputed with M8 on 20.11; Secs. 6.6 and 6.11 and the D*-tag scale factors still use the models of their time (list in
  Sec. 5.13). The branching-fraction scale of the peaking template is withdrawn (Sec. 5.13); the recomputations of Secs. 5.11, 5.12 and 6.12 and the
  fits by trigger region of Sec. 6.11 still contain it (effect on the WP70 yields −0.5 % / +1.4 to +1.8 % / +2.6 to +3.1 %; no conclusion depends on it).
  <!-- src: charm_work/WPTIGHT_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (M1, D3, Zastrzeżenia); charm_work/WPSCAN_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (Korekta); charm_work/FITBIAS_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (Odpowiedź, §1–§3); 20.11: charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §3, §5; charm_work/M8_przeliczenie_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md -->
- **Inclusive cross-sections** (Sec. 6.13): preliminary and post hoc; final computation of 20.11, which replaces the values of 19.11 (those
  were too high because of the efficiency denominator), in its version v2 (D⁺ fit model of Sec. 5.14, scale-factor errors of Sec. 6.8). Open: D⁰ at 5–8 GeV is 3.0σ below CMS (the definitions were checked one by one; a
  post-hoc comparison with unverified ALICE values suggests that CMS is about 1.5 times ALICE in that bin); data over Pythia 8 A3 is about
  0.6 above 5 GeV; the tracking, vertexing and multiplicity uncertainties are taken from the literature (Sec. 6.9), not measured; the scale
  factor of D⁺ and of D⁰ at 3–5 GeV is conditional on the transfer (Sec. 6.8); the D⁰ fit has two minima and an uncorrected bias of
  −22 ± 10 % on MC at 3–5 GeV (Sec. 5.13); the D⁺ fit model does not meet its closure criterion on MC at 5–8 GeV (+9.2 ± 6.2 %; Sec. 5.14) and its yield at 3–5 GeV depends on the fitted width by up to 13 %; the calibration of the
  luminosity (2.2 %) is an assumption. Settled since 19.11: the luminosity variant and the trigger efficiency (Sec. 6.11), the energy
  correction (FONLL), and the 19.11 finding about the decay table of the minimum-bias MC, which is withdrawn (Sec. 5.13).
  <!-- src: charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5, §6; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/tabele/final.md F2, F4; charm_work/XSEC_2026-11-19/WYNIKI.md §8.1–§8.2; charm_work/SFABS_wynik_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0; charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5; charm_work/CVER_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md; charm_work/FITBIAS_Dplus_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md; charm_work/XSEC_final_2026-11-20/v2/ZMIANY.md -->
- **Model uncertainty of the predicted tagged yield** (Sec. 6.11). The generator scenario spans a factor of ten (R(D⁰) =
  0.154–1.53 %), dominated by two variations that no measurement constrains in the AFP ξ window; it is an envelope of models. Which
  version of the A3 settings and of Pythia produced the MC samples of the analysis was not verified.
  <!-- src: charm_work/SD_var_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md §5, §6 -->
- **Run 428770 gives relative quantities only** (20.11; Secs. 2 and 6.11). No trigger region of the run of the target measurement gives an
  absolute normalisation: the jet-trigger efficiency for events with a D (2.5–12 % for L1_J12) is known only from run 427929, with errors of
  14–34 %, and cannot be transferred between the runs to better than about 16 %; the events of the remaining chains of the skim (12–24 % of
  the yield) have no luminosity; in R1 there are about 80 D in total. Absolute quantities and the D⁺/D⁰ ratio stay in run 427929.
  <!-- src: charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §0, §3–§5 -->
- **Isolation scale factor in run 427929: background-model spread** (20.11; Sec. 6.2.2). The 0.98 ± 0.04 for ptcone40 < 2 GeV is stable
  between three estimators, but sideband counting gives 1.05–1.12, a difference that sits at 3–5 GeV and is not contained in the quoted
  error. The conclusion of Sec. 6.2 (minimum bias agrees with MC, the jet-triggered run does not) does not depend on it. It is not shown to be
  the same effect as the +0.10 of the two-dimensional fit of the classifier scale factor (other pT bins; Sec. 6.8); common to both is an estimator
  dependence of 0.05–0.10 of D*-tag efficiencies in this run, which the fit errors do not contain.
  <!-- src: charm_work/AUDYT_Dstar_tlo_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §3, §6 -->

**Not possible without a new production or new data (state of 20.11).**
- **Λc⁺ → pK⁻π⁺:** 0 candidates. Our files hold no rows of that decay type (data of run 428770 and both MC samples in v1.2), the v2.0
  configuration does not enable the Λc finder, and there is no Λc (or D_s) in the truth record. A Λc/D⁰ ratio needs a new production from AOD.
- **Calibration of the classifier inputs.** The attempt on D⁰ from D* was stopped (above). It needs either a wrong-sign sample for a clean
  closure, or MC samples with the standard tracking variations (material, impact-parameter smearing with a vertex refit; Sec. 6.9), which
  cannot be emulated on the converted files; a V0 sample (K_S⁰, Λ⁰) would give dE/dx and two-track vertex resolution at track pT near 1 GeV.
- **Background MC at 2–3 GeV.** Our minimum-bias MC starts at a candidate pT of 3 GeV. A 2–3 GeV production is listed in the production
  documentation, but its grid container holds no files today; without it there is no fit-bias test, no peaking template and no reflection
  ratio in that bin (Sec. 6.12).
- **Absolute cross-section in run 428770.** It needs the jet-trigger efficiency for events with a D from minimum-bias data: about 1 nb⁻¹ for
  20 % on D⁰ at 8–30 GeV and about 6 nb⁻¹ at 5–8 GeV (run 427929 has 0.46 nb⁻¹), or an MC sample with the trigger decision (Sec. 6.2.5).
- **Statistics of the minimum-bias MC.** One realisation of about the size of run 427929: the 3–5 GeV dip of the D⁰ closure, the fit bias of
  H+DLZ (Sec. 5.10, at least four times more) and the D⁰ closure of the relative methods (Secs. 5.11–5.13) cannot be settled with it.
<!-- src: charm_work/INDICO_1709031/ (review of 20.11) §0 pkt 4, §A row 6 (Λc, V0), rows 1a–1b, §B pkt 6; charm_work/INCAL_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md §9; charm_work/BKGCAT_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §5 (truth record), §6 (2–3 GeV production); charm_work/REG428_2026-11-20/RAPORT.md §3; charm_work/FITBIAS_2026-11-19/RAPORT.md (Zastrzeżenia) -->

**Outlook: open benchmark.** The current `charmbkg.synth` is a test fixture (independent distributions, no constraints, no
hypothesis pairs, no background categories) and is not a benchmark. A fully open benchmark "D⁰→Kπ in combinatorial
background" — Pythia 8 minimum-bias + simplified track acceptance/resolution + the `charmbkg` toy dE/dx — would provide true
hypothesis pairs and known background-category composition, allowing the B1 protocol, the Z ceiling and the weak-supervision
threshold to be reproduced without ATLAS data, scanning S/B from 0.05 % to 5 %, with NPLM as reference test. This is the
only route to an external methods publication (proceedings at ML4Jets/EuCAIFCon), with thesis 4.2 as the focus, and requires
informing the ATLAS group beforehand. Estimated effort 4–6 weeks.
<!-- src: charm_work/RECON_methods/RAPORT.md §0, §2 (Open data), §3, §4 -->
